# Stage 3 Design: Skins

Stage 3 of the roadmap (design.md "Roadmap") covers the presentation of the 7-key play screen, song selection, the song decision screen and the single-chart result screen. This file plans the skin system: the runtime model the player draws from, the importers that make existing skins usable, the player integrations, tapestry's own play-skin format, and its export to beatoraja-family players.

**Status.** Decided; the decisions that change design.md are in it ("Goals", "Play Lifecycle", "State Model", "Personal Bests", "Rendering and Skins", "Custom Skin Format", "beatoraja Lua Skins", "Memory Budget", "Load Pipeline", "Extension Boundaries", "Settings Scopes", "Language and Crate Structure", "Libraries", "Roadmap", "Open Questions"). The four screen kinds are the owner's decision of 2026-11-03 (§7, decision 7), and where Lua runs, compiled for play and bounded at runtime outside it, the owner's decision of 2026-11-04 (§7, decision 8); the non-play adapters and integrations are planned, not built. The fast and slow counts in `Tally` are in design.md ("Evaluation Stages", accounting). This file holds the plan and the details, and is removed when stage 3 exits.

**Citations.** `file:N` points into the repository at commit 17e17de unless it names another revision (8622dd7 for the canvas, regions and lanes of S5, c613b6f for the number, judgment and gauge widgets of S5b), and design.md is cited by section name. "ED" is LR2oraja Endless Dream; "upstream" is beatoraja itself. What is said about their skin loaders was read from their published source as a description of the file format; none of their code is ported (design.md "Libraries").

**What the skin system must do:**
- **Existing skins first.** Skins made for beatoraja-family players become playable in tapestry before tapestry's own format exists. The importers are separate from the format. What the real skins needed, as the importers found it, is an input to the format's design: S12 decides each item, taking it in or leaving it out with the reason, and the format need not express everything they needed.
- **A small format of its own.** Few people make skins today. A spec that is large, or has features an author gains nothing from, goes unused. Every concept an author learns has to pay off for them.
- **One source, two skins.** A skin written in tapestry's format also builds a beatoraja skin, so one piece of work reaches the players of both. The beatoraja skin is a faithful subset: tapestry's may do more, and what beatoraja cannot express degrades in the export and never breaks it.
- **Not bound to 16:9.** The format adapts to other aspect ratios such as 21:9. The beatoraja export stays 16:9.
- **The player adjusts the composition.** A home setup is not a cabinet. Players move their monitor or where they sit because a skin fixes the whole layout and the existing players can only stretch the screen. Here the player moves and resizes the parts of the screen, and the skin author does nothing for it.
- **Crisp at any size.** Image skins blur or get jaggy when stretched or shrunk. Scaling is handled at load, so it costs nothing per frame.

**Direction applied throughout:**
- **Two levels.** A broad compiled *runtime model* is what the player draws from; the importers and the compiler of the small *authored format* both target it. The runtime model grows only with the PR that first needs a feature, which for import-only features is an importer.
- **The authored format is specified on its own terms.** Its semantics are written for tapestry; the beatoraja export is a constraint every construct must meet (it has an export rule), never the source of the semantics.
- **The logic thread never knows skins exist.** Everything a skin shows comes from the play view, the effect queue and the chart, as today (design.md "Rendering and Skins").
- **Nothing is built ahead of the PR that needs it,** and skin work before stage 2 exits lives in new portable crates that touch neither `render`, `player`, `game` nor `eval`. S10 is the exception, by the owner's decision: its PRs land one by one before stage 2 exits, each gated by Windows CI. S7-01 to S7-04 run beside S10-06 and land before S10-07, also by the owner's decision, since the owner's 7-key play skins are all Lua; #83 and S9 land before S10-07 too, by the owner's decision (§6).

## 1. Scope

**In:**
- **The runtime model and its evaluation** in portable crates: elements, keyframe tracks, timers, typed expressions, regions, widgets, the atlas.
- **The beatoraja importer** (JSON and Lua skins) into the runtime model, before the authored format.
- **The authored format**, its compiler and diagnostics.
- **The beatoraja exporter** and an authoring CLI that checks, previews, and builds both skins in one command.
- **Player layout adjustment**: per-region position, scale and visibility, lane width, and an adjust mode drawn with the play screen's sprites.
- **The standard skin**, written in the format with self-made art, and the removal of `SimpleSkin`.
- **BGA**, starting from the stage 0 spike's decoder.
- **Imported screen skins** (§7, decision 7): beatoraja JSON and Lua skins for 7-key play, song selection, song decision and single-chart result, each kind chosen on its own. An unconfigured or refused non-play skin leaves that screen's built-in presentation (design.md "Rendering and Skins", screen skins). S7 implements the 7-key play profile; each non-play profile comes with its screen's importer (S10-09d, S10-09f, S10-09g to S10-09h).
- **Selection presentation:** the bounded song-list widget, visible-row text, levels and own lamps, search and its IME presentation and native action dispatch, over the minimal selection of https://github.com/shuymn/tapestry/issues/179 (folders entered and returned from, beside the existing all-chart view and search), with charts of other modes not listed (https://github.com/shuymn/tapestry/issues/177).
- **Decision presentation:** the chosen chart's prepared labels and the skin's own scene and fade-out times; its input declaration starts timer 1 (S10 item 9.4b), but no command acts on the decision screen, besides Back and the quit key past its time while practice waits for its read (design.md "Play Lifecycle", the decision screen). Skipping a skinned decision screen after its input time is planned (https://github.com/shuymn/tapestry/issues/478).
- **Result presentation:** the existing result and best data, plus bounded judgment and early/late timeline data, drawn by four widgets: the gauge history, the judgment timeline, the early/late timeline and the timing distribution.
- **Graphs and visualizers drawn by skins** (owner decision): the play score graph with the score figures it needs (best and target EX, their differences and rates, the current rank and the ranks' pass marks), the notes and BPM graphs, the timing and hit-error visualizers, and selection's density and BPM previews (S10-10).
- **Every option value:** an imported skin works under each of its options' values and file choices, not only its defaults; a part that tapestry cannot draw under some value is planned like one under the default.
- **UI string IDs** (https://github.com/shuymn/tapestry/issues/176): named IDs with a locale choice and a fallback for the player's own strings, before the first new public text binding and S11.

**Out:**
- **The LR2 importer**, which comes right after stage 3 (§7, decision 1).
- **Unsupported screen kinds:** non-7-key play skins, courses and course-result skins come later; skin-select skins are S11's work, not one of the four kinds of S10-09; the practice page stays built in (https://github.com/shuymn/tapestry/issues/223). Non-play screens in the authored format, its export and the standard skin (S12–S14) are not stage 3's.
- **Features tapestry does not have yet** (owner decision): IR, rivals, difficulty tables, favourites, advanced search and courses. A skin's parts for them show their empty state, as the source shows them without the feature: their text empty (1003 among them), their numbers omitted and their conditions false (1008 among them).
- **Folder distribution graphs:** folder lamp and rank distributions.
- Double play, mouse dragging in the adjust mode (it comes with `egui`), SVG images, SDF shapes, MSDF text.

## 2. The runtime model (`skin-model`)

### R1. Elements, tracks and draw order

A compiled skin is a list of elements in draw order. Each element has a source (an image frame, an image choice (R6), a number, text, a bar, or a widget), a keyframe track, a timer, a visibility condition, a blend, a region and the covers it follows (`Follow`: none, LIFT, SUDDEN+ or both; R6, "Followers"). The runtime model carries the union of what imported skins need: five blends (alpha, add, subtract, multiply, invert), quadratic and cubic eases, per-object or per-keyframe ease, clip rectangles, and derived edge timers. Each of these enters with the PR that first needs it: an importer PR for import-only features, the LR2 importer for the LR2-only ones. The lanes' bar line groups are parts of the lanes, not elements: each has its own track, timer, visibility, blend and placement and draws before the notes (R6).

**Draw order across blends.** The runtime model's `QuadBatch` is one ordered list with preallocated runs; a run breaks wherever the blend, the clip rectangle or a cover paint's inline trim clip (`Run::clip_rect`, R6 "Cover paint") changes, so two neighbouring elements with different clips never share a scissor. Render's `SpriteBatch` is that batch (S10-03b-1), and the renderer draws the runs in order, each under its clip's scissor (S10-01, S10-02), so an alpha element over an additive one is drawn over it (§6, "Render integration"). The CPU preview applies the same runs.

### R2. Animation

Times are integer milliseconds in files and microseconds inside. For an element with timer `T` and keyframes at `k0 ≤ … ≤ kn`:
- The element is not drawn while `T` is off.
- Local time is `t = now − start(T)`; an element without a timer uses the built-in `screen` timer, which starts at the loading lifetime's origin while the play screen loads (R3, the loading runtime) and runs on from the play state's screen origin into the play (D3), so it is drawn from the screen's start as the target loaders draw untimed objects. An element authored on `ready` stays off while loading.
- After the last key, one of three behaviors, named by the author: **hold** (keep the last key's values), **hide** (drawn while `t ≤ kn`, not after), or **loop from `L`** (`−4 h ≤ L < kn`): for `t > L`, `t' = L + (t − L) mod (kn − L)`, so `t = kn` already shows `L`; an `L` before `k0`, a negative one included, leaves a gap each period, where `t'` falls before the first key and nothing draws. This matches the target loaders exactly (ED `SkinObject.prepareRegion`), so the export is one-to-one.
- Before `k0` the element is not drawn.
- Of keys sharing a time, the last shows at that time and the track before it moves toward the first, so the keys between never show; the target loaders insert a key before the first later one and sample from the last segment back (`SkinObject.java:240-255, 545-575` at `0.8.9`).
- Between keys, x, y, w, h, alpha, r, g, b and angle interpolate; a field left out of a key keeps the previous key's value. A key's `(x, y)` is the rect's top-left corner. The target loaders' destinations are bottom-left and y-up, in the skin's own size, scaled by the output size over it; the beatoraja importer scales and reflects them (§3, "Mapping"). Each element names a 9-point pivot, a point of the sampled rect (corner, edge midpoint or centre), about which it rotates: with x = 100 and w from 40 to 80 the corner stays and a centre pivot moves from 120 to 140. The angle is in degrees and interpolates numerically (350 to 10 passes 180); drawing reduces it into `[0, 2π)` (`rem_euclid` before the conversion, so large keys keep their precision). `Quad` carries the pivot as a canvas point and the angle in radians.
- Ease per track: linear, in, out, step (in and out are quadratic). Cubic and per-keyframe ease come into the runtime model with the importer that needs them.
- **Image frames** are not part of the track: an image's frame cycle runs on the image's own timer, so an element can show on `ready` while its frames restart on `judge`. With `n` frames over a period `p` (1 µs to the longest key time), `t` after that timer started shows frame `((t mod p) · n) / p`; frame 0 shows while the timer is off, without a cycle, or with one frame, as in the target loaders (`SkinSourceImage.java:73-89`). Both timers come from the same frame's fill, before any element is drawn. Each entry of an image choice (R6) cycles over its own frames on its own timer in the same way, so switching entries never restarts a clock.
- **Bar groups** (R6) sample their tracks, visibility and image cycles as an element does, on the frame's `now` and their timers' starts; the display offset moves only the bars' travel, so through a STOP a bar holds still while its animation runs, and before `play` starts a group on `ready` animates while one on `play` stays off.
- In the authored format, keys must be strictly increasing from `k0 ≥ 0` and a loop point must lie in `[0, kn)`, before the first key leaving a gap each period (the target loaders hide a finished track by setting its time to −1, which a negative key would make visible again); anything else is a load error. The target loaders accept unsorted and equal-time keys and apply a loop only when the last key is after 0; the importer reproduces that, and the exporter emits only tracks that need neither. Tests pin `k0`, `kn` and `L` at exactly and ±1 µs, for hold, hide, loop and a single-key track.

### R3. State and timers

The skin reads a per-frame `SkinFrame`, filled from the render thread's `FrameInput`, the chart, a play context given to `prepare`, and a reducer over the drained effects. The play context is fixed for one attempt: the preset's gauge descriptors (`start`, `max`, the gauge's `look` and its `border`, in the order of `PlayView::gauges`, which carry values only, crates/game/src/render.rs:17-28), the attempt's start position and, in practice, its section, so the lanes widget draws only the section's targets (design.md "Mid-Chart Start and Section Repeat") and `ready` and `play` start from the attempt's own READY state, not chart time 0, the preset's default long-note mode (`ln_default`), which with the chart's own mode decides whether an imported skin draws a long note's end cap, and the end of the attempt's notes on the play clock (`last_note_end`, where `last_note` starts: the last note's time for the whole chart, played or autoplayed alike, background sounds never moving it, and in practice the section's end mapped exactly to the play speed). Each `SkinFrame` says whether its update entered the play in its playing state (`playing`): false during READY and after a stop, the finish or the fade-out, and still true when a gauge fails in that same update.

**Where each kind of value comes from:**
- **Numbers and note state come only from the play view.** A dropped effect loses an animation, never a number or a note's state on screen (design.md "Rendering and Skins", the note snapshot). Counts, EX score, BP, combo, gauges and the like are read from `PlayView`, never accumulated from effects; judged taps, hit mines and each lane's visual hold, which the lanes and the `hold` timers read, come from `SkinFrame::notes`, the view's note facts.
- **Edge timers come from ordered effects.** A timer's value is its start time, taken from the event's chart time, not the frame's. Two are the exceptions: `last_note` starts at the notes' end once a playing frame reaches it, not on an effect, and each lane's `hold` is the start of the visual hold in the play view's note facts (`SkinFrame::notes`), off once its long note's end is at or before `now`, whatever effects were dropped or arrive late.
- **Lane geometry is resolved once per frame** from `SkinFrame::lanes` (a loading frame's lanes while loading), before any element is drawn, whether or not the lanes draw (`LaneFrameGeometry`): the lift's, SUDDEN+'s and HIDDEN+'s lengths, the line and the scroll span. The lanes, the cover paints (each resolving its own trim line from it, R6 "Trim"), followers, bar groups and grades all read that one result, so nothing shows the previous frame's covers. Bar lines travel from the chart position at the display head (`now + display_offset`), as the notes do, while the bar groups' clocks stay on `now` (R2); the prepared chart's bar lines are not section targets, and a section's `prepare` resets the groups' timers with the others.
- **Score rates come from the snapshot and the attempt** (S6c-02). `rate.current_bp` is `⌊10 000 · EX / (2 · judged)⌋` and `rate.whole_bp` the same over the attempt's targets (the reducer's, prepared from the chart and the attempt: one per tap or long note, mines excluded, a practice section's own), each 10 000 when its denominator is 0. EX is the snapshot's `Tally::ex_score`; `judged` is PERFECT + GREAT + GOOD + BAD + POOR, less the empty POORs when the play context says the preset counts them as POOR (`PlayContext::empty_poor_counted`, from `preset.empty_poor.counted`), since the evaluator then adds each to both counts. The sums are taken in `u64` before the field's range, which is 0 to `i32::MAX`: a custom EX weight can pass 10 000 and nothing caps it. Holding a long note, a mine, BP or the combo never moves a denominator, and a frame's rates and the `when` timers reading them follow the snapshot even ahead of its effects. A snapshot with more counted empty POORs than POORs is not the evaluator's: `Skin::build` returns `BuildError::InconsistentTally` before applying any effect, leaving the batch, reducer, fields and timers unchanged, and the `Play` is poisoned, returning the same error to every later `build` until `prepare` (the drained effects are not replayed, so continuing would show stale state).
- **The gauge kind comes from the shown descriptor** (S6c-02): `gauge.kind` is the look of the descriptor `SkinFrame::gauge` selects, the same `GaugeDesc::look` the gauge widget draws (R6), and `gauge.valid` is true only while both that descriptor and the snapshot's gauge at that index exist; otherwise the kind reads `normal`. A switch of the shown gauge changes both in that frame, without an effect. During loading the definition is the loading runtime's (below): `gauge.kind` is the look of the loading frame's descriptor and `gauge.valid` means that the frame gives one.
- **Play readouts come from prepared chart data and the current settings** (S6c-03, design.md "Play readouts"), never accumulated from effects, inferred from painted covers or recovered from the scroll map's STOP slopes. `prepare` takes the original 1× chart's BPM range, its initial BPM included, from the play context (`PlayContext::bpm_range`), with whether the chart's whole BPM extremes differ, uncapped (`PlayContext::bpm_whole_change`, `bpm.change` and `bpm.change_valid`, slots 86–87), the chart's play level (`PlayContext::play_level`, `chart.level` and `chart.level_valid`, slots 88–89), the whole chart's MAIN tempo (`PlayScore::main`, the attempt's, computed once per load over the whole 1× chart whatever section a practice round plays or speed it is loaded at; `chart.main_bpm` and `chart.main_bpm_valid`, slots 151–152, in whole BPM truncated as the source's `(int)` cast, unknown without a MAIN tempo or past the readouts' maximum), the chart's 1× length from the chart itself (`ChartMeta::length`, `chart.length_ms`, slot 153, whole ms, the same in every practice round and at every speed; numbers 1163–1164 its minutes within the hour and seconds within the minute), whether the attempt is autoplay (`PlayContext::autoplay`, `autoplay`, slot 90, known while loading from the loading frame), and the attempt's end: the chart's last resolved object (the latest note end, mines included, or background sound start, at least 0; no sound tails, no margin) or the section's end, both in real time at the practice speed. Each frame carries `SkinFrame::readouts`: the playback tempo at the display head (`now + display_offset`), the hi-speed, the scroll policy's nominal travel duration as an exact ratio in ms, STOP waiting excluded, the stored cover settings and the speed policy's hi-speed fix (`settings.fix_hispeed`, slot 140, 0 Classic and Floating 1 to 4 from the start, maximum, main and minimum tempos); the display offset in whole ms truncated toward zero, as on selection, is `display_offset.ms` (slot 139), from the frame's own offset. The application's readouts (slots 141–150) come from the frame's `SkinFrame::app`, which the player samples once per frame whatever the attempt's state: `app.fps`, the frames presented in the last second, at most 1 024 (D28); the local date and time, `clock.year` to `clock.second`, month 1 to 12 and the 24-hour hour; and the uptime since launch on the QPC clock (D29), whole hours unwrapped and minutes and seconds within them, `app.uptime_h`, `app.uptime_m` and `app.uptime_s`, split from the `i64` µs before any field is stored; numbers 20 to 29 read them on play. The clocks are actual time from the attempt's start, `time.remaining_s` by ceiling and `time.elapsed_s` by floor, in `i128` and bounded afterwards; READY holds them at the attempt's length and 0, and neither the display offset nor completion moves them. The progress (fidelity item 5, the source's `musicprogress`, `skin/property/FloatPropertyFactory.java:128-136`: `min(elapsed TIMER_PLAY / playtime, 1)`, 0 while `TIMER_PLAY` is off) is `time.progress_ppm` (slot 101), the share of that same span in parts per million, `⌊10⁶ · (now − start) / (end − start)⌋` in `i128`, one end feeding the clocks and it; each choice against its source line: its end is the clocks' end, with no margin, in a normal play and in autoplay alike, where the source's playtime adds 5 000 ms to the last note, or in autoplay to the last object (`play/BMSPlayer.java:66, 198-199`), a D4 difference (below); it reads 0 in READY and while loading (`TIMER_PLAY` is set only on leaving READY, `play/BMSPlayer.java:605-610`, and the loading class "Before"); its span is real time at the practice speed, so a speed lengthens the time it takes, not its share; it reads 10⁶ at and after the end, as the source's `min(…, 1)`, completion and the display offset moving nothing; and a retry's fresh `Play` and a rearm's frames, on the new candidate's clock, start it at 0, their READY reading, as the source turns `TIMER_PLAY` off before a practice round's next READY (`:526-534`). The duration is rounded half up exactly, and the green value is `⌊3·ms/5⌋` of the rounded duration. The hi-speed and the duration are projections into the source's coordinates (S10-04b, design.md "Play readouts"): `Hs = H / (1 − lift)`, unclamped and unavailable where no field is left, with eight duration variants beside them (the current, MAIN, minimum and maximum tempos, with the configured lane cover and with none, slots 51–74); the player's own HUD reads a separate native block (slots 75–85: the active multiplier, the current duration and green number, the target green number, the three whites and the Floating flag), each value with its own rounding and validity. `prepare` refuses a section ending before its start (`PrepareError::InvalidSectionRange`) and a range out of order or past `i32::MAX − 1` (`PrepareError::InvalidBpmRange`). `build` refuses a readout past `i32::MAX − 1`, a cover setting above 1 000, a zero denominator, or stored covers that are not what the lanes paint (`BuildError::InvalidReadouts`), after the tally check and before any effect; the `Play` is poisoned as for `InconsistentTally`, every later `build` returning the first error until `prepare`.
- **The recent timing comes from the effects** (fidelity item 5, the source's `getRecentJudgeTiming`, `play/JudgeManager.java:457`, `:690`): `judge.recent_ms` (slot 91) is the latest selected judgment's note time minus press, in ms truncated toward 0, so an early press is positive, `-(deviation_us / 1000)` of the evaluator's deviation; `judge.recent_imperfect` (slot 92) says whether that judgment is worse than PERFECT. The reducer selects what the source's `updateMicro` sees: a tap's `Judged`; a long note's `Judged` at its release or end, never its `LnHeld` (the source's held head leaves the timing, `:428-438`), taking of its head and its release the one farther from its time, the release on a tie (`:533-537`), and the release's when that one is early and the judgment BAD or worse (`:538-540`, `:565-567`), held to its end the head's (`:572-573`); a miss, the note's time minus the miss's evaluation time (the source's miss path takes the frame past the late BAD edge, `:594-598`, here the deadline or the press that passed the note). Two values may differ from the source's by up to a frame, since the evaluator reads no frame clock: that miss, and the early release that leaves a long note BAD, whose timing the source takes from the frame that read the release (`releasetime = mtime`, `:539`, used at `:567`) and tapestry from the release's own evaluation time; an empty POOR, the note it is against (`EmptyPoor::deviation`; the source's judgment 5, `:400-404`, `:457-458`). A BAD head decided at its press takes its head's timing there, where the source's waits for the release, the preset's known difference. Both read 0 and false before the first judgment, after a rearm and in a retry's fresh `Play`, and are unavailable while loading. Like condition 241, they read the judgment state whichever widget draws judgments, where the source keeps them only for a judge region its judge widget makes (`:129-133`, `:678`). The source's names for 1P alone are mapped; 526, 527, 1262, 1263, 1362 and 1363 stay unsupported.
- **The chart's metadata comes from its ingestion** (fidelity item 5, the source's song data: `skin/property/BooleanPropertyFactory.java:435-452`, `IntegerPropertyFactory.java:389-394`): `prepare` takes it from the play context (`PlayContext::meta`, a `ChartMetadata`) as `chart.difficulty`, `chart.total`, `chart.bga` and `chart.judge_class`, each with its validity (slots 102 to 109), constant over the attempt; a caller without a chart gives none, and every condition on it reads false. The player fills it from the attempt the game makes of the loaded chart (`game::Attempt`, its labels from the same ingestion), so a direct launch gives it as a play chosen in the library does. Each id against its source line: 150 to 155 (`chart_difficulty_0` to `_5`, `:442-447`) test the song data's difficulty, `#DIFFICULTY` read by `Integer.parseInt` (jbms-parser `CommandWord`, which sets it only when the value reads, so a later unreadable line keeps the earlier value and none leaves 0; the loader and the library listing keep the last readable text, `bms_parser::java_int`), 150 for `d <= 0 || d > 5`: `chart.difficulty` holds 1 to 5 and 0 for any other value or none, the same parse as the play level's (`difficulty_of`, `level_of`), and 150 reads `chart.difficulty_valid && chart.difficulty == 0`, the others `chart.difficulty == d`; 170 (`song_no_bga`, `:435`) is `!hasBGA()`, set when the decoder listed a `#BMPxx` image (`song/SongData.java:216`), whether or not a channel shows it: `chart.bga` is whether a line the decoder registers stands in the branches the ingestion took (`bms_parser::Labels::bga`): as observed in the 0.8.9 decoder (`BMSDecoder`), `BMP` in any case after the `#`, an object id in the next two characters and a line at least eight UTF-16 units long, the path read from the eighth on and kept even when it is only spaces, so `#BMP01` and two spaces registers, with one space it does not, and `#BMP001 a.bmp` registers id `00`; the library listing reads its top level the same way, and 170 reads `chart.bga_valid && !chart.bga`, 171 staying unsupported. 180 to 184 (`chart_judge_veryhard` to `_veryeasy`, `:448-452`) band the song data's rank: `chart.judge_class` holds the class the source computes from the chart's own rank, whatever the play preset (design.md "Rendering and Skins", chart metadata: the beatoraja-style policy's reading, the source's 7-key validation, so `#DEFEXRANK 120` is 90, and its bands), and 180 reads `chart.judge_class_valid && chart.judge_class == 0`, the others `chart.judge_class == k`, by id, by name or from a frame-time Lua callback, with no note, critical uses included; a chart in no class holds none. Number 368 (`chart_totalgauge`) is the song information's `(int) getTotal()`, the total after the source's rule validated the model (the owner's decision, option (a)): `chart.total` is the `#TOTAL` the reference decoder reads, a number above 0, the last such declaration (`rules::HeaderPolicy::DefExRankFirst`), truncated, else the default the source sets for an absent one, `max(260, 7.605 n / (0.01 n + 6.5))` (`play/BMSPlayerRule.java:68-80`; an absent `#TOTAL` leaves the decoder's model at 100.0 and the rule replaces it), computed in `f64` in the source's order and truncated as its cast is, over the whole chart's taps and long notes, each once, no mine, the source's count in its default long-note mode (jbms-parser `TimeLine.getTotalNotes`), whatever the play preset's own default; a value past `i32` saturates and draws nothing, as the source's number widget reserves `i32::MAX`. It reads under `chart.total_valid`, with no note: the source shows it wherever its song data has a song information, which a play chosen in its selection has (`song/SongData.java:141-142`, `:219`) and a direct launch has too, its `new SongData(model, false)` (`PlayerResource.java:175`) naming no text file and creating the information (`song/SongData.java:130-139`). While loading, the difficulty and the BGA flag are the chosen chart's (`ChosenChart::difficulty` and `bga`, from its library entry's `#DIFFICULTY` and BGA flag, the library's schema 7), and the total and the class, which need the ingestion, are unavailable.
- **Each lane's visual state comes from the effects and the lane input** (fidelity item 5, the source's per-lane judge value, `play/JudgeManager.java:805-811`, read through image-index ids 500 to 507, `IntegerPropertyFactory.java:929-935`): `judge.lane.N` (slots 93 to 100, `N` the `Lane` index, 0 the scratch, as the source's skin offsets for 7 keys, `play/LaneProperty.java:50`) is a code from 0 to 11. A judgment the reducer takes sets `j == 0 ? 1 : 2j + late` for the source's index `j` (PERFECT 0 to BAD 3, the empty POOR 5), late meaning the source's timing, note time minus press in µs, is 0 or less, so on time counts as late (`:671-672`); that timing is the one `judge.recent_ms` takes before its truncation. The events, each against its source line: a tap's `Judged` at its press (`:457-458`); a long note's `LnHeld`, 8, the normal long note in progress (`:438`, `:266` in autoplay); its `Judged` at its release or end, of the long note's judgment and the timing `judge.recent_ms` selects (`:533-542`, `:565-573`), so a release replaces the 8; a miss leaves the lane as it was (`:671`, the miss path's index 4, `:594-619`), whether at its deadline or passed by a press; an empty POOR, 10 or 11 by the side of the note it is against (`EmptyPoor::deviation`; the source's index 5, `:400-404`); a press that matches no note, a press after the lane's last note and a press on a lane with no notes included, clears it to 0 (`:460-462`). The press is known from the view's lane input, since no effect comes from it (the evaluator emits nothing, or only a sound), and it is ordered against the effect that set the code by time and, in one µs, by count: the reducer keeps that effect's time and its `presses` (its lane's presses before it in its µs, its own press included), and the lane reads 0 while its latest press (`LaneInput::pressed`) is later, or in the same µs with more presses there (`LaneInput::presses`). So a judgment at a press keeps its code, two presses taking two notes of one µs keep the second's, a press then a release in one µs keeps the release's judgment, and a release's judgment followed by a press in its µs (a reversal), a BAD press followed by a release and a press matching nothing, or a release, a press and a release all clear it; a deadline's judgment counts every press of its µs. A miss leaves the code, its time and its count. Two frames may differ from the source's: a press whose effects a drain has not delivered yet reads 0 until they arrive, since the view's input can be ahead of the effects (as for conditions, below); and a long note's early BAD release, which the source takes a frame later (`:539`, `:567`), here at the release. A BAD head decided at its press reads its BAD code (6 or 7) from that press, where the source's long note shows 8 until its release (`:428`, BAD vanishing), the preset's known difference. Every lane reads 0 before its first judgment, after a rearm and in a retry's fresh `Play`, and is unavailable while loading. The source's names for 1P's eight lanes alone are mapped; 508, 509 and 510 to 519 stay unsupported.
- **Conditions sample once per frame** after the drained effects are applied. Because the newest snapshot can be ahead of a drain that the effect buffer splits over frames (crates/player/src/render_side.rs:415-421), a condition may see a newer combo beside an older judgment for a frame; this is accepted and documented, and edge timers never derive from snapshot values that effects already carry.

**Feedback is not scoring.** A long note's `Judged` comes at its release or end, so feedback follows the existing play screen rules (crates/render/src/simple.rs:265-335):
- A press feedback (bomb, judgment display) comes from `Judged` at a press, or from `LnHeld` for a long note's head, never from the long note's completion, which is known from its own judgment and the chart (a release, or its end after a head press before it); a completed long note adds its judgment text but no second bomb, even when its `LnHeld` was dropped.
- The visual hold, a BAD head's included, comes from the play view's note facts, which start it at the head's press and end it at the release or the end; `LnHeld`, `LnHoldEnded` and a BAD head's `Judged` start only feedback, never the hold.
- `EmptyPoor` drives the empty-POOR feedback.

**Timers in stage 3:** `play`, `ready`, `failed` (from the play view's Closing start, `PlayView::closing`: the play-clock time the lifecycle entered Closing, on the displayed survival gauge's failure under `on_failure = "close"` or the quit key during play; never from `GaugeState::failed_at`, which stays the evaluator's; off when the displayed gauge fails under `play-to-end`, where only a quit during play starts it), `last_note` (at the notes' end `E` itself, from the first frame with `playing` whose play-clock time is at or past `E`, however late that frame comes), `full_combo`, `gauge_max` (off until `play` starts, `play/BMSPlayer.java:639`), `beat` (from the scroll map, on the display clock: the latest integer-beat crossing, which a STOP does not restart); `rhythm` (S10-13, the source's timer 140: the time since the latest bar line on the play clock at 1 000 ms per chart beat, `⌊Σ (at_{i+1} − at_i)·tempo_i · s / (1 200 · 10¹⁸)⌋` over the rows from that bar line to `now` with `s` the speed's twentieths, through a STOP at the tempo it holds; off before `play` starts; running while playing and through the completed tail outside Closing until the progress end + 5 s, its start held from then, from Closing's start and on any other terminal frame; off for a frame whose product overflows; D27); `fadeout` (the source's timer 2, from the play view's fade-out start, `PlayView::fading`: the play time a completed full play's hold ended, design.md "Play Lifecycle", Audio tail; never in Closing or a practice round, and off while loading, D30); `judge`, `judge.<grade>` (only the latest judgment's grade is on), `combo.up` (the field `combo` holds the count); per lane `press` and `release`, the key-on and key-off timers, which exclude each other (from the view's lane input, S9-02: while the lane is held, `press` from its latest press, or from `ready`'s start for a lane held since the opening, and `release` off; while it is released, `release` from its latest release, off before the first, and `press` off; µs origins the display offset moves neither, running whatever `playing` is), `bomb` (on a press judged up to the skin's `bomb_up_to` grade, empty POOR included; never with `None`), `hold` (the start of the lane's visual hold in the view's note facts, off once its long note's end is at or before `now`), `mine`; `screen`, the untimed elements' timer, at the loading lifetime's origin while loading and running on from the play state's screen origin into the play (D3); `loading`, at that origin while loading and off in a play; and `when(<condition>)` timers, which start on the condition's rising edge and turn off on its falling edge. A `when` condition may read state and built-in timers but not another `when` timer, so there is no cycle and no order to choose; every `when` timer is updated once per frame before any element is drawn. `gauge_max` reads off while the snapshot's gauge is below its maximum; if a below-maximum effect is dropped and the gauge later refills, it keeps the earlier start. Every timer has a fixed slot sized in `prepare`. The reducer is reset in `prepare`, so a retry starts clean. Its tests split one sequence of effects across drains at every point and compare against explicit expected states, and cover a mid-chart READY state, a practice round and two elements sharing one `when` timer.

**The loading runtime** (S10-03c-1, design.md "Rendering and Skins", loading state). `Skin::prepare_loading` makes a loading state from the compiled skin with no chart: its own fields, built-in timer starts and readings, `when` starts and readings, image-cycle state and loading text slots, and no notes, reducer, effects or `Play`. `Skin::build_loading` fills it from a `LoadingFrame` (`now` on the loading clock, the lifetime's origin as `LoadState::since`, the percent clamped to 0..=100, the target, the layout, the lanes, the stored covers, the display offset and the hi-speed fix, the chosen chart's play level if known and the descriptor of the shown gauge, `LoadingGauge`: look, maximum, border and its index among the preset's gauges, no start or value) and draws with the play's own field and timer evaluation and element drawing, so nothing is frozen at an initial value. The origin is the moment the play screen begins showing its loading state (today the moment a chart is chosen). At `now − since = 250 000 µs`, `loading` reads 250 ms; a progress of 40 reads 40. The lanes draw their judgment line from the frame's geometry with no notes or bar lines; the gauge draws its empty frame (every segment unlit, its below-border images below the border) from the descriptor and nothing without one; cover paint follows the frame's covers; judgment feedback never draws. Text slots draw what was installed in them (`Skin::install_loading_text`, `Skin::install_loading_bitmap_text`) and nothing once cleared (`Skin::clear_loading_text`), a set of slots named by the chart fields they show, a composite with its parts; installing or clearing never touches a timer. The build allocates nothing and returns `Result<(), BuildError>`, refusing stored covers or a hi-speed fix that `Play::build` would refuse (`InvalidReadouts`); after a refusal the state builds no more. Every PLAY field and built-in timer has one class for loading (`skin_model::loading_field`, `loading_timer`):

| Class | Fields and timers | While loading |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Live | `load.percent`, `autoplay`, the application's readouts (`app.fps`, `clock.year` to `clock.second`, `app.uptime_h`, `app.uptime_m`, `app.uptime_s`); the timers `loading` and `screen`; every `when` timer | From the loading frame; a `when` timer starts on its condition's rising edge |
| Setting | `cover.permille`, `lift.permille`, `hidden.permille`, `cover.absolute_permille` (the white numbers), `cover.enabled`, `lift.enabled`, `hidden.enabled`, `cover.adjusting`, `display_offset.ms`, `settings.fix_hispeed` | The frame's settings, from the presentation snapshot: its stored covers, its display offset and its hi-speed fix |
| Descriptor | `gauge.kind`, `gauge.valid` | Available while the frame gives a descriptor: the kind its look, `gauge.valid` true |
| Chosen | `chart.level`, `chart.level_valid`, `chart.difficulty`, `chart.difficulty_valid`, `chart.bga`, `chart.bga_valid`, `chart.length_ms` | Each available while the frame gives that datum of the chosen chart (`ChosenChart::play_level`, `difficulty`, `bga` and `length_us`, from its library entry's `#PLAYLEVEL`, `#DIFFICULTY`, BGA flag and summary's 1× length, never a previous attempt's) |
| Before | `time.progress_ppm` | Its READY value, 0, which a program and a progress slider read |
| Off | `ready`, `play`, `failed`, `last_note`, `full_combo`, `gauge_max`, `beat`, `rhythm`, `fadeout`, `judge`, `judge.<grade>`, `combo.up`, and per lane `press`, `release`, `bomb`, `hold`, `mine` | Known off: read as off, never unavailable |
| Unavailable | `judge.recent_ms`, `judge.recent_imperfect`, `judge.lane.0` to `judge.lane.7`, `combo`, `max_combo`, `ex_score`, `bp`, the `count.*` fields, `notes`, `gauge`, `rate.current_bp`, `rate.whole_bp`, the `bpm.*` fields, `time.remaining_s`, `time.elapsed_s`, `hispeed.x100`, `hispeed.valid`, `duration.ms`, `duration.green`, `duration.valid`, `chart.total`, `chart.total_valid`, `chart.main_bpm`, `chart.main_bpm_valid` (no library main tempo yet) | Not read |

Literals and the chosen chart's text slots are eligible. Since a program reads a missing field as its lower bound, unavailability is explicit: each final folded program's read set (both surviving arms of a conditional) is taken when the skin is loaded, and a program reading an unavailable field is rejected for loading, its number drawing nothing, its condition false, its selector drawing nothing and its `when` condition false, which clears a start it had. A program reading a descriptor field is checked each frame, so a descriptor that arrives after preparation lets it draw from that frame on. The gauge's value is unavailable whatever `gauge.valid` says. A play's frames are unchanged: `loading` stays off and `load.percent` reads 100.

**Lane input in the play view** *(coordination PR, §6 S9-02, done)*. Key beams and key pads need presses without notes and releases, which no effect carries: `PlaySound` comes only for a press with a target note that has a sound. `PlayView::input` (`eval::InputFacts`, design.md "Rendering and Skins", lane input) holds, per lane, whether it is held now and the evaluation times of its latest press and release, with the time conversion has reached, filled by the evaluator from every edge of the conversion that feeds it, so a scratch reversal (a release and a press at one time) and a control held at the opening without a press edge come out right. Autoplay's generated input fills it too. It is generic input state, not skin state: `SimpleSkin`'s key beam and the model's `press` and `release` timers read it.

**Fast/slow** are counts in `Tally` (§7, decision 6; design.md "Evaluation Stages", accounting), so the skin reads them from the play view like the other counts: `count.fast` and `count.slow`, fields 37 and 38, then each kind's early and late counts and the empty POORs', fields 39 to 50 (`field::COUNT` 51), counted as the reference player counts them: early at or before the note, a miss late, a long note on the side of its farther endpoint.

### R4. Expressions

Expressions give conditions, values and image selections (R6); they never move or color anything (§4).
- **Types:** `bool`, `int`, and enumerations (judgment grades, gauge kinds: `gauge.kind` takes `assist_easy`, `easy`, `normal`, `hard`, `ex_hard` and `hazard`). An enumeration compares for equality and inequality with its own variants; it has no arithmetic or ordering and never compares with an integer. Durations are written `120ms` or `1.5s`. There is no float and no fixed point: values that are fractions are integers in a stated unit (the gauge and the covers in ‰, the score rate in basis points (10 000 is 100 %, R3), BPM and hi-speed ×100, the travel duration in ms), and only an image's on-screen geometry is float, after evaluation.
- **Readout units and availability** (R3): `bpm.current_x100`, `bpm.min_x100`, `bpm.max_x100` and `hispeed.x100` are floored hundredths, `duration.ms` and `duration.green` ms, the cover fields ‰, `time.remaining_s` and `time.elapsed_s` whole seconds, and `bpm.change` and the switches `bool`. `chart.level` is the play level as `level_of` parses it, signed, between `−(2³¹ − 1)` and `2³¹ − 2`: the source's number widget draws nothing at `i32::MIN` and `i32::MAX` (`SkinNumber.java:139`), so those read unknown, and a callback reading such a level sees it unavailable, where the source compares the raw value. A value the play may not have carries a validity field (`bpm.current_valid`, `bpm.range_valid`, `bpm.change_valid`, `chart.level_valid`, `hispeed.valid`, `duration.valid`): unknown, it reads 0 with its validity false, which is distinct from a known 0 (a tempo below 0.01 BPM, an enabled cover at 0). An imported number showing it is drawn only under its validity, so it draws nothing while the value is unknown (§3).
- **Range-checked integers.** Every state field declares its range, and the compiler bounds every subexpression; an expression that could leave `[−2^31, 2^31 − 1]`, the range of the target's integer properties, is a compile error. Times read by expressions are in milliseconds (`elapsed` is milliseconds, −1 when off and at most 4 h, so a chart's time range fits and `elapsed(t) * 100` stays in range). State reads clamp to the declared range, so a value outside it can never break a bound the compiler proved. Within that bound the native evaluator and the exported Lua, whose numbers are Java `int` or `double`, compute the same results, so arithmetic needs no saturation. `/` and `%` floor and yield 0 for a zero divisor; the export lowers them to guarded prelude functions, since `math.floor(a / b)` alone does not give 0. Tests run both divisor signs and zero through the exported Lua as well as the native evaluator.
- **These rules are the authored language's.** The importer lowers a Lua callback into an expression only when the native operations give the same result as Lua's: a fractional division whose result is compared (`n / 2 > 0` is true at 1 in Lua and false under floor division) is not lowered. A play skin's callback that no expression represents compiles into the import-only presentation program (§3, S7-06), which keeps Lua's own numeric semantics, floats included; the authored language does not grow with it.
- **Operators and built-ins:** `+ - * / %`, comparisons, `&& || !`, `if c then a else b`, `min`, `max`, `clamp`, `abs`, `on(timer)`, `elapsed(timer)`, and option tests.
- **Pipeline:** a hand-written lexer and Pratt parser with spans, names resolved against a static schema (name → type, range, slot, export mapping), type and range checks, folding of options and constants, and a postfix program with its stack depth computed. The evaluator runs it on a fixed stack, allocates nothing, and is in `IN_PLAY`.
- **Bounds** (design.md threat model "Skins"): source length (1024 bytes), nesting depth (an operator height of 64, counting `clamp` as its lowering; checked while parsing, before recursion), operations per expression (256) and per skin (65536), and stack depth (32) are capped, so per-frame cost has a bound known at load.
- **Rejected:** `evalexpr` (AGPL since 12.0, allocates), `rhai` and `cel-interpreter` (dynamic, allocating), `fasteval` (unmaintained, float), `exmex` (untyped). A combinator library adds nothing a ~300-line Pratt parser needs.

### R5. Layout: canvas, regions and player adjustment

**Canvas.** The canvas is 1080 reference pixels tall and `1080 × aspect` wide; narrower than 16:9 it is 1920 wide and grows taller instead (§7, decision 3). The aspect is capped at 4:1 (4320 wide) and 1:2 (3840 tall), with bars beyond; the branch is decided in integers, so a target just narrower than 16:9 grows taller, never narrower. It is scaled uniformly to the render target. At 16:9 every coordinate is exactly what the author wrote, so authors keep drawing at 1920×1080. `Skin::canvas` computes it from the target size; a skin with `Fit::Fixed` (imports) uses 1920×1080 pillarboxed or letterboxed, as render's letterbox does today (crates/render/src/renderer.rs:681 at 8622dd7).

**Regions** are a fixed set defined by the format, so the player sees the same controls on every skin:

| Region | Holds | Player adjusts |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `playfield` | The lanes widget and every per-lane element, automatically | x, y, scale, lane width |
| `judge` | The judgment and combo widget, automatically; placed relative to `playfield` | x, y, scale, visible |
| `left`, `right`, `top`, `bottom` | HUD elements the author tags | x, y, scale, visible (when marked optional) |
| `screen` | Everything untagged, backgrounds (`fill = cover`, `stretch` or `none`) | nothing |

- Each region has a 9-point anchor; on a wider or taller canvas it shifts by `(i + fx·(cw − 2i − 1920), fy·(ch − 1080))` for anchor fractions `fx`, `fy` and side inset `i`. The player's inset keeps edge regions near the centre on very wide screens; it is clamped to `max(0, (cw − 1920) / 2)` each frame and never moves `screen`.
- A region's box is the union of its elements' key rects (unrotated; for an image choice, every present entry's track), computed at load; the point of its anchor in that box is its scale pivot. The box, its pivot and the clamp take the authored rects as if nothing followed the covers, so a moving cover never moves another element. A bar group (R6) stands in the lanes' region with its own pivot, binding, native size and `Follow`, as an image does; neither its keys nor its bars' travel enter the box.
- The transform order is: author coordinates → the follow translation (R6, "Followers") → lane width (playfield, horizontal) → region scale about the anchor point → region translation → anchor shift → canvas to target. A `fill` background on `screen` (`cover` or `stretch`) skips the regions and fills the canvas. `judge` is placed relative to `playfield`: it is scaled about its own anchor and translated by its own adjustment first, then moved by the playfield's translation and anchor shift, once, so the inherited move is never scaled; it does not take the playfield's scale or lane width. A test with the judge at a scale other than 100 % moves the playfield and checks the judge moves exactly as far.
- **Ranges and the clamp.** Adjustments are integers: offsets within ±4320 reference pixels, scale and lane width 50–200 %, inset ≤ 1200; `Layout` refuses anything else. Each frame the translation is clamped per axis so that the region's logical box (its box after lane width, scale, translation and anchor shift) overlaps the canvas by at least `min(extent, 64)` pixels. `judge` and `playfield` are clamped jointly: with `P` the playfield's range and `J` the judge's (its inherited anchor shift included), the judge's own offset is clamped to `[Jlo − Phi, Jhi − Plo]`, then the playfield's to `P ∩ (J − local)`, so the judge still moves exactly as far as the playfield. A range whose author placement overlaps less keeps 0. The clamp does not promise that rotated geometry or a pivot stays on screen; `visible` is the control for hiding.
- **Controls.** `Skin::controls` says what the skin offers per region (this table; lane width only with the lanes in `playfield`, none for a region without elements or under `Fit::Fixed`); the layout's value for a control the skin does not offer is ignored.
- **Lane width** scales lane-bound elements horizontally about the playfield's anchor; nine-slice frames keep their borders.
- **Judgment-line height** is the `playfield` y: the whole field moves, its top included, so the visible time is unchanged. Lift, which shortens the lane and changes the visible range, stays a play setting (design.md "Algorithms").
- **Storage:** layout settings outside the skin, keyed by skin and aspect class (16:9, 21:9, 32:9, 16:10), so a skin update keeps them; a reset returns a region to the author's layout; they are device settings, one file per skin under the data root's `layouts\` (§7, decision 2).
- **Adjusting:** an adjust mode on the ready screen. A key or the turntable selects a region, arrows or the turntable move it, a modifier scales it, and region outlines are drawn with sprites, so no `egui` is needed. The values are also editable in the settings file.
- **Imported skins** use `Fit::Fixed`: one `screen` region, pillarboxed at other aspects, never stretched. They get no composition adjustment, since `screen` has no controls.

**Resize.** Today `prepare` gets the GPU and the chart and `build` gets no viewport (crates/render/src/skin.rs:14-34, 63-66). `prepare` gains the target's pixel size, from which it picks @2x images and rasterizes text at final size, and `FrameInput` gains the target size, from which the skin computes the canvas each frame (`SkinFrame::target`), so a resize during play recomputes anchors and the canvas fit without allocating. Assets chosen by pixel size stay as prepared until the next `prepare`, which runs between plays; a scale change in the adjust mode takes effect on screen at once and in the assets at the next `prepare`. Tests cover two pixel sizes of one aspect, and a resize during a prepared play followed by the next `prepare`.

**Rejected:** free per-element offsets (every skin would invent its own and players would face a different set per skin), constraint or flex layout (hard to learn, no export), per-element editing (players do not think in elements), non-uniform stretch (distorts).

### R6. Widgets

Built in, because they carry player logic or the scroll math, which stays in the player:
- `lanes` (S5): notes, long-note head, active and inactive bodies, tail, mines, the judgment line, one image per part and lane, and bar line groups ("Bar line groups"). What SUDDEN+, LIFT and HIDDEN+ paint is elements of their own ("Cover paint").
  - **Geometry.** Explicit lane rects `[x, w]` with an authored scratch side, the scratch wholly outside every key; key lanes may overlap or coincide, each keeping its rect (no repacking, no x order). **Note order** (`LanesDef::note_order`): `LaneMajor` paints lane by lane in index order, each lane in chart order; `Timeline { lanes }`, a permutation of the eight lane indices checked at load (`LanesError::NoteOrder`), merges every lane's notes by their heads' `(position, time, rank in lanes, index in the lane)`, in integers, through at most eight cursors on the stack, each lane's range search and section limits kept, every candidate charged a visit before it is drawn or skipped; a long note's body, tail and head stay together at its head's place, and the shared visit and quad budgets, the bar groups first and the reserved line keep their order and drops. Scratch side, binding, lane width and `Follow` never change a rank. On the other side, the scratch moves to `span_start + span_end − x − w` and the key block to the near end, each key keeping its offset inside the block. Elements bound to a lane (`Bind::Lane`) or to the span (`Bind::Lanes`) map their x and w from the authored lane to the current one; `Skin::placement` takes the scratch side, which `build` reads from the attempt (`PlayContext::scratch`).
  - **Cover and lift.** The lift is in ‰ of the lane length and the cover in ‰ of the length left above the lift, each up to 1000, so together they may hide the whole lane. The lane length is `line − top` for the authored lanes (`LaneStyle::Simple`) and the source's lane height for an imported skin (`LaneStyle::Source`). The lanes paint no cover. The notes scroll at the exact original-lane gain (`LaneView::gain`, S10-04b-1) over the whole lane length whatever covers it; the scroll span, the uncovered length, only bounds what is drawn, and the lift raises the judgment line. With no span left (LIFT or the cover at 1000) nothing is scaled or visited: both styles draw only the line, so with no field left no note geometry is drawn and future notes never collapse onto the line; without a gain only the line is drawn too. These `LaneView` amounts are the effective ones: the configured amount while its cover is on and 0 while it is off. The configured amounts, kept while a cover is off, and the switches are readouts (R3) that nothing paints, and `build` refuses a frame where the two disagree.
  - **Hidden cover.** HIDDEN+ (`LaneView::hidden`) is in ‰ of the length left above the lift, up to 1000; the caller passes 0 while the player has it off. With `L` the lift's length, its share is `K = (lane length − L)·hidden/1000` (`play/LaneRenderer.java:335-345`). It is an overlay: the notes under it are drawn and the scroll span does not change. The lanes paint none of it ("Cover paint").
  - **Cover paint.** What SUDDEN+, LIFT and HIDDEN+ paint is ordinary elements (`Source::CoverPaint`, a `CoverPaintDef` in `WidgetDefs::cover_paints`): an image, or an image choice table's entry on that entry's track and native size (R6), drawn in the element's place in the draw order with its own track, timer, visibility, blend, clip and placement. It moves by its `Follow`, by the cover it paints (`CoverMotion`: none, `Lift` up by `L`, or `Hidden` up by `L + K`, from the frame's one lane geometry, so a `Follow::Cover` adds SUDDEN+'s slide once) and, as a slider (`Slider`: `R` reference pixels up to `MAX_COORD`, a rate and a direction, up, right, down or left, `skin/SkinSlider.java:116-120`), by `R` times its rate toward its direction: SUDDEN+'s, `R · cover · (1000 − lift) / 10⁶` of the frame's effective amounts, 0 while SUDDEN+ is off, or the progress's, `R · time.progress_ppm / 10⁶` (R3), each an exact integer numerator divided once in `f64`. A progress slider paints no cover: it is the source's progress bar, drawn on this path. Edges keep their fractions until drawn. A `Hidden` paint draws only while HIDDEN+ is on (`CoverSettings::hidden_enabled`), at any amount, 0 included.
  - **Trim.** A trim line (`CoverTrim`: none, `Fixed(t)`, or `Lift(b)` at `t = b − L`, y down) gates a paint against its gate rect `[x, y, w, h]`: its `gate_track`, the unstretched destination sampled on the element's timer and moved as the paint is, or else the paint's own sampled rect, before any native size or rotation (`play/SkinHidden.java:61-90`, `skin/SkinObject.java:649-667`). With `y ≥ t` it draws nothing; with `y < t < y + h` it draws under the clip `[x, y, w, t − y]`, mapped through the binding and the region's transform but never rotated, and nothing when that clip has no area (`skin/Skin.java:567`); otherwise it draws unclipped, so a fitted image whose destination lies wholly above the line overhangs it. The quad keeps its whole uv, pivot and rotation: the clip is a `ClipRect` of finite positive area carried by the batch's run (`QuadBatch::push_clipped`, `Run::clip_rect`), merged only with a neighbour of the same key and clip, which the renderer intersects with the element's own clip (§6, "Render integration"). A hidden gate track draws nothing.
  - **Paint and coverage.** A paint never changes the coverage, the scroll span or the notes: a hidden, transparent, timer-off or empty paint draws nothing and nothing replaces it, so the lanes with all three covers may still hide the whole lane. Bounds and the variant use the paint's authored tracks; its gate, trim and motion add nothing. `Skin::new` checks every definition, referenced or not, refusing (`LoadError::CoverPaint`, naming the paint) an unknown image or table, a slider range past `MAX_COORD`, a slider with a motion, a trim or a gate (`SliderMotion`), a gate without a trim, a gate outside the coordinates or of negative size, and a trim base past `MAX_COORD`; then a trimmed element without a gate whose own or choice tracks have a negative size (`LoadError::CoverGate`), and a moving paint that follows the lift, or HIDDEN+'s paint that follows HIDDEN+ (`UnsupportedFollow`), since its motion already holds it. A paint's slider extends the coordinate check by `R` toward its direction, on x for a horizontal one, a moving paint's tracks and gate are checked moved by a whole lane length as a follower's, and a linked trim base lowered by it must stay within `MAX_COORD`. A gate's keys count toward `MAX_SKIN_KEYS` once.
  - **Followers.** An image, number, gauge or text element (bitmap text included), a notes graph, BPM graph, timing visualizer or hit-error visualizer, and a judgment display's grade part carry a `Follow`: a set of `lift`, `cover` and `hidden`. With `L` the lift's length, `C` SUDDEN+'s slide and `K` HIDDEN+'s own share this frame (R3), it moves by the sum of `−L`, `+C` and `−K` over its set, in reference pixels, as the source adds its offsets (`skin/SkinObject.java:401-410`), and by 0 in a skin without lanes. With lanes, a follower of HIDDEN+ is not drawn while HIDDEN+ is off (`play/LaneRenderer.java:335-346`): an element, a bar group or a grade's paint is skipped, but a timing visualizer draws its lines and not its band, as the source clears only its destination's alpha and the lines keep their own colour and alpha (`skin/SkinTimingVisualizer.java:138-148`), and a grade's combo still draws, attached to it, as the source only clears the follower's alpha (`skin/SkinObject.java:474-481`, `play/SkinJudge.java:121-137`). It follows HIDDEN+'s cover as drawn (D9). The importer admits a follower of HIDDEN+ only on a fixed colour with no configured alpha above 0, refusing it with `OffsetAlpha` otherwise: the source skips every alpha offset at an interpolated colour and clamps each in turn (`skin/SkinObject.java:474-504`), so such a follower would show where the model hides it. `Follow::translate` moves the sampled rect's y in `f64`, keeping x, w and h, before the lane map and the region transform (R5), so the pivot moves with the rect, a region's scale scales the motion, and tracks, clocks, clips and run order are unchanged; a text's raster request does not change. The combo has no `Follow`: it attaches to its grade's translated rect. The lanes and the judgment display as a whole refuse a `Follow` (`LoadError::UnsupportedFollow`), and `Skin::new` refuses a follower whose authored keys, moved by a whole lane length (`ceil` of the source height) either way, leave ±`MAX_COORD`.
  - **Notes leave** on the authored lanes when judged (a tap) or at their time or when hit (a mine); a long note when its end passes below the line.
  - **An imported skin's notes** draw as its source does (`play/LaneRenderer.java:546-581, 701-727`), which draws nothing below the line: a note sits on its time by its lower edge, `offset y` above the lanes' bottom, its geometry kept unrounded; a tap or mine stays until its time, judged or not; a long note stays while its end is ahead (`display < end`, its end above its head or the line), its body from the end's lower edge to the head's upper edge, and its end cap is drawn only when the end is judged on its own (CN or HCN: the chart's mode, else `ln_default`).
  - **Bar line groups.** The lanes hold a table of groups (`LanesDef::bar_groups`, a `BarGroupDef` each, at most `MAX_BAR_GROUPS`, 16 384), each an image with its own track, timer, visibility, blend and placement (pivot, binding, native size and `Follow`) in the lanes' region, with no fill; its colour multiplies the lanes' tint and the lanes' clip still applies. At each chart bar line at position `p` a group's sampled rect, moved by its `Follow`, moves by `−(p − at_line) · px_per_pos` of the frame's scale, so at the display head it stands where its track places it, whatever the notes' height or `offset y`. It draws its whole quad, uncropped, when its vertical extent after the region's transform overlaps the lanes' band by a positive height; touching the band draws nothing, and with no scroll span no bar draws. Bars draw before the line and the notes, in chart bar order and then group order, through a min-heap of the groups' cursors, each cursor over the bars a conservative window holds (the bounds inverted through the scale, widened by `256 · f32::EPSILON · M` for the construction's rounding, `M` the largest magnitude involved, then rounded outward). Each group is sampled once a frame, its visibility evaluated once; a hidden, timer-off or empty group visits nothing. A `Play` holds a frame record and a heap slot per group, made in `prepare` only when the skin has groups and lent to the lanes for each `build`. `Skin::new` refuses more groups than the cap before building any, and a group with an unknown timer, a non-`bool` visibility, coordinates or a `Follow` reach past `MAX_COORD`, another region or a fill (`LanesError::BarGroup`, naming the group); a group's keys count toward `MAX_SKIN_KEYS` and its visibility toward `MAX_SKIN_OPS`.
  - **Judgment-time placement**: the declarative skin draws notes, bar lines and the `beat` timer with the exact playhead (design.md "Rendering and Skins", S6c-13), which meets every note at its integer-µs judgment time; only D1 rounding of the final geometry remains. `SimpleSkin`, the default renderer, draws its notes and bar lines with the same playhead (S10-06a), verified on WARP from a fractional-time chart whose sampled-map placement was tens of pixels off; this closes https://github.com/shuymn/tapestry/issues/167.
  - **Section targets.** In a practice round only the section's targets are drawn (notes from its start, ending before its end, as the evaluator decides), and once evaluation is complete the round rings out without notes (`SkinFrame::complete`).
  - **Budget.** `LANE_QUADS` (8 192) quads for notes and bar lines plus 1 reserved for the line; every cover paint is an image quad of its own, emitted whether or not the notes ran out, and its gate adds none (followers take no reservation of their own), and `LANE_VISITS` (16 384) candidates visited, charged before any skip (judged, outside the section, culled); a bar line costs a visit per group that considers it and a quad per group that draws it, from the same counters, so a skin's groups never multiply the budget; running out stops the frame's lanes with one drop.
  - **Cropping.** A part crossing the lanes' top or bottom is cut with its uv, never squashed; a bar line draws whole ("Bar line groups").
  - The lanes' track, like the judgment display's, gives its color and alpha only; geometry or an angle is a load error. Number and gauge elements are placed by their track like images.
  - The scroll math is today's `layout::Scale` (crates/render/src/simple.rs:60-115), copied into `skin-model` parameterized by the span and the line, until render switches over and then deleted from render; with no cover or lift every output bit equals render's, and a test reads render's source to guard the copy. Render's own lanes draw no cover or lift yet; they take the model's (`LaneStyle::Simple`) when render switches over.
- `number` (S5b): a glyph strip (`DigitsDef`) showing an `int` expression over the play schema, charged to the op budget once per element that draws it.
  - **Glyphs.** `Ten` (digits), `Eleven` (a padding glyph 10) or `TwentyFour` (12 glyphs for values at or above 0, then 12 for values below, each half with the padding glyph 10 and a sign 11). An image of `k` times as many frames holds `k` glyph sets, cycled over the sets on the image's own timer; set 0 shows while that timer is off.
  - **Cells.** 1 to `MAX_CELLS` (11: the 10 digits of an `i32` and a sign). Cells fill from the right with the digits of `|v|` (`unsigned_abs`, so `i32::MIN` shows every digit); a value longer than the strip keeps its low digits. Left of the digits, the padding: zero, glyph 10, or blank. With `TwentyFour`, zero or glyph padding gives cell 0 to the sign, and blank padding puts the sign in the first blank left of the digits, so a value filling every cell shows no sign; without `TwentyFour` a negative value shows its magnitude.
  - **Geometry.** The track's rect is cell 0; cell `j` is at `x + p·j − s` with the pitch `p = w + space` (space signed, within ±2^20) and `s` 0, `p·b` or `p·b/2` for right, left or centre alignment over `b` blank cells. Each cell turns about its own 9-point pivot. A bound number maps each cell's x and w through the lane map, so the spacing follows the lane width. Its logical box is, per key, cell 0's rect with cell `n − 1`'s.
  - **Digit offsets** (S6c-09). `DigitsDef::offsets` holds at most one `DigitOffset { dx, dy, dw, dh }` per cell, in target pixels with y down, each component within ±2^20; offset `j` belongs to fixed cell `j` (cell 0 the leftmost) whatever the alignment, padding, sign or blanks, and a cell past the table has none. At placement (`Brush::quad_offset`) the cell's rect is placed and mapped to the canvas as without one, the offset divided by the canvas's scale is added in `f64`, and only then is a native size (stretch 8 and 9) fitted into the moved rect and the 9-point pivot taken from it, so the delta is the same at every target size and overlapping cells keep their cell order. A zero offset is none. The logical box, the region, the variant and a combo's shift keep the unmoved cells: an offset moves paint only, and the logical box may not cover it. `Skin::new` checks each strip an element draws, as it checks the strip's other parameters (more offsets than cells is `NumberError::OffsetCount`, a component outside ±2^20 `OffsetRange`); a strip no element draws is not checked and never drawn. It counts the offsets of every number and every judge combo it defines, drawn or not, against `MAX_DIGIT_OFFSETS` (65 536), `LoadError::TooManyDigitOffsets` past it, before anything is loaded; `Skin::digit_offset_count` gives the count. A loaded strip owns its offsets, so `DigitsDef` and `Digits` are no longer `Copy`.
- `judge` (S5b): one grade image per slot (`JudgeSlot`: PERFECT, GREAT, GOOD, BAD, POOR, empty POOR, PERFECT at a full gauge) and one optional combo strip per slot, each part with its own track, timer, 9-point pivot, blend and visibility.
  - **What it shows.** The reducer's latest judgment's slot, nothing before the first one. PERFECT while `gauge_max` is on uses the full-gauge slot; its grade image and its combo each fall back to PERFECT's on their own. The combo shows the snapshot's `combo` field, only when it is above 0, the slot has a combo, and the combo part's timer is on and its visibility true; it reads no value from the effect, which may be behind the snapshot (R3). Nothing is drawn while the grade part is not.
  - **Placement.** A grade paints its own track and attaches its combo to its attachment rect: its painted rect, or with an attachment track (`GradePart::attachment`) that track's rect. An attachment track has the grade's key times, ease and end; only its geometry is read, it has no timer, visibility, image, cycle, `Follow` or blend of its own, and it is sampled at the grade's local time. Both rects take the grade's follow translation once. The combo's track is relative to the attachment rect, sampled on the combo's own timer: the combo has no `Follow` of its own and moves with its grade: x from its left edge, y from its top or bottom edge (`Attach`). With `shift`, the grade's paint moves left by half the combo's baseline width `p·(cells − blanks)`, its digit offsets ignored; neither the attachment nor the combo moves. Native sizing fits the paint after the shift, and the grade turns about its painted rect; neither changes the attachment. The combo's cells are pushed first, then the grade, each under its part's blend and the element's clip, so a judgment display is at most two runs. The element's own track multiplies its color and alpha into both parts.
  - **Region.** In `judge` (in `screen` under `Fit::Fixed`) and never bound to the lanes. Its logical box is the union over the effective states (each slot, and the full-gauge PERFECT with its fallbacks) of the grade's keys and the combo's strip boxes moved over the range of the attachment keys' corner on the attach edge, with the grade's box widened on both sides by half the widest strip when `shift` is on; an attachment rect is never drawn, so its own box is not included. Every part's keys, attachment tracks' included, count toward the skin's key limit, in every stored definition, referenced or not; a referenced judge's attachment tracks are refused with timing other than their grade's (`JudgeError::AttachmentTiming`) and keep their coordinates within ±`MAX_COORD`, moved by the lanes' length when their grade follows the covers (`JudgePart::Attachment`). Part visibilities are charged per element.
- `gauge` (S5b): the shown gauge (`SkinFrame::gauge`) as segments of the track's rect.
  - **Looks.** Front, back and tip images, each at or above and below the border, for each look (`GaugeLook`: assist easy, easy, normal, hard, ex hard, hazard). The descriptor's look comes from the preset gauge's id, or for another id from its kind (an end-threshold gauge is normal, a survival gauge hard); the schema's `gauge.kind` (R3) and the importer's gauge-type conditions read that same look, so no other classification exists. Its border is the clear threshold or the value a survival gauge fails below.
  - **Segments.** `P` segments (1 to `MAX_SEGMENTS`, 16 384, the widget quad budget). With the value `V` and the maximum `M`, `n = 0` lit segments when `V ≤ 0`, else `⌊V·P/M⌋` within 1 to `P`, so any non-zero gauge lights one; segment `i` (from 1) is below the border when `i·M < border·P`. The count is independent of the ‰ gauge field: the widget reads the gauge's exact value (`|V·P| < 2^77`, in `i128`), so a maximum of 10 000 000 lights 8 192 of 16 384 at 5 000 000 and 8 193 at 5 000 611, both 500 ‰. A referenced gauge reserves `P` quads, one more for an effective flicker even at a value or tip alpha of 0, per drawing element against the shared budget (R6, "Budgets"), so 16 384 flickering segments are refused (`LoadError::TooManyWidgetQuads`) while 16 384 still ones or 16 383 flickering ones fit alone. A motion's range may reach `P`, its modulus `P + 1`. Segments are drawn in order, the tip right after its segment, and a full batch keeps the ordered prefix.
  - **Motions,** pure functions of the element's timer, with `k = ⌊t / step⌋` and the offset `a`: still (`a = 0`), random (`a` = splitmix64 of `k` mod `range + 1`), rise (`a = −k mod (range + 1)`), fall (`a = k mod (range + 1)`). Segment `n` is the tip, below it front while `i + a < n`, the rest back. Flicker draws front up to `n` and back after it, and overlays the tip on segment `n` with alpha `1 − |2φ − T|/T` at the phase `φ` of the period `T`. These differ from the target: its flicker curve differs (1.032 against 0.970 at 16 ms of a 33 ms period), though its phase follows elapsed time modulo the period as here; its rise, fall and random take at most one step per prepare call once the step's time has passed, drawn or not, then schedule the next a step after that time (`play/SkinGauge.java:110`), rather than following elapsed time, so their phase and catch-up after a frame gap differ; and its random sequence differs.
  - Each segment turns about its own 9-point pivot, and a bound gauge maps x and w through the lane map.
- `text` (S5c): one line (`TextDef`) in a font file of the sheet (`FontRef::File`) or the player's text font (`FontRef::Default`: the menu font in the player, the test font in the preview and goldens).
  - **Strings.** A literal of 1 to 256 bytes with no control character (C0, DEL or C1), or a `TextField`: title, subtitle, artist, subartist, genre, and the full title and full artist, joined with a space when the second part is not empty (refs 12 and 16, the most used). The caller passes `ChartText`, for display only, from one of two sources. A loading frame's is the chosen chart's static top-level header (what `bms_parser::read_header` reads and the library stores, as the select screen shows it): headers inside `#IF`, `#SWITCH` or `#SETSWITCH` blocks are ignored, overrides of a top-level value included, and a field with no top-level declaration is empty. An attempt's, from READY on, is the labels of the chart's ingestion (`game::ChartLabels`), resolved through the branches it took, so a title declared only inside a taken branch is empty while loading and shown from READY. Table, target, rival and player names do not exist in tapestry; string expressions are not in the model, and the one first-non-empty fallback is a `TextValue::FieldOr`: a field, or a literal where the field's string is empty, tested after a full field is joined and before control characters are dropped.
  - **Requests.** `Skin::text_requests(&ChartText, target, &Layout)` runs at `prepare` and may allocate. Per text element (its slot is its place among the text elements in draw order) whose visibility does not fold to false: the string, control characters dropped after the full fields are joined, cut to 256 bytes at a character boundary; an empty one gives no request. The em is `min(largest key h × canvas scale × the region's effective scale, 256)` pixels, from the exact fractions `variant` uses (`Fit::Fixed` ignores layout adjustments). An element in a hidden region still asks, since the adjust mode can show it.
  - **Table.** The rasterizer (S8) answers a request with a `TextImage`: its uv and layer, the ink rect and the pen advance, in em. `TextImage::new` derives the line's extent, `left = min(0, ink.x)` and `width = max(advance, ink.x + ink.w) − left`, and refuses a non-finite value, ink 0 wide or tall, a negative advance, and any value or extent past ±4 096 em. `Skin::attach_text` checks the table's length against the text slots and gives it to the `Play`, which every `prepare` makes without one, so a table never outlives its attempt.
  - **Layout.** The track's rect `[x, y, w, h]` is the box: `h` is the em, and the box's top is the font's cap height, which the rasterizer folds into the ink rect. With `tw = width·h`, `Shrink` squeezes horizontally to `sx = w / tw` only when `tw > w`, else `sx = 1`, so it never enlarges; the drawn width `d = tw·sx` sits at offset 0, `(w − d)/2` or `w − d` for left, centre and right, and `Spill` overflows (centred text both ways). The quad is `[x + off + (ink.x − left)·h·sx, y + ink.y·h, ink.w·h·sx, ink.h·h]`, turned about the element's 9-point pivot on the box, sampled linearly, with the track's color and alpha over white premultiplied coverage. A line drawn 0 wide, a slot without an image and a table of another length draw nothing. The metrics are in em, so the raster size changes only sharpness, and an animated `h` rescales the line uniformly. The text stays as prepared through a resize (R5).
  - **Bitmap layout** (`FontRef::Bitmap`, S6b-3). `Skin::attach_bitmap_text(&mut Play, &ChartText)` runs at `prepare` beside `attach_text`, chooses and cleans the string as the requests do, and lays out each bitmap slot whose visibility does not fold to false as the source's runtime lays out one run without tight bounds (gdx 1.9.9 `GlyphLayout`), in em of the font's `size`: the pen starts at 0 and moves by each glyph's advance plus the kerning to the next, applied when the previous glyph is the primary's own; the line's width is the last pen plus its advance.
