A glyph's ink is `[pen + xoffset, yoffset − yAdj − ascent, width, height] / size` from the box's top, with `ascent = base − (capHeight − padTop − padBottom)` and, for a glyph from a fallback, `yAdj = fallback.rep − primary.rep`, `rep` being the raw `yoffset` of the first of `S P A N O T H E R [ ] M 0` the font has (else its first char line with a height). These cap-height and alignment probes are the source's: authors placed text against them, so imported text lands where its author put it. A character missing from the primary comes from the fallbacks in order, then the wave dash and fullwidth tilde stand in for each other, then `□ ▢ ☐ ■ ?` in the primary and each fallback, then the primary's missing glyph (`char id=0` or `-1`), else it is skipped; a substitute carries no kerning. Every character is treated alike whatever its plane: a supplementary glyph kerns, falls back to the missing glyph and counts as a font's drawn glyph as a basic-plane one does, where the source's runtime stores only the basic plane. A glyph 0 wide or tall advances but draws nothing, and a line with a value past ±4 096 em gets none. Each frame `Text::draw_glyphs` places the line as the outline text's layout does, with `width` the line's (a line of width ≤ 0 still draws, never squeezed), one quad per glyph `[x + off + ink.x·h·sx, y + ink.y·h, ink.w·h·sx, ink.h·h]`, its uv found inside its page's frame in the atlas that frame (`(u1 − u0)/page width` is exactly one texel), sampled linearly; a page frame missing from the uvs draws nothing. A fresh `prepare` has no bitmap lines.
  - **Load checks** in `Skin::new`'s order: more than 64 text elements is `TooManyTexts`; `UnknownWidget` covers a missing `TextDef`; a font index past the sheet's fonts is `UnknownFont`; a text element bound to the lanes is `Bound`, one with a fill `Fill`, and one with a key of `h ≤ 0` or `w < 0` `Size`; a bad literal is `Literal`. Text charges no ops beyond its visibility. Its region box is its keys' rects: spilled ink does not count. `Skin::variant` skips text, which has its own pixel size.
  - **Fonts.** `Sheet::with_fonts` takes at most 16 font files, each name at most 1 024 bytes, resolved inside the skin folder to a regular file of at most 32 MiB, 64 MiB in all, whose first 4 bytes are `00 01 00 00`, `OTTO`, `true` or `ttcf`; `skin-model` parses none of them. Bitmap `.fnt` fonts (BMFont text) come through `SheetBuilder::admit_fnt(name, charge)`: in order, a font slot (outline fonts and distinct `.fnt` files share the 16), one file attempt, the name and confinement checks, a regular file of at most 4 MiB, its length charged to the 64 MiB and never refunded, the read, then `FntFile::parse` with the per-font caps (16 384 glyphs and 16 384 kerning pairs, a synthesised space counted) or the sheet's remaining 65 536 of each if fewer; then each page in turn charged its attempt, bytes, image and frame and admitted as a whole-file image of one linear frame, all pages or none, each glyph's rectangle inside its page, and each page decoded once and dropped, so a corrupt page fails its font rather than the atlas. Every charge stays spent when the admission fails. Two counters bound the bytes, each at 512 MiB a variant: the importer's ledger counts every font page charged, failed admissions included, and so bounds the page reading across admissions; the builder's totals count only the files the sheet keeps, ordinary images and admitted pages, and so bound what the atlas reads. A page of a failed admission counts only in the ledger, so failed pages and kept ordinary files together can reach about 1 GiB, while font pages stay within 512 MiB. `charge` is called before each guarded step and its refusal aborts. `add_bitmap_font(primary, fallbacks)` forms a chain (at most 15 fallbacks, 64 chains) that `FontRef::Bitmap` names. A sheet holding bitmap fonts takes no fonts through `with_fonts` (`FontError::BitmapFonts`): its outline fonts are admitted with `admit_font`, so both kinds share the slots and bytes. The parser is strict: every byte UTF-8 but the quoted value of line 1's own `face` attribute, each line's encoding checked before its fields, and a carriage return allowed only before a line feed; line 1 is `info` with `size` and a `padding` of four integers; line 2 `common` with `lineHeight`, `base`, `scaleW`, `scaleH`, `pages` at their positions (pages 1 to 16); then the page lines `id file` with ids in order; then the `char` lines `id x y width height xoffset yoffset xadvance page` (later keys ignored), ids `-1` or `0` the missing glyph, 1 to 0x10FFFF glyphs, no duplicate; then the `kerning` lines `first second amount`, amounts −128 to 127, only blank lines after them. Values are at most 16 384, `chars count` is declared at most once, a declared `count` must match the lines read, and a refused file is a file-local `FntError`. As the source stores them, a duplicate kerning pair's last wins, a pair whose first glyph is absent or whose second is past 0x10FFFF is dropped, and a missing space takes the advance of `l` or the first glyph drawn with a size. A font with no glyph drawn with a size (the missing glyph aside) is refused.
  - Not in the model: wrapping, truncation, outline, shadow and editable text; the surveyed play skins use none of them.
- **Budgets.** At most `cells` quads for a number, `segments` (one more with flicker) for a gauge, the most over a judgment display's effective states of 1 plus the resolved combo's cells, and 1 for a text, or for one in a bitmap font its literal's characters or 256 for a field. The skin's widget quads are capped at `MAX_WIDGET_QUADS` (16 384, the element limit). `quad_capacity` adds them to the images' and the lanes', so the worst batch is 40 961 entries (16 384 image quads, cover paints among them, 16 384 widget quads, `LANE_QUADS` and the line's reservation), at `64 + size_of::<Run>()` B an entry: 2 621 504 B of quads (64 B each) and 1 474 596 B of runs (36 B each, an inline clip among them), 4 096 100 B without container overhead.
- `image choice` (S6c-04): a table of entries (`ImageChoiceDef`), each an `ImageChoicePart` or empty, and an `int` selector, charged to the op budget once per element that keeps it.
  - **Selection,** each frame: a negative value hides the element, a value inside the table draws that entry, any other value (`i32::MAX` included) entry 0, and an empty selected entry hides it, so `i32::MIN` hides.
  - **Entries.** An entry is a run of one image's frames (`ImageSpan`: the image, its first frame and their count) with its own cycle on its own timer (R2). It may keep its own track, sampled on the element's timer while it is selected, and its own native size (R7); the element's own `Place::native` must then be none (`LoadError::ChoiceNative`).
  - **Load checks,** every table whether an element references it or not: a selector of another type (`ImageChoiceError::NotInt`), no entries (`Empty`), and per entry an unknown image, a run outside its image's frames, a cycle period outside 1 µs to the longest key time, an unknown cycle timer and a track leaving the model's coordinates. More tables than `MAX_ELEMENTS` is `TooManyWidgets`; more than `MAX_IMAGE_CHOICE_REFS` (65 536) entries over the tables, empty ones and repeats counted, `TooManyImageChoiceRefs`. An entry's track counts toward the key cap once per table, and a follower's entry tracks are checked as its own (Followers).
  - **Budget.** One quad per element.
- `bga`.
- **Screen widgets** (planned; their import is §3's "Screen profiles"). Each draws from its screen's typed snapshot, allocates nothing per frame and charges the widget quad budget for each element that draws it:
  - `song list` (S10-09d, built in the model): the selected and unselected row placements (1 to 60, the source's own cap) with their text, level, lamp and label styles over the snapshot's visible rows, a bounded window around the cursor. Slot `i` shows row `cursor + i − center`; a slot past the list's ends draws nothing, so the list never wraps (owner decision), and a slot whose placement does not draw draws none of its parts. Each part's keys are an offset from its placement's sampled bottom-left corner, as the source draws every part at its bar's y-up origin; the parts draw pass by pass, bars, texts, lamps, levels, then labels (long notes, mines, random). A row's style is chosen by its kind (chart, folder, "all charts"); the importer resolves each kind's image and text style. Row text and values bind per row; a lamp style reads the typed own lamp (`LampKey`, from the cleared gauge's look; a hazard clear is a full combo's), never a formatted string; a style whose fact the row lacks draws nothing. Rows snap: the source's slide between slots is a stage-3 follow-up.
  - `ratio bar` (S10-09d, built in the model): a bar cut to an exact integer ratio of its width from the left or its height from the bottom, uv alike, its ratio never clamped as the source's graph's is not, though a ratio above 1 draws the whole box (a deviation: the source extends its region past the image there, `skin/SkinGraph.java:100-105`, which an atlas cannot give, which valid data reaches only on a graph whose numerator may exceed its denominator, the best max combo over a drawing chart's summary notes (graph 145); a best EX never passes the largest), or a slider moved by the ratio of its range, for the list position and a valid EX-rate bar (none without a best or with a largest EX of 0). A press on a changeable slider's travel, as the last build drew it, gives the ratio under it as a command (`Skin::hit_at` for a press, `Skin::drag_at` for a drag); the skin never changes state itself.
  - `gauge history` and `timing distribution` (S10-09h-1, built in the model): the view's emphasized gauge as its 288 condensed columns up to the input's coverage end, so an aborted play's line stops there: a back split at the gauge's border, and a line 2 units thick that stays in its columns and takes one colour per stroke unless its values cross the border, revealed left to right over 1.5 s of the screen timer, with a white mark one unit wide where a what-if's recorded input ends (no failure mark: a survival failure reads as the drop to 0); a view without an emphasized gauge draws no gauge graph. The distribution's own histogram: the timing statistics' samples in 1 ms slots from −150 to +150 ms by their ms truncated toward zero, early positive (`ResultGraphs::deviations`), separate from the built-in 2 ms histogram, whose samples, overflow counts and recommended offset are unchanged. It draws one ms a column around its centre (`width / lineWidth` columns, the ±150 ms endpoints included) over the view's key-lane judge areas, with ticks every 10 columns, the mean and ±σ lines and the bars from the same samples, scaled at 10 rows or the step of 10 above the largest slot, whatever range the graph shows; without a sample only the areas and ticks draw. Each graph's layers are composed as the result graphs' are (design.md "Rendering and Skins", premultiplied colour).
  - `judgment timeline` and `early/late timeline` (S10-09h-2): the result's timelines (S10-09g, `game::Timelines`): 1 s bins over chart time from the play's start (a round's section start) to the attempt's last judged note, at most 512, past which every bin takes the whole second that keeps the count within 512 (a 600 s chart has 301 bins of 2 s), the same under every view. Each bin has nine channels: PERFECT, then GREAT, GOOD, BAD and POOR early, then the same four late, the side the evaluator's totals count (a long note by the farther of its head's press and its release, a miss late); the six channels of a judgment graph are their sums. A judgment counts when it was finalized at or before the input's coverage end and sits in its note's bin, so a long note finalized at its release sits at its head; mines and empty POORs are no notes of it. Every judged note of the attempt (taps and long notes, mines excluded, within a round's section) is counted in its bin as well (the result's basis), and each one without a counted judgment draws grey ("not judged") in its bin, covered or not, a note after an abort in the abort's own bin and a long note still held included; the grey chips count in the graph's scale. The graph stacks each bin's channels from the bottom (grey first, or last with `orderReverse`) in chips of a generated grid, cut at its scale: 20 cells, or the step of 10 above the largest bin, at most 100.
  - `score graph`, `notes graph`, `bpm graph`, `timing visualizer` and `hit-error visualizer` (S10-10): the play's current, best and target EX over the chart with their rank marks; the chart's notes by time and kind and its tempo over time, from its prepared summary (on selection, the cursor's chart's scan summary); the latest judgments' offsets from a ring reserved at preparation. Each sized at preparation and drawn without allocating.

Composed by authors from elements, per-lane repetition and timers: key beams, bombs, key pads, progress (a `bar`), frames and backgrounds.

### R7. Images and scaling

Before play, never per frame, so per-frame cost is unchanged apart from trilinear sampling. Loading a skin (`Sheet::new`) checks its files and images and reads only their headers; the atlas (`Sheet::atlas`) decodes, packs and builds the mips for the chosen variant whenever that variant changes, between plays and off the in-play path, so a retry keeps it. This is how the loading thread and `prepare`'s pick of @2x (R5) fit together. A file whose dimensions changed since load is refused, and the player keeps its current skin. A sheet also holds generated images of one colour (`SolidImageDef`, `SheetBuilder::add_solid_image`), such as the importer's fallback note paint and the source's built-in black and white: one frame, never animated, at one size in both variants; each charges an image, a frame and both variants' texel totals but no file, attempt or byte, its cells sort after the files', and the atlas writes its premultiplied colour through the whole allocation, gutter included, after the files are read, reading nothing for it. A generated grid (`SolidImageDef::grid`) paints, in each 5 × 5 cell, a set number of columns from the left and rows up from the bottom and leaves the rest transparent, its gutter repeating the nearest content texel: a result judge graph's chips are runs of its cells.
- **Atlas pages** packed with `etagere`, sized within the limits the device was created with (`Device::limits()`; device creation requests wgpu's default limits, crates/render/src/gpu.rs:95), not the adapter's, so a skin that fits the adapter but not the device is refused at load rather than failing at texture creation. The pages are one texture array: every layer has one side, a multiple of 128, from 256 (or the largest frame rounded up) to the device's side rounded down to a multiple of 128 and capped at 8 192 (a cell larger than that page holds less its gutters is dropped at import, or refuses the skin when critical, D19), chosen as the fewest texels across all layers, reserved ones included, ties to fewer pages, among the sides whose pages fit the device's layers and 64. The skin's pages start after the reserved layers (the bitmap font's layer 0), and a frame's uv is its content over the side, in `f32` within 1/2048 of a texel of exact at the largest side, well inside the 8-texel gutter; the built-in atlas on layer 0 is read by integer texel, exact on any side.
- **Mipmaps** generated on the CPU, 4 levels, with premultiplied alpha (`(c·a + 127) / 255`) and a 2×2 box filter in stored (non-sRGB) space. Every frame of a grid and every glyph of a digit strip is packed on its own with an 8-texel gutter, its allocation aligned to 8, so every texel of every level covers one allocation and lower levels never mix neighbouring frames. The gutter and any rounding in the allocation repeat the frame's nearest edge texel, as clamp-to-edge would. Two images cutting the same rectangle share one packed frame. The tests use contrasting adjacent frames and colored transparent edges and sample them at every level.
- **Per-image filter:** linear by default, nearest for pixel art: nearest within a level and between levels, so shrinking still uses the box-filtered levels and never blurs two levels together. A generated grid (a result graph's chips) is read at its nearest level-0 texel whatever the minification (`Filter::NearestBase`), as the source's unmipmapped nearest texture is, so its gaps stay crisp; only such sprites take that read (design.md "Rendering and Skins", atlas array).
- **Text** (S8; uploaded in S10-08a) rasterized at each attempt's preparation, and at each loading text preparation for the loading presentation's slots, at its final pixel size by `render::OutlineText`, the bundle's producer: `cosmic-text` lays the line out, swash extracts each glyph's outline unhinted, and zeno renders white premultiplied coverage from the checked outline. Fonts are passed as bytes (`fontdb::Source::Binary` over the snapshot read at installation), never memory-mapped or found through fontconfig; `skin_model::FontReader` reads the files the texts use against one budget of 64 MiB actually read, each path resolved again, each opened handle a regular file of at most 32 MiB and of the budget left, at most its length plus one byte read, the magic checked on the bytes read. The default font is the menu font, read once per process. A skin font's system holds its face and the default face with empty fallback lists, so a glyph the skin's font lacks comes from the default font (D16), and one neither has is the skin face's `.notdef`. Every face is materialised and each system lays out and rasterizes a probe line at installation; a face that fails refuses the installation. The play screen still never calls into `render::text`.
  - **Collections.** Only a validated face 0 is registered (as the target's FreeType generator uses face 0), with `push_face_info` under a synthetic family. No bulk loader that enumerates or reserves by a collection's declared face count is called (fontdb 0.23's `load_font_data` reserves `count` IDs before validating); a `ttcf` must declare at least one face whose offsets fit the data, so a 12-byte header with count `0xffffffff` is refused, by a test. A face with a `VARC` table, without `glyf`, `CFF ` or `CFF2` outlines, or with zero units per em is refused.
  - **Cap height,** the box's top: a positive `sCapHeight`, else the positive top above the baseline of the same face's own `H` glyph (no glyph fallback), else a positive ascender; zero and absent values both fall through. Tests cover a zero and an absent `sCapHeight`.
  - **Text layer.** The lines are packed into one layer after the skin's pages, reserved in the atlas's layer count and texel budget when the skin has rasterized text (`Sheet::atlas_reserving`), its side at least 2 048, created zeroed, with `etagere`, an 8-texel zero gutter, alignment 8 and the pages' box-filtered mips. A line is at most `min(4096, side) − 2·GUTTER` pixels wide and `min(1024, side − 2·GUTTER)` tall; a wider one is set again at the em that fits, at most twice. A line is keyed by font, string and em; the cache of each set of resources keeps at most 320 lines and 320 aliases (a request's em to the line it was placed under), and never writes to or frees a line the installed or staged attempt, the installed loading lines, the staged loading text, or the current round holds. When the layer is full, unheld lines are evicted, least recently used first; past that every request is set again at half the em cap, at most 8 rounds. New lines are written only after placement succeeds, inside error scopes, and drawn only after the GPU has finished their upload; a failure releases them after that wait.
  - **Raster bounds.** Each image's pixel dimensions and temporary raster bytes are bounded before anything is allocated, since a font's units per em and glyph bounds can make one glyph thousands of em tall: a line taller than its bounds, or still too wide, is refused (`TextNote::TooLarge`), and one laying out more than 1 024 glyphs for rendering (`TextNote::TooComplex`). Every outline point, pen position and metric is checked finite and inside the line's pixels plus a 64-pixel margin, and the translated points and the mask's size again right before the rasterizer, which converts them to fixed point unchecked. A request that gets no image (refused, or no room after the rounds, `TextNote::LayerFull`) draws nothing for its slot, with one report line (`Renderer::text_report`), not an error for the whole `prepare`.
- **@2x variants** (S4): a file may name an @2x file at exactly twice its size. `Skin::variant` picks them when some image the skin can draw is magnified on both axes: per element the canvas scale times its region's scale (hidden regions skipped), a `cover` or `stretch` fill's own scale on `screen`, and for lane-bound images and the lanes `min(lane width, 100 %)` on x, so a lane width alone never picks @2x; a bar group (R6) counts with the lanes, under their region and visibility, by its own binding, so an unbound group takes no lane width, and a group whose visibility folds to false does not count; exact rationals, one variant for the sheet. The atlas then doubles the frames' rectangles; a file without one keeps its 1x frames, and track geometry does not change. A scale change in the adjust mode reaches the assets at the next `prepare`. The authored syntax for @2x, and nine-slice, come with the authored format (S12).
- **Elements and quads** (S4): an element's source is an image of the sheet, or one chosen from a table (R6), each entry fitting its own native size under stretch 8 and 9; `Skin::build` takes the uv table of an atlas of the skin's sheet (a table of another sheet draws nothing) and writes the frame's uv and layer and the image's filter into the `Quad`. Neither breaks a run: blend and clip alone do (R1).
- **Bounds:** file, image and frame counts, name length, file size, header position, image dimensions and texels per file and per variant, atlas texels, and element, keyframe and timer counts are capped before anything that grows with them is allocated, and one file's decoding is bounded (design.md "Memory Budget"). Every referenced file resolves inside the skin's folder and must be a regular file, checked before opening and on the handle (design.md threat model "Skins", with its precondition and the known limitations). The caps (`skin_model`'s `MAX_*` constants), enforced cumulatively by each importer and the custom format's loader:
  - **Files and images:** 4 096 files, each attempt counted before the file is examined and never refunded; 16 384 images; 1 024 frames an image and 65 536 a skin; a name at most 1 024 bytes, a file at most 64 MiB. Admission checks a file's name, confinement, type, size and the variant's encoded total before its header is read, and caches a failure. The header lies within the first 1 MiB read; a side is at most 16 384, a file decoded whole at most 12 Mi texels and a non-interlaced, non-animated PNG decoded in bands at most 64 Mi, and a variant at most 192 Mi texels and 512 MiB encoded. Each atlas build charges the bytes it reads again.
  - **Fonts:** at most 16 font files, each a regular file of at most 32 MiB and 64 MiB in all. A bitmap `.fnt` font takes one of those 16 slots before anything is read and is at most 4 MiB, its length charged to the 64 MiB before it is read; each glyph and kerning pair is counted before it is kept (16 384 of each a font, 65 536 a sheet), values are at most 16 384, and a font has at most 16 pages, each admitted as an image file with its attempt and charges, all pages or none, and decoded once at admission so a corrupt page fails its font rather than the atlas; a page past what an atlas page holds fails its font the same way (D19). A font chain has at most 15 fallbacks, and at most 64 chains are kept.
  - **Text:** a text literal is at most 256 bytes, and a chart string a skin shows is cut to 256.

## 3. The beatoraja importer (`skin-beatoraja`)

- **What it reads:** JSON skins and Lua skins (`.luaskin`), whose returned table has the JSON shape. Load-time branching, includes and options are resolved at import; the result is a runtime model. Includes are bounded in depth and in total input bytes and expanded values, and a cycle is refused, header processing included; fixtures cover a self-include and a two-file cycle.
- **The JSON reader** (S6a, our own) follows the source's lenient reader, libGDX 1.9.9's `JsonReader.rl`: `//` and `/* */` comments, trailing commas, and a line break in place of a missing comma (not inside a block comment; two values on one line are refused). Unquoted names end at `:`, a line break or a comment, unquoted values at `,`, `}`, `]`, a line break or a comment, both trimmed; in an object only `}` closes, so a name may start with `]`, and no value starts with `}`. Only unquoted tokens are numbers, booleans or `null`. Members keep their order, duplicates and spelling. Intentional policies: a UTF-8 BOM is skipped; invalid UTF-8, an invalid escape and a lone surrogate (`\u` takes four hex digits) are syntax errors found by the first scan; an integer read truncates the token's exact decimal value toward zero, never a rounded float, and must fit `i32`. Its caps (`skin_beatoraja::limits`), each charged before the work it bounds: a file at most 16 MiB by its metadata before it is read, and 32 MiB read across one import, an include read again counted again; nesting 64 deep across files, 2 Mi nodes, 64 KiB a string, 32 MiB of decoded strings, 2 Mi expanded values and 65 536 digit offset rows resolved, each counted before it is read; includes 16 deep and 1 024 parses; and 4 096 folder entries enumerated an import, each counted before it is examined.
- **Resolution** (S6a) expands `include` and `if`/`value`/`values` for one set of enabled options only where the source registers wrapper serializers (`JsonSkinSerializer.java` at `0.8.9`): in the header, `property`, `filepath`, `offset` and their arrays; option items and `category` are read literally, and nothing they name is read; the object and destination fields are resolved by the mapping. In an array field a conditional item adds its `value`, then its `values`; an `include` item splices the included array; a record held in an array is a first-match choice. A condition is `n` (enabled), `-n` (not enabled), or an array whose items all hold, an inner array holding when any number does. An unselected branch's include is never read. Wrapper keys match ignoring ASCII case, first wins; ordinary fields match exactly, last wins. An include resolves from its including file's folder, an image or font name (font fallbacks included) from the entry file's: the name's own form is checked as an include's, then it is joined to that folder and `..` is resolved lexically, never above the skin folder, and the model admits the normalised name and checks its canonical path. Spellings differing only in separators, `.` and `..` are admitted once, and a font's pages resolve from its normalised folder; case, 8.3 and link aliases are separate names, each held to the skin folder by the canonical check. Intentional policies: a missing include is an error, an include name with `*` is refused, a non-object record reads as the defaults, and a condition of −2147483648, which no option number negates, is a range error.
- **The header** (S6a): `read_header` resolves with no option enabled, as the source's header pass does (`JSONSkinLoader.java:91-113`), and keeps the parsed definition for `resolve` with the configured options. `type` names one supported screen kind (`skin_model::ScreenKind`), which the header records: 0 (7-key play), 5 (song selection), 6 (song decision) or 7 (single-chart result); any other type, or none, is refused. Both import entries take the slot, and a slot holds only its own kind: a header of another kind is refused before resolution, any asset or the configured Lua pass; a configured Lua pass that returns another type is refused at that type, before its callbacks are admitted. No skin is read as another screen. The size is 1280×720 (also when omitted), 1600×900, 1920×1080, 2560×1440 or 3840×2160, any other refused. It gives the name, author, options (items with numbers, the default), file choices and offsets (id and axis flags), with 1 024 options, 16 384 items, 1 024 file choices and 1 024 offsets, each checked before its record. The offsets are those the definition declares; the source's four built-in play offsets need a home before S11's adjust mode. A file choice is a folder and a pattern with `*` in its last component only, optionally closed by a tag, `|tag|`: exactly two `|`, the closing one last, the tag not empty and without `/`, `\`, `*` or `|`. Its whole name, tag included, is checked as an include's, resolved from the entry file's folder, and is the choice's substitution key; the folder is listed with the pattern without its tag (`SkinConfigurationView.java:374-389`), which matches ignoring ASCII case, as the source's glob does on its target's case-insensitive file system (other letters do not fold). Any other `|`, and `?`, `[`, `]`, `{` and `}` in the pattern (inside a tag they are plain text), are refused before any folder is listed. Files and folders are both candidates, a missing folder gives none, candidates are sorted by name (by bytes), and each choice's listing, tagged choices over one folder each, counts against the 4 096 folder entries.
- **Mapping** (S6b-1) maps a play skin's images, image sets, numbers and text. `import` resolves the definition again with the configured options, also through the object and destination fields the source wraps (`JsonSkinSerializer.java:34-97`), and walks the destinations in source order (`JSONSkinLoader.java:316-339`):
  - **Policy.** A role comes from what an element shows on its screen, never from its id. Play's critical roles are below; selection's and result's are in "Screen profiles". An unsupported essential dependency refuses that screen's skin, and a refused non-play skin leaves its built-in screen, which does not change play's installation and admission (Q4-A). On play, EX score and max combo numbers (71, 101, 75, 105 or their names, through the effective property) are play-critical: their value, visibility and timing, and everything those depend on (property, ops, timers, track, offsets, stretch, blend, images and files), must be reproduced, or the skin is refused. On play, the failure presentation is play-critical: a destination that can still draw once the configured options are folded (resolution's branches, and its option-item ops; a numeric `draw` never folds) and whose timer resolves to `failed` (3, directly or through a chain of numeric custom timers) shows what the play presents when a survival gauge fails. Its content, visibility, timing and everything they depend on must be reproduced under these policies, or the skin is refused. Its role is decided from its timer and conditions alone, before any of its fields is mapped, so no unsupported object, stretch, property, condition, font fallback or asset drops it; a part with a role of its own (an EX score or max combo number, the covers, the lanes, the judge, the gauge) keeps it. A destination the source's lookup provably makes no object for is ignored with a located note before any role applies, as the source skips it (`JSONSkinLoader.java:326-338`); a negative id is a built-in image, resolved before any object (`JSONSkinLoader.java:316-339`), and drawn as "Images" says, outside this rule. Such a destination is one whose id names no object (`UndefinedObject`), or whose id only one table names, an `image` (any `len` or offsets), `slider`, `graph`, `liftCover` or `hiddenCover` record whose `src` names no `source` record, absent or an id no record has, the last record of an id deciding (`UndefinedSource`, `JSONSkinLoader.java:312-314, 484-492`, `JsonSkinObjectLoader.java:42-72, 394-455`, `JsonPlaySkinObjectLoader.java:183-214`). It reads no file and charges no image. So SUDDEN+, LIFT and HIDDEN+ paints and failure presentation with an undefined source are ignored too: the source paints nothing there, and coverage does not depend on the paint. An id several object tables name keeps its existing outcome and reason, which do not reproduce every fall-through of the source's lookup: one a judge also names refuses with `UnsupportedField{id}` ("Timers"), and a failure-timed graph sharing a `bga` id with `Unsupported{Graph}`. A defined source with no `path`, or naming a missing or unreadable file, is a missing asset, reproduced or refused as above. Every other consumer of an image that reaches the source lookup keeps its outcome with `UndefinedSource` as its reason: a judge grade or count whose source is undefined refuses (`Critical{Judge}`), though the source leaves the grade blank (index 6 falling back to PERFECT's) or drops the combo (`SkinJudge.java:107-123`), since that would remove part of the judge; numbers, gauge nodes, note parts and bar groups refuse when critical, where the source fails the whole skin (`JsonSkinObjectLoader.java:99-384, 509-556, 601-621`; a gauge whose nodes are all undefined, which the source makes an empty gauge, refuses too). A chain the model has no timer for refuses as unsupported; a script timer is not followed (a documented gap), and an image's own cycle timer never decides the role. Everything else is decorative: what cannot be reproduced is dropped with a located note, and what the source does not draw either is noted as ignored. An ignored note aims to mean the field has no effect in the source; the exceptions are documented deviations (D15 clips on the lanes and the judge, D17 image actions). An object with a `mouseRect` is drawn by the source only while the mouse is inside it (`skin/SkinObject.java:603-607`): on selection it keeps that rect as a hover condition (design.md "Input", pointer input), and on the other screens it is dropped, or refuses when critical. Geometry never refuses; aggregate limits and geometry outside the model always do. On play, `bga`, graphs, sliders and the other unsupported objects are dropped, or refuse the skin when they are failure presentation.
  - **Geometry.** One rule for every element, on exact `i128` rationals: a key is scaled by the output size over the skin's into reference pixels, offset, stretched, reflected (`top′ = 1080 − (y + h)`, `bottom′ = 1080 − y`), and each final edge rounded half up; the pivot comes from the stretched rect (`center` 0–9, anything else bottom-left) and the angle is negated. Overflow, or a value outside the model's limits, is `ImportError::Range`. Tests cover an asymmetric rectangle with a non-central pivot and a rotation, and an odd aspect whose stretched height is not truncated before centring. A cover's stretch-5 crop follows the same rule in texels: it keeps `destination height × frame width / destination width` texels about the frame's middle, each edge rounded half up; the source computes it in `f32` and truncates both the first row and the count, so the first row may differ by one and the count and last row by more. With a 1600×900 skin, a 15×4 frame under a 430×86 destination (516×103.2 reference pixels) keeps rows 1 to 3, where the source keeps 0 to 2; a 25×128 frame under 430×258 keeps rows 57 to 71 (15), where the source's `f32` quotient 14.99… keeps 56 to 69 (14).
  - **Tracks.** The first key defaults position and size to 0 and colour to 255, later keys inherit omitted fields in source order, colour is clamped to 0–255, so an imported skin looks as its author saw it in the source rather than being refused, and a destination with a clamped channel gets one `Fallback` note (`ColourClamped`) at its first such key, and keys are sorted stably. No key is added, moved or changed: of keys sharing a time the first and the last are kept, the keys between never drawing, and one when those two are equal; a negative first key is unsupported. The end follows the source's sampler (`SkinObject.java:361-376`): loop −1 hides; keys all at 0, or a loop at the last key, hold, keys all at 0 only when the first and last are equal, since after 0 the source shows the first; a loop inside the track loops, and one below −1 wraps into negative times down to 4 h before 0 (beyond it `Limits`); a loop past the last key holds only when the first and last keys are equal; a loop before the first key never draws keys all at one time, and for others wraps into a gap each period where nothing draws, as before the first key (the model's sampler wraps the time, then hides a time before the first key). `acc` 1, 2 and 3 are in, out and step, anything else linear.
  - **Stretch.** Modes 1, 2, 4 and 6 are baked per key against the frame's native size, on exact rationals: 4 and 6 always (affine in the rect), 1 and 2 when every key falls in the same branch; −1, 0 and unknown modes are the identity. On an image (not a group), the trimmed modes 3, 5 and 7 (`skin/StretchType.java:47-80, 131-153`) are decided per key on the same exact rationals: 3 takes the larger scale, 5 the width's, 7 the height's, and a scaled frame that overflows the destination's other axis is cut about its middle there while the destination stays, else the destination shrinks and recentres to it (an exact meeting fits). When every key fits, each key is baked so, whatever its aspect; when every key crops one axis keeping the same texels (cropping keys of one aspect, whatever their size), the sheet is cut once about its middle, each edge rounded half up where the source truncates in `f32` (D1), and the keys stay. The importer crops only a sheet of one cell along the cropped axis, whose frames the model's grid splits each cropped alike; a crop across several cells, which the model could represent by cutting each, is an implementation restriction. Keys mixing the branches, cropping keys changing aspect, trimmed image groups and image sets, and mode 10 (native-size crop, no case) are unsupported; a crop past the file's right or bottom edge pads from it as any cut, and one starting past it draws the edge line. Modes 8 (no expanding) and 9 (no resize) size the frame in output pixels after the destination is scaled to the window (`skin/StretchType.java:85-98`, `skin/SkinObject.java:626-629`), so they are not baked: the element keeps its frame size (`Place::native`) and the model fits each quad at placement, at the canvas's scale, then turns it about its pivot in the fitted rect. A number or combo applies 8 and 9 to each glyph in its own cell (`skin/SkinNumber.java:191`), and accepts any other stretch only when it is the identity on its cells and, for a mode other than the identity, every digit offset row it keeps changes the size in the frame's aspect (`dw·ih = dh·iw`), so each moved cell keeps it too; a judge grade under 8 or 9 keeps the destination its combo attaches to (`play/SkinJudge.java:124`).
  - **Offsets.** `offsets` then `offset`, ids 1–199, deduplicated, applied unscaled in reference pixels from `Config` (`MainState.java:115-133`), only for ids the header declares; the axis flags belong to the settings screen and do not mask the values. Alpha folds only into a fixed colour with deltas of one sign. Offset 1 makes an image or number element turn with the scratch's turntable angle, added to its own angle and negated as source angles are; a text element accepts it and stays unrotated, as the source draws text; the runtime offset 2 is unsupported and offset 10 waits for S11. Offsets 3, 4 and 5 make an image, number, gauge, text, notes graph, BPM graph, timing or hit-error visualizer, or judgment grade follow LIFT, SUDDEN+ and HIDDEN+ (R6, "Followers"), a combo takes 3 and 4 without motion of its own, and LIFT, SUDDEN+ and HIDDEN+ consume their own (3 on LIFT, 3 and 5 on HIDDEN+, an extra 4 on either following SUDDEN+); on any of these a configured y is ignored, since the play sets it (`play/LaneRenderer.java:332-346`), and a configured x, width, height, angle or alpha is refused by the element's role, as are 3, 4 and 5 anywhere else. Each retained follower is noted once as D9.
  - **Ops and conditions.** An `op` of a Lua skin's own table that is not a table (a number, string, boolean or function) is no condition, as the source's Lua loader reads any array field it is not given a table for (`skin/lua/LuaSkinLoader.java:171-186`), noted ignored at the value, once however often it is read. Any other `op` the importer reads that is not an array fails the skin: a JSON value, in a JSON skin or in a JSON file a Lua skin includes, as the JSON loader fails it (`skin/json/JSONSkinLoader.java:109`), and a Lua table with string keys, whose entries the source reads as the conditions. Op 0 is ignored and ops are deduplicated. A built-in id wins over a declared option: 230–240 (gauge ranges, compared on the exact ‰ value), 241 (perfect) and the gauge types 42 (`gauge_groove`: assist easy, easy or normal), 43 (`gauge_hard`: hard, ex hard or hazard) and 1046 (`gauge_ex`: assist easy, easy, ex hard or hazard), each `gauge.valid && …` over `gauge.kind` (R3) with a D4 note, and the cover conditions 270 (`lanecover1_changing`: `cover.adjusting`), 271 (`lanecover1_on`: `cover.enabled`), 272 (`lift1_on`: `lift.enabled`) and 273 (`hidden1_on`: `hidden.enabled`), and the judgment-count conditions 2242 to 2245 (`judge_great_exist` to `judge_poor_exist`: the count above 0, POOR's the scored POORs' `count.early.poor + count.late.poor`, without empty POORs; `BooleanPropertyFactory.java:554-561`), and the BPM-change conditions 176 (`chart_no_bpmchange`: `bpm.change_valid && !bpm.change`) and 177 (`chart_bpmchange`: `bpm.change`) with a D4 note, the source's comparison of the chart's truncated extremes (`BooleanPropertyFactory.java:439-440`, `song/SongData.java:176-177`), evaluated once per chart there (`skin/Skin.java:199-212`) and constant over a play here, and the loading conditions 80 (`now_loading`: `on(loading)`) and 81 (`loaded`: `!on(loading)`), the source's preload state and its complement (`BooleanPropertyFactory.java:426-429`), which entering READY flips and a load's progress reaching 100 does not (`play/BMSPlayer.java:514`), are supported, a negative id negates the whole condition, validity included; when a destination's complete generated visibility (its ops, its `draw` and its availability guards) exceeds an expression budget, the importer refuses the import at that destination's location; 1242 (`judge_1p_early`: `judge.recent_imperfect && judge.recent_ms > 0`) and 1243 (`judge_1p_late`: `… < 0`), the recent timing's side (`BooleanPropertyFactory.java:234-247`, R3), are supported; 32 (`autoplay_off`: `!autoplay`) and 33 (`autoplay_on`: `autoplay`), the attempt's play mode (`BooleanPropertyFactory.java:413-416`), are supported; the chart's metadata conditions 150 to 155 and 170 read the `chart.*` fields (R3) and are supported; 290 (`mode_course`) and 1008 (`table_song`) fold as scene constants, false, so −290 and −1008 are true, there being no courses (`BooleanPropertyFactory.java:523-524`) and no difficulty tables, outside one of which the source's table name is empty (`BooleanPropertyFactory.java:351-352`, `PlayerResource.java:128-129`); other built-ins are unsupported (the rank conditions 200–347 among them, whose raw-ratio thresholds rounded basis points do not reproduce); declared option items fold at import; an undefined op hides the element for either sign. Two views of the options, as the source keeps them: `if` in the definition sees the union of every option's selected item (`JSONSkinLoader.java:254-260`), and ops see each item's state with a later option's entry overriding an earlier one's for an item both list (`JSONSkinLoader.java:291-297`). 241 is false and −241 true when the skin has no judge region, as below. Visibility is the conjunction, compiled within the budget.
  - **Timers.** Ids 0–2999 are the source's namespace. `ready`, `play`, `failed`, the last note, full combo, gauge max, the rhythm clock 140 (`rhythm`), the judgment timers, the lane timers and the rank timers 348–350 are supported; 46 and 446 start on every judgment, and condition 241 reads the latest judgment, whether or not the skin draws a judge: the judgment state gives them, at every entry point (element and cycle timers, custom aliases), where the source sets them only for the judge region its judge widget makes (`JsonPlaySkinObjectLoader.java:283-287`). A destination whose id names a judge and, earlier in the lookup order, an image, text, slider, graph or LIFT refuses: the source makes the judge only when that object comes out null and the lookup falls through, an image's or LIFT's file or a slider's or graph's texture failing to load or a text's font id naming no font (`JsonSkinObjectLoader.java:71, 387-391, 396-416, 420-453, 628`, `JsonPlaySkinObjectLoader.java:31, 200-213`), a load-order rule not reproduced. The other kinds before the judge always construct, throw, or (HIDDEN+) end the lookup even when null (`JsonPlaySkinObjectLoader.java:183-196`), so they keep the id; a rank becomes a `when`, `notes > 0 && 27·ex ≥ 2·i·notes`, exact against the source's `f32` comparison within the parser's object cap. 0 is never set, so an element on it is never drawn and a cycle on it stays on frame 0; nor are 108, 109, 128 and 129, keys 8 and 9's key-on and key-off, which this 7-key mode does not have and which never alias another lane, while 100–107 and 120–127 are the scratch's and keys 1–7's `press` and `release`. 2, the fade-out, starts at a full play's fade-out only, never in Closing or a practice round (D30). Every other id is unsupported. A custom timer (above 2999) is supported only as a direct alias of a supported id, the last declaration winning; an alias of a never-set id is never set. A chain of numeric custom timers is followed only to classify the failure presentation ("Policy"), each id once, a cycle never reaching `failed`. The top-level `judgetimer` (default 1, `JsonSkin.java:20`) is the bomb grade: a lane's bomb starts on a judgment whose index, PERFECT 0 to empty POOR 5, is at most it (`play/JudgeManager.java:673`), so a negative value gives `None` and one above 5 every grade, exactly on its whole domain. An element without a timer runs on `screen`, which in a play runs on from the play state's screen origin (D3). A critical one is admitted when its placement (rect, angle and clip) and its colour other than alpha are the same at every key: only its presentation phase may then depend on when the state started, through its alpha, a first key after 0, a held, looping or ending window and an image cycle without a timer, under any blend the importer supports, and each such destination is noted. An image cycle without a timer also runs on `screen` under a timed destination, so a critical destination keeping one is noted too. One that moves, resizes, turns or changes another colour channel is admitted only if it settles, playing its authored keys (D3); one whose keys need an alpha offset while its colour interpolates, a stretch changing branch or stretch 5 refuses. It settles when its end is a hold, which the source's sampler gives by `loop` at the last key's time, or past it when the first and last keys show the same values (`skin/SkinObject.java:360-371, 549-574`). A `loop` before the last key loops and a `loop` of -1 hides, so these still refuse with `Timer: Untimed`; a `loop` past the last key with different first and last keys shows the first key after the last and refuses as that track does anywhere. This admission is provisional until a visual comparison of a real skin's start-dependent phase against the source player's timing, including a critical part whose visibility ends (a Hide end) after READY.
  - **Properties.** A number's effective property is its `value`, resolved as the source's serializer does, else its `ref`; a text's is its `value` or `ref`, else `constantText`. An element with nothing to show is ignored, as the source draws nothing.
  - **Images.** The source's lookup of an image's `src` returns nothing for an absent `src`, an id no `source` record has, and a record whose file does not exist alike (`JSONSkinLoader.java:484-520`, `JsonSkinObjectLoader.java:561-582`); what follows depends on the object: an `image` of any `len` is no object, and the play loader's own kinds are tried after it (`JsonSkinObjectLoader.java:42-72`, `JsonPlaySkinObjectLoader.java:31-34`); an `imageset` entry is empty (74-97); a `slider` or `graph` is no object and the lookup goes on (394-455); `hiddenCover` is no object and ends the lookup, `liftCover` goes on (`JsonPlaySkinObjectLoader.java:183-214`); a judge grade draws nothing and a judge count drops the combo (`SkinJudge.java:107-123`); a `value`, `floatvalue`, gauge node with others defined, note part or bar line fails the whole skin (`JsonSkinObjectLoader.java:99-384, 509-556, 601-621`, `JsonPlaySkinObjectLoader.java:46-170, 584-599`). The importer keeps an undefined source apart from a missing file, as "Policy" decides. An omitted `w` or `h` is 0 (an empty cell, unsupported), −1 the texture size, a division of 0 or less 1; frames are row-major, and a grid of more frames than the skin holds is the frame limit; one image per object, filter and cut. Selection (S6c-04) becomes an image choice (R6), decorative, following the covers as an image does, and selecting as the source does (`skin/SkinImage.java:95-139`). An `image` with `len` above 1 is a group: its `N` frames, row-major, cut into `len` runs of `q = ⌊N / len⌋`, the remainder never shown, each run cycling over its `q` frames on the image's timer, so a run of one frame ignores its cycle and timer, and never drawn (an ignored note) when `q` is 0; a `len` of 1 or less is an ordinary image whatever its `ref`. A group selects by its `ref`, and an `imageset` by its `value` when that resolves as a value property (the number's resolution, unsupported scripts included; a value that may be unknown hides the set while it is), else by its `ref` (`JsonSkinObjectLoader.java:49-64, 74-91`); a value the model cannot show drops the set without falling back. A `ref` here is read in the image-index namespace, never as a number's or an op's id: 40 is the shown gauge's type, 0 to 5 over the six looks while `gauge.valid`, 42 the lane option, always 0 (OFF) as tapestry applies none (`IntegerPropertyFactory.java:1136-1143`), 330, 331 and 332 the cover switches (`cover.enabled`, `lift.enabled`, `hidden.enabled`, 1 when on), and 500 to 507 the 1P lanes' visual states (`judge.lane.0` the scratch to `judge.lane.7` key 7, R3, the source's skin offsets for 7 keys, `play/LaneProperty.java:50`), with no note; an id the source's factory does not know, 0 and an absent `ref` included, selects entry 0, as its null property does; any other id it knows (`skin/property/IntegerPropertyFactory.java:920-961, 1088-1383`), 508, 509 and the other players' 510–519 among them, drops the element with an image-index property note naming it. An image set's entries are its `images` in order, repeats kept and each image's own cycle and timer kept, its `len`, `ref` and action unused: a null item, an undefined id, an image whose source is undefined (`UndefinedSource`), a movie and a missing or unreadable file are empty entries, all but the first noted at their item; a source only this importer refuses (its folder confinement, its formats, or its size, dimension and header allocation caps) drops the set, noted with `OverCap` naming the per-file cap that refused it, else with `MissingAsset`; a set with no entries, or with only empty ones, is never drawn; `images` other than an array of names and nulls is a type error. Each entry's stretch is baked against its own cell: the first present entry's keys are the element's track, an entry whose keys differ keeps its own, charged to the key cap before it is built, and stretch 8 and 9 keep each entry's native size. An element's entries are charged to the choice entry cap (`ImportLimit::ChoiceRefs`) before its table is gathered or its images admitted, and its selector, compiled once per import, is charged for each destination that keeps it. A source rectangle with a negative corner or size (a mirrored cut), or whose frame coordinates overflow the source's `int` sums, is dropped with `SourceRect`, or refuses when critical. The source cuts any other as given (`JsonSkinObjectLoader.java:601-621`), from a texture of its own file that clamps to its edge (libGDX 1.9.9 `GLTexture` defaults to `ClampToEdge`, and the source never changes it). A frame lying wholly at or past the file's right or bottom edge, on an axis where the import admits one frame, is that edge's last texel column or row: the import cuts that line in its place and keeps the source's cell as the native size, the stretch base and a stretch-5 crop's frame, and a stretch-5 crop of a frame taller than one row cut to its last row is dropped with `SourceRect`, or refuses when critical. A cut starting inside the file and running past that edge, one frame or a grid of them, is drawn as the source draws it, with no note: the model image keeps the cut's whole rectangle, and the atlas build repeats the file's nearest edge texel over each cell's part past the file (design.md "Rendering and Skins"), so a grid's cell wholly past the edge is that edge's texel line too. A stretch-5 crop of such a cut (a cover's) that starts at or past the bottom edge is that edge's last row, cut in its place with the crop's rows kept as its cell, as the source samples it. Several admitted frames starting at or past the edge on an axis are dropped with `SourceRect`, or refuse when critical or essential. Only PNG, JPEG and BMP files decode (design.md); a file in another format the source reads (WebP, CIM and others, `PixmapResourcePool.java:224`) is a decorative element's `MissingAsset` and a critical element's `ImportError::Asset`. Text consumes neither the angle nor the blend, which it ignores, and a box the model cannot draw is the model's refusal. A destination whose id is a negative number draws the source's built-in image of its negation (`JSONSkinLoader.java:316-339`, `skin/SkinSourceReference.java:25-43`): 110 and 111 are opaque black and opaque white 1×1 regions, imported as generated 1×1 images of that colour, one per colour and filter, reading no file and charged as any image; each destination then draws it as an image of a 1×1 cell, stretched to its destination with its track, colour, alpha, blend, conditions and timer, following the covers, and painting SUDDEN+ when it lists offset 4 and may reach the lanes, where it is play-critical. On a play or result skin the chart's stage file and back image (100 and 101) are chart images (S10-12, design.md "Rendering and Skins"): the element keeps its track unbaked, its colour, blend, filter and conditions, and its stretch for placement against the image's own size, a trimmed one (3, 5, 7, 10) dropped with a `Stretch` note and one that would paint SUDDEN+ dropped as unsupported; a failure-presentation one still refuses the skin, and they charge no image. The banner (102), every other negative id, and 100 and 101 on selection and the decision screen are dropped as unsupported system objects, until S10-12's later parts.
  - **File choice.** The configured candidate, matched by its entry name exactly, case included, else the default by full name or stem ignoring ASCII case (the first sorted when several match, reported as ambiguous), else the first sorted candidate; the choice is reported. Paths are substituted by prefix of the choice's whole name, tag included (`JSONSkinLoader.java:231-235`, `SkinLoader.java:95-125`): of the choices whose names prefix a source path, the first declared gives the path's text up to its last `*`, the candidate, then the path's text after the name, and the result is read through the same name and containment checks as any other path; a source glob no file choice covers is unsupported.
  - **`SheetBuilder`** (`skin-model`) admits files one at a time: each attempt counts against the file cap and is never refunded, metadata and the variant's encoded total are checked before the header is read, a high variant must be twice the low one, and `finish` builds the same `Sheet` as `Sheet::new`. The importer charges images and frames before admitting, and caches a failing file so other references read nothing.
  - **Budget.** One budget per import, each charge before the work it guards: elements, then keys, then the condition's source (1 024 B), its `when`s and its ops per referencing element, then images and frames, then widget quads and texts; the lanes' bar line groups are counted before any of their records is read (`ImportLimit::BarGroups` past `MAX_BAR_GROUPS`), each group's keys before its destination is read, and a dropped group keeps its charges; the digit offsets a number or combo keeps, after its quads and before they are stored (`ImportLimit::DigitOffsets` past `MAX_DIGIT_OFFSETS`), each copy counted and a strip dropped after its charge keeping it spent; notes past 4 096 are counted only. The final counters are the report's `usage`.
  - **Deviations**, documented here and reported as follows: D1, D2, D5, D14, D16, D18, D21, D23, D24, D25, D26, D27, D28, D29 and D30 with no note; D3 with a deviation note on a critical destination admitted with a start-dependent presentation phase, settling moves included, one per destination, and no note elsewhere; D4 with a deviation note on a number that shows 407, the section's note count, a score rate or a play readout, on a retained type-6 slider (the progress) and a retained graph of type 101 or 102, and on a retained element or judgment part whose conditions read the gauge type or the BPM-change fact or that selects its image by the gauge's type, a cover switch, the hi-speed fix, a deviating value or the EX score, one per destination, the gauge ranges and the cover switches with none; D6, D9, D10, D12 and D13 with a deviation note where they apply; D8 in the report's file-choice records; D15 and D17 with an ignored note (D17 for an image's `act`); D19 with a dropped note, or a refusal when critical. D1 geometry (above), with angles interpolated in fractional degrees where the source truncates to whole ones (`skin/SkinObject.java:537`), the covers' and followers' moving edges kept in `f64` with their fractions until drawn where the source moves them by `f32` offsets (`play/LaneRenderer.java:324-343`), the covers' trim lines converted exactly and rounded half up where the source scales them in `f32` (`skin/json/JsonPlaySkinObjectLoader.java:188, 205`), no +0.01 px draw nudge (`skin/Skin.java:616`), and each edge of a clip's scissor placed in `f64` and rounded half up to a target pixel on its own, where libGDX's `ScissorStack.calculateScissors` rounds the scissor's origin and size each on their own, so its far edge may differ by one pixel; D2 sampling in µs, with track acceptance at whole ms; D3 untimed elements on the `screen` timer, which runs from the play state's screen origin through READY, every round and every practice Start into the play, as the source's state clock does, except that: that origin is the play state's first loading lifetime's (re-stamped at the decision deadline's reveal), which precedes the lifetime's first presented loading frame (by 28-45 ms in the measured runs), where the source's clock starts after the skin's preparation (`MainController.java:275-281`); `screen` is not the state clock that `main_state.time()` reads, whose origin stays at the choice (design.md "The state clock"), where the source's two are one clock (`skin/Skin.java:317-319`, `skin/lua/MainStatePropertyLuaApiExporter.java:250-254`); a practice session's origin is its first Start's loading lifetime, even when that Start is cancelled or refused, where the source's is the practice state's creation, before its page (`play/BMSPlayer.java:442-445`), and a later Start's loading frames run `screen` from their own lifetime while its play runs on from the session's, where the source's Start enters no fresh PRELOAD and runs on (`play/BMSPlayer.java:550-594`); and a play with no loading lifetime (a retry the bank covers, a retry held at its opening, `tapestry play <chart>`) starts it the skin's loading minimum before its first READY opens (recording's opening, before a round's READY timer by 200 ms), where the source first shows a new loading phase lasting at least `loadstart + loadend` and over 1 s past the last START or SELECT press (`play/BMSPlayer.java:500-501, 715`, `result/MusicResult.java:231-243`, `MainController.java:341-343`); one clock for `screen` and the state clock from the play state's creation, running on through a practice session's page and Starts, loading frames included, would close these, and needs the state origin moved and loading frames on the session clock; D4 play-state displays on decorative elements (exact gauge ranges, number 407 below 0.1 %, the section's note count in practice; score rates from one exact floored basis-point value where the source rounds each portion's `float` product on its own (`ScoreDataProperty.java:94-99`), so an EX score of 39 over 100 targets shows 19.50 where the source shows 19.49; the rates' denominators from tapestry's own target accounting, one per long note and a practice section's own, where the source counts its passed notes (`play/JudgeManager.java:639-646`); 100.00 current and 0.00 whole shown before the first judgment and after a retry, where the source draws no rate digits until it has a score (`skin/property/IntegerPropertyFactory.java:176, 690`); the gauge-type conditions over the six looks, a custom gauge falling back to normal or hard, where the source classifies its nine gauge types, course types included (`skin/property/BooleanPropertyFactory.java:394-414, 693-704`), and every gauge-type condition false, negations true, while the shown gauge is not valid, and while loading without a descriptor nothing reading the gauge type draws, negations included; the BPM-change conditions exact where the source truncates a `double`, so they can differ near an integer BPM, and, like every condition on an unavailable field, not evaluated while loading, so any element under 176, 177 or their negations (critical SUDDEN+ paint included) draws nothing then, where the source keeps the sign the chosen chart gives; the play readouts (R3): the remaining time and the progress (slider 6) to the last resolved object, background sounds included, with no margin in normal play and autoplay alike, where the source adds `TIME_MARGIN` (5 000 ms) to the last note in a normal play and to the last object in autoplay, so a last object at 17 s ends at 22 s and the progress reaches 1 that much later (`play/BMSPlayer.java:66, 198-199`); whole seconds by ceiling, reaching 0 exactly at the end, where the source adds 1 000 ms before dividing (`skin/property/IntegerPropertyFactory.java:207-219`); the progress's share in parts per million rounded down, where the source divides in `float` (`skin/property/FloatPropertyFactory.java:128-136`); in practice the section's own real-time span, reset each round and excluding the lead-in, the progress running from 0 at the section's start, where the source adds a second and the margin to the section's end and runs `TIMER_PLAY`, so the progress, from the chart's start less a second (`play/BMSPlayer.java:586-587, 610`); the BPM range and current tempo from explicit provenance, hidden while unknown, where the source reads cached song extrema and the renderer's timeline (`IntegerPropertyFactory.java:161-166`, `song/SongData.java:175-177`, `play/LaneRenderer.java:300-318, 685-698`); the main BPM (92) from tapestry's deterministic MAIN, a tie going to the earliest row, CN and HCN ends counted and mines excluded, where the source takes the most-noted tempo in its `HashMap`'s order over its total-notes rule (`song/SongInformation.java:180-204`), so it can differ on a tie or a CN or HCN chart; and unavailable while loading, so what reads it draws nothing on the loading screen, where the source reads the chosen song's information; a Lua callback without a value while a read on any of its reached paths is unknown, the branches it does not take included (design.md "beatoraja Lua Skins"), where the source runs only the path it takes and converts what it returns, nil as 0 (`SkinLuaAccessor.java:640-651`). The BPM range is unknown when the chart's lowest or highest tempo, in floored hundredths, passes `READOUT_MAX` (about 21.47 million BPM, `player`'s `attempt_context::bpm_range`), whatever its ratio; so on such a chart a callback whose untaken branch reads that range has no value. Whether anything is painted is the destination's own draw condition: the source evaluates a number's value before its draw conditions and paints only when they hold (`SkinNumber.java:132, 147`, `SkinObject.java:586`). Among the real skins used for testing this concerns only Rm play7main's two adjusted-rate numbers (`play.lua:1800`, `:1815`). Under Classic, START and MIN their draw callbacks (`:3600`, `:3605`, `:3633`, `:3638`) return nil too, so the source paints nothing and nothing visible differs. Under MAIN the source paints the capped current/MAIN rate wherever those draw conditions hold, where tapestry paints nothing. Under MAX the taken branch reads the range itself, which is the play readouts' own unknown range; among the real skins used for testing this hides only Rm play7main's two adjusted-rate numbers (`play.lua:1800`, `:1815`) under every hi-speed fix but MAX, where the branch taken reads the range itself; the song progress graph (101) to the last resolved object as the progress slider, where the source's runs over the last note + 5 s (`skin/property/FloatPropertyFactory.java:128-137`, `play/BMSPlayer.java:66, 199`), so its fill is larger at every instant by (last + 5 s)/last and full 5 s earlier; the load's percent (165) and graph (102) from tapestry's load (`load.percent`: 90 % at the sounds' end, 100 at readiness, design.md "Loading state"), where the source averages its BGA's and audio's loaders while BGA is on (`skin/property/IntegerPropertyFactory.java:224-229`, `FloatPropertyFactory.java:285-290`), so the ramp differs; the chart's length (1163–1164) from its notes' ends and its background sounds (`ChartSummary::length`), where the source's `getLastTime` also counts hidden notes and BGA and layer events (`song/SongData.java:210`), so a chart ending on one of those reads shorter, and while loading from the library's summary of the chart's seed-0 branch, where the source reads the song data of the file it parsed; exact digits for the cover amounts and the hi-speed, so 251 ‰ shows 251 and 1.05 shows 105 (portions 1 and 05) where the source's `float` products can show 250 and 104 (`IntegerPropertyFactory.java:68-89, 586-602`); the duration from the scroll policy's exact ratio, never from rounded readouts or a multiplier, where the source recomputes it from BPM, hi-speed, speed, scroll and the enabled cover (`play/LaneRenderer.java:309-330`), with the green value taken from the rounded duration as the source does (`IntegerPropertyFactory.java:292-314`) but without its overflow, NaN or float-to-int behaviour; every readout from the current frame, its first build included, where the source updates BPM and duration while drawing the lanes, after the other objects are prepared (`skin/Skin.java:316-328`, `play/LaneRenderer.java:318, 330`); image selection by the gauge's type over the six looks, hidden while the shown gauge is not valid, where the source selects by its scoring gauge types, course types 6–8 included (`skin/property/IntegerPropertyFactory.java:1128-1135`, `play/GrooveGauge.java:20-28`); by the cover switches as the play has them now, where the source reads the configuration's flags while its lanes draw from a copy (`IntegerPropertyFactory.java:1261-1295`, `play/LaneRenderer.java:91`), so a switch toggled during play (`ControlInputProcessor.java:162-166`) changes the selection here and not there; by the hi-speed fix (image index 55 and `main_state.event_index(55)`) from the play's live presentation, where the source reads the stored play configuration (`IntegerPropertyFactory.java:1161-1186`), the same value while the speed policy cannot change during play; by a deviating value, whose last digit can select another image; and by EX score from 0 before the first judgment, where the source's `Integer.MIN_VALUE` hides the set (`IntegerPropertyFactory.java:606-615`)); D5 text always drawn smooth and alpha-blended, with the target rasteriser's metrics, and bitmap font pages sampled trilinearly through the atlas; D6 literals cut at 256 bytes, control characters removed, overflow 2 shrinks, outline, shadow and wrapping removed; D8 file choice deterministic where the source picks at random (`SkinLoader.java:95-130`); D9 the lane approximations accepted by the owner (below); D10 the gauge drawn as the model's segments (exact integer lit counts and border, the authored part count kept, the native motions' phase, random sequence and flicker curve); D12 a bitmap font's size `size·dstW/skinW` rounded half up to a whole reference pixel, reported when it is not one (the source does not round: `SkinTextBitmap.java:472`); D13 distance-field `.fnt` fonts (types 1 and 2) drawn with the default font at the source's size, its top kept, no file read; D14 the screen's own timing (`playstart`, `input`) not read, the player's lifecycle deciding READY and input; timer 2, the source's fade-out, never started in Closing: the source starts it only on an ending without a failure, a completed play or a quit before play, in autoplay or once every note is judged (`play/BMSPlayer.java:754, 947-967`), never on a failure or a quit with notes left (`:660, 968-970`), the endings Closing models, and starting it with Closing would draw a skin's fade-out over the failure presentation, which is play-critical ("Policy"); a quit once every note is judged but before the final deadline, which enters Closing (design.md "Play Lifecycle") where the source finishes and fades, is not faded either; `close` read as Closing's declared duration in µs, at least 1 s (design.md "Play Lifecycle", Closing), whether or not the skin defines a note, where the source sets it only while loading the note's destination, so a skin without one declares nothing there (`skin/json/JsonPlaySkinObjectLoader.java:37-40`), a known difference; and the closing sound is a player system sound, not read from the skin (S10-26); D15 a `clip` on the lanes or the judge ignored, where the source scissors the whole object (`skin/Skin.java:361`), and on any other element dropped, or refused when critical; D16 a TrueType font's `fallback` list not read, glyphs its primary lacks coming from the player's text font; D17 image and image-set actions (`act`, `click`) and `customEvents` not run (on selection an action is a target the pointer hits, and only its native commands act, §3 Selection); D18 a nonzero `filter` sampled linearly, not with the source's alpha-preserving shader and its nearest bypass at native size (`skin/SkinObject.java:634`); D19 a tapestry limitation: every cell shares an atlas page, so a frame, or a bitmap font's page, wider or taller than the largest page (8 192) less its gutters is dropped with its note, or refuses the skin when critical, where the source cuts a region of any size from the file's one texture and has no such cap of its own (`SkinLoader.java:136-178`, `JsonSkinObjectLoader.java:601-621`). The largest page is 8 192 whatever the device (8 176 texels fit, 8 177 do not: `skin_model::cell_fits_page`, the atlas build's own rule, content plus two 8-texel gutters rounded up to alignment 8). The size judged is the cell the sheet would admit, after an edge substitution or a cover's crop, not the file's: a whole-file frame of a sheet goes alone, its fitting frames stay, and the size of its destination plays no part. An image set with such an entry goes whole, and none of its images reach the sheet unless another element uses them. Import removes only this one mismatch; the atlas build (`Sheet::atlas_reserving`) stays authoritative for a device's lower limits, the pages all the cells need, and installation; D21 notes and bar lines during READY: the lanes follow the display head, which runs on the chart clock through READY (design.md "Play Adjustments"), so the lead-in's notes and bars scroll toward the judgment line, where the source holds lane time at 0 until play unless timer 141 is on (`play/LaneRenderer.java:300-301`) and its first bar stands on the judgment line through READY. Kept because notes approaching before play show the player where they come from; holding the head at 0 until play is the alternative. D23 the multiply blend's colour: the atlas's 8-bit premultiplied texel unpremultiplied, so a transparent texel multiplies by black and a low-alpha one by its coarse colour (straight `[127, 127, 127, 1]` is stored `[0, 0, 0, 1]` and multiplies by black), and filtering weights texels by alpha, where the source multiplies by the file's straight colour (its scaling of a nonzero `filter` is D18's); and the destination's alpha kept, where the source multiplies it by the source's alpha, unseen on an opaque window. Kept because the atlas is premultiplied throughout (design.md "Rendering and Skins"); a straight copy of each multiplied image is the alternative. D24 a play slider moved by a Lua `value` (§3 "Slider") is not drawn in a frame where its own displacement, `R · value`, is beyond 2^40 reference pixels, where the source draws it there; such a paint is off the window unless its destination or a signed, stretched native size is itself of that order (stretch 8 under a ±10⁶ destination scale, mirrored or not), degenerate geometry no surveyed skin comes near. Kept because the cutoff keeps every drawn edge finite in `f32` without a proof over every placement, stretch and rotation; deriving the cut from the final placed geometry is the alternative. D25 the play's score figures (design.md "Rendering and Skins", score figures): the best, the target, their rates, Lua's best rate, the bars of the best and the target and the pass timers 351 and 352 not drawn while the first play's chart loads, though read once its bytes are known, so they appear at READY, where the source shows them from the first loading frame; an element that shows a value only while `best.valid` (the paced best's difference, the best's rate) not drawn while loading either, since only the parsed chart knows whether it declares `#RANDOM`; the rates of the best and the target in exact floored basis points (D4's rule) and 0 over no target, the ranks and the pass conditions compared exactly in 27ths and EX points, where the source divides in `float` (`ScoreDataProperty.java:94-130`); the paced best linear, `⌊B · p / n⌋`, where the source paces by the best play's ghost when one is stored (`ScoreDataProperty.java:114-122`); the rates, the targets and the ranks over twice the targets, where the result and the selection use the targets times the preset's largest EX weight, so under a custom EX weight a play's best rate differs from its result's; on a chart that declares `#RANDOM` or `#SETRANDOM`, nothing measured against the best (its paced value's difference, its rate, bars 112 and 113, Lua's best rate as 0), where the source shows them though the best may come from another draw; each practice round's figures at their values before the first judgment from its READY, where the source keeps the previous round's until the next judgment (`play/BMSPlayer.java:525-582`); and a bar's texture cut fractionally, where the source cuts whole texels (`skin/SkinGraph.java:95-106`). Kept because each is exact, belongs to the attempt it is drawn over and appears at once; the source's float figures, a stored ghost (stage 3, S10-14), per-round carry-over and whole-texel cuts are the alternatives. D26 the play graphs and visualizers (design.md "Rendering and Skins", play graphs and visualizers): prepared with the attempt and drawn from READY, the reveals running on `screen` (D3), so at READY a reveal has run as long as `screen` has, where the source draws them, revealing, while the play screen loads; a notes-graph bin of a chart from 511 s long is its fullest second of `⌈len/512⌉`, and its 10- and 60-bin lines mark bins, not seconds; the notes graph's length is the notes' and the BGM's; the cursors drawn every frame at their exact place, where the source redraws them every 50 ms at whole texels; a BPM graph sized by its element's first key in reference pixels at preparation, at most 2 048 columns, where the source takes the destination's size in window pixels on its first frame, so an animated or scaled destination stretches it; at most four runs a column, the rest of a busier column one run in the transition colour; MAIN tied to the earliest tempo; the visualizers' widths, line widths and positions in reference pixels, scaled with the skin, where the source keeps the line width in window pixels; a hit-error average that takes every offset recorded since its last read, where the source takes only the newest a frame, so frames of more than 100 judgments skip the overwritten ones; its line alpha by age at most 1, where the source wraps it; its line places truncated exactly where the source multiplies by a `float` rate; its average's triangle rasterized per column by its own rule; and an imported BPM graph whose stroke passes 16 texels, or whose stretch is not the default, dropped with a note where the source draws it (its `int` stroke arithmetic wraps for the widest strokes, and it stretches its first frame's texture drawn with a negative height), a BPM graph's or visualizer's `filter` ignored with a note where the source's bilinear shader blends adjoining strokes when the graph is scaled, and a timing or hit-error visualizer whose judge width is not 1 to 1 000 ms or whose width is outside the model's range dropped with a note, where the source draws a degenerate band or fails on its pixmap: a refusal over a picture the model does not reproduce. Kept because each keeps one frame's drawing exact, bounded and independent of the frame rate; the source's per-frame or first-frame rasterization is the alternative. D27 the rhythm clock, timer 140 (R3, `rhythm`): exact on the play clock, its rate the tempo in force at `now`, where the source integrates the previous frame's tempo read at `now` plus the judge timing offset (`play/LaneRenderer.java:300-318`, `play/RhythmTimerProcessor.java:64`), so after a tempo change the rate differs for at most a frame, and under a display offset the source's rate switches that much earlier than its restarts; every bar line a frame passes consumed in that frame, the value counting from the latest, where the source restarts at one line a frame (`RhythmTimerProcessor.java:67-70`), so after a frame spanning two lines it counts from the first and restarts again a frame later; in a practice round, off through the lead-in and on from the section's start with the rhythm since the latest bar line at it, fresh each round, at the round's speed, where the source's practice play, and its clock, start a second of chart time before the section (`play/BMSPlayer.java:586, 610-612`), its integer `100 / freq` leaves the lines unscaled from 55 % to 95 % and passes them all above 100 %, and its line cursor and value carry from round to round; and the tempo run after completion ending at the progress end (the last object, R3) + 5 s, where the source's ends at the last note + 5 s (the last object in autoplay, `BMSPlayer.java:66, 199`), so where objects follow the last note it runs longer.
