Kept because the clock follows one exact clock for its rate and its restarts, the lead-in is READY, where no play timer runs, and the readouts already end at the progress end; the source's frame-lagged, display-shifted tempo, one line a frame, its practice arithmetic and a last-note anchor are the alternatives. D28 the frames per second, number 20 (design.md "Rendering and Skins", play readouts): the frames presented in a sliding window of the last 1 000 000 µs at the frame, counted over a ring of the latest 1 024 present instants and so at most 1 024, where the source shows its graphics library's count, updated once a second (`IntegerPropertyFactory.java:98`); the two differ by a frame or two at a steady rate, and by more while the rate changes, since the source shows the previous whole second's count: through a stall it still shows that count (30, say) where tapestry, counting the last second, shows 0; the number here also moves within the second. Kept because the row asks for the frames presented over the last second, and a fixed ring keeps the count allocation-free; a per-second bucket without a ring is the alternative. D29 the uptime, numbers 27 to 29 (design.md "Rendering and Skins", play readouts): read from the monotonic QPC clock the player starts at launch, where the source subtracts wall-clock times (`MainController.java:60, 671-673`), so a correction of the system clock moves the source's uptime and not tapestry's; no difference in normal use. The local time, numbers 21 to 26, is read from the system each frame (`GetLocalTime`), so a change of the time zone while the player runs shows at once, where the source keeps the zone it started with. Kept because that clock is the player's one monotonic clock, and the system's local time is what the player shows elsewhere. D30 the play's ending (design.md "Play Lifecycle", Audio tail), where the source finishes at its anchor plus 5 s of play time, waits `finishmargin`, starts timer 2 and goes to the result after `fadeout`, carrying its audio (`play/BMSPlayer.java:66, 199, 683-686, 750-790`): (1) the wait for the audio: a full play's fade-out starts at the later of its audio's end and its finish point plus `finishmargin`, so with audio sounding past that point the result comes later than the source's, BGM events after the finish point still play where the source stops starting them (`:752-754`), and a press's key sound in the hold can extend it; the autoplay anchor is the last note or background sound, without the source's hidden notes, BGA and layers; alternative: the source's fixed time, with the audio carried into the result. (2) The move-on press: the quit key after completion shows the result at once, skipping the rest of the hold and the fade-out, where the source starts timer 2, ignores presses during it and always plays `fadeout` (`:756, 956-967`); alternative: the source's. (3) The declared values: their whole range is honoured in `i64` µs, and a negative `fadeout` skips `finishmargin` and the fade-out as the source's does (`:756`, `TimerManager.java:37-42`); what departs: `finishmargin` is read top-level for every play skin, with or without a note definition, as `close` is (D14), where the source sets it only through the note's destination (`JsonPlaySkinObjectLoader.java:37-44`), so a skin that declares it but defines no note waits its whole declared `finishmargin` here and none in the source; a negative `finishmargin` is read as 0, which starts timer 2 on the same frame or one frame later than the source; and a `fadeout` of 0 draws no timer-2 frame, where the source draws one at timer-2 time 0; alternative: the source's frame-exact behaviour. (4) Practice rounds: a round rings out 1.5 s with no 5 s margin, no `finishmargin` and no fade-out (design.md "Mid-Chart Start and Section Repeat"); alternative: the source's `playtime` (`:587`), then `finishmargin` and the fade-out. (5) Feedback past the finish point: from the anchor plus 5 s until the hold ends, presses sound and key beams light (design.md "Audio tail"), where the source's `stopJudge` stops judging, key sounds and beams there (`:751`, `play/KeyInputProccessor.java:124-131`); alternative: stop the feedback at the finish point.
  - **Report**: the definition files read, each file choice's rule, the notes located by file, line and column (each file walked once), and the charged counters. A note is `Dropped`, `Deviation`, `Ignored` or `Fallback`: a part drawn with the source's own generated paint, or a colour channel clamped as the source clamps it, a recovery the source also makes, neither a deviation nor a dropped part.
- **Mapping** (S6b-2) adds the play objects. Objects are looked up in the source's order, `note` last, so an id shared with `hiddenCover`, `judge` or the others is theirs; a `pmchara` sharing the note's id refuses.
  - **Lanes.** Exactly eight notes and lanes, all on one `y`, key lanes overlapping, coincident or in any x order as authored (rounded edges admitted as before) and the scratch wholly beside them (a scratch reaching a key is the model's `ScratchNotAtSide`, located); the notes in `NoteOrder::SOURCE`, keys 1 to 7 then the scratch, as the source paints by timeline then by lane (`play/LaneRenderer.java:470-518`, `play/LaneProperty.java:46-50`), disjoint lanes included, no note added for an overlap; heads merge by their timeline rows (S6c-13), so heads whose distinct timelines collapse to one position and time keep their timelines' order; the source's lane 7 is the scratch, model lane 0, and its side is the side the skin draws it on (S11 adds the setting). A note's height is its lane's: `size[i]`, else the note frame's, plus the `h` offset, each lane its own (source slot 7 the scratch, model lane 0), kept unrounded, positive, and with its note at its judgment time inside the lanes, each checked on the exact value before conversion, then converted to the model's `f32` as the nearest value unless the model's own fit (`SourceLanes::max_note_h`, the one definition of fit in `f32`, over the rounded bottom) is lower, then as that, so the exact decision stands, moved at most by the bottom's rounding and one `f32` step of the height; the field (its top, line and region) is built on source slot 0's height, as the source takes it from lane 0 (`play/LaneRenderer.java:323-326`), and the tallest height pads the candidate window. `size` is streamed once, every entry a number and at most eight kept, and a size is read exactly from its token as written (sign, digits, point, exponent: `20`, `20.0` and `2e1` are one value, and a value one `f32` would round across a bound is judged on its exact value); a used size the rationals cannot hold is a range error. The source reads and scales it in `f32` (`JsonSkin.java:339-375`, `JsonPlaySkinObjectLoader.java:83-92`); the exact reading differs from it by at most a rounding step, under D1. The line sits half a note above the lanes' bottom. A note's `offset y` is the height of its lower edge above the lanes' bottom at its time; one below 0, which the source draws on another branch below the lanes (`play/LaneRenderer.java:577-581`), or one that puts that note above the lanes' top refuses. Long notes draw as the source draws them (R6), and the `bpm`, `stop` and `time` guides are dropped with a note. Lane offsets apply as direct deltas. The outer destination gives only lifetime (from 0, the constructor's key), timer and conditions, over an opaque white neutral track; its blend is noted and ignored, and runtime offsets refuse. The dedicated HCN images are ignored with a note: a hell charge note draws with the CN parts.
  - **Note parts** (S6c-08). Optional paint is not optional structure. The six arrays the lanes draw are checked before any part is resolved, admitted or generated, scanned in place with only the eight lanes' entries kept: exactly eight `note`, and at least eight `lnstart`, `lnend`, of the two selected bodies and `mine` (`JsonPlaySkinObjectLoader.java:46-77`). The bodies are (`lnbodyActive`, `lnbody`) when `lnbodyActive` is a non-empty array, else (`lnbody`, `lnactive`). A drawn array that is missing, `null`, not an array or short, and a `null`, object or array entry for one of the eight lanes, refuse with `NoteKind::NotePart`, its field and `PartWhy`, at the failing node. Entries past the lanes, the unselected alias and the HCN arrays, which the lanes never draw, are not checked, where the source throws on some of them by transposing every long-note array by `lnend`'s length (`play/SkinNote.java:26-30`); `hidden` and `processed` keep their reading and are ignored with a note. Each lane's part is the first image-table record of its id (`JsonSkinObjectLoader.java:584-598`), with its full grid, timer and cycle. An id naming none, an empty id included unless an image of the empty id exists, draws the paint the source generates (`play/SkinNote.java:112-140`): opaque white for a tap, yellow for a long note's head, end and bodies, red for a mine, each a 32×8 nearest-filtered generated image (R7) added once and shared by every part that draws it, where the source makes a texture per lane. Each such part is reported at its id as a `Fallback` note (`NotePartFallback`: field, model lane, paint) and counted in `Usage::note_fallbacks`, at most 48; a generated image charges an image and a frame on its first use only. A resolved image that fails (its file, rect, grid, cycle or admission) refuses as before and never falls back. A tap naming no image with no `size[i]` refuses (`PartWhy::MissingSize`): the source reads that height from the missing image (`JsonPlaySkinObjectLoader.java:91`). Only the eight lanes' parts are resolved and admitted, where the source resolves whole arrays (`JsonSkinObjectLoader.java:585-594`), so a broken image named only past the eighth entry fails there and not here. An unresolved end keeps the mode's rule (`play/LaneRenderer.java:714-726`): an LN draws body and head only, a CN or HCN its yellow end cap too.
  - **Bar line groups.** Every resolved `note.group` record maps to a bar group (R6), in order, a repeated id kept, as the source makes one image per record (`skin/json/JsonPlaySkinObjectLoader.java:94-113`) and draws each at every bar line (`play/LaneRenderer.java:441-445`). The id is looked up in the image table alone, the first image of it, with its whole grid, timer and cycle; its `len`, `ref` and actions are unused there and here, so an image with `ref` 500 gives ordinary frames. The destination maps as an image's: inheritance, the loop, colour, timer, conditions, blend, pivot, the accepted stretches and native sizes, and offsets 3, 4 and 5 as `Follow`; its rect keeps its reflected and rounded y, neither centred on the notes nor given the lanes' x and width. A group's `clip` is ignored with a note, since the source draws the image itself, outside the scissor (`skin/SkinImage.java:169-174`). Each group is decorative unless it is failure presentation ("Policy"), which refuses instead: a missing image, a failing file, a trimmed stretch or keys past their limit drop that group alone with a note, the notes and other groups kept. A `group` that is not an array, a record that is not an object, a `dst` that is not an array of objects, and a record whose `dst` has no keys refuse, since the source fails on them rather than drawing (`JsonPlaySkinObjectLoader.java:95-113`); its exceptions are not reproduced. A single object written or included where `group` or a `dst` stands refuses too, an intentional difference: the source's JSON reader takes it as a one-element array. A model error in a group points at its record.
  - **SUDDEN+'s paint.** An image listing offset 4 that can reach the lanes, an image group or image set listing it likewise, and a type-4 `slider` without a `value` are each SUDDEN+'s paint, play-critical: a cover paint (R6) in its destination's place in the draw order, with its own track, timer, conditions, blend, colour and placement, drawn whole wherever it stands, wider than the lanes, split, above them or before the notes, as many as the skin has (`skin/json/JSONSkinLoader.java:316-337`, `skin/Skin.java:316-328`, `skin/SkinImage.java:136-149`). Offset 4 moves it down by SUDDEN+'s slide and offset 3 up by the lift's, as any follower. Its stretch is baked into its keys as an image's, stretch 8 and 9 keeping its native size; stretch 5 maps on a static track, fit to its width or, when the destination is lower than the fitted frame, exactly when `h · iw < w · ih` (the source's comparison, for any sign of `w` and `h`), keeping the destination and the frame's middle rows, computed exactly (D1, `skin/StretchType.java:61-68, 125-129, 140-153`), only a single frame cropping and a grid refusing, as does a trim to no rows (a negative height); stretches 3, 7 and 10 and a moving stretch-5 track refuse. An image group or image set shows its choice as an image choice does, a selector the model cannot read refusing. As play-critical, an untimed paint that moves or changes a colour channel other than alpha refuses (D3) unless it settles, when it plays its authored keys (D3). Another offset-4 image is an ordinary image following SUDDEN+ when no frame can reach the lanes: no rotation of any kind, the identity stretch, and every key's x edges outside the lanes' span, read ahead of the walk for an image before the notes; when the notes' destination cannot be read ahead, nothing before it qualifies. Its keys are charged once for both readings. Off the lanes, an image group or image set follows SUDDEN+ as any image does. Numbers, text, gauges and grades listing offset 4 keep their own role and follow.
  - **Slider.** A `slider` (`skin/json/JsonSkinObjectLoader.java:393-417`) maps only as type 4 moving down (`angle` 2) or type 6 (`musicprogress`) at `angle` 0 to 3, without a `value` or `isRefNum`, with a range of 0 or more; on play, one with a Lua `value` maps as below; any other slider is dropped with a note. Its range is scaled to `R = ⌊1080 · range / h⌋` reference pixels, `h` the skin's height, which for a horizontal one (`angle` 1 or 3) is the source's `⌊1920 · range / w⌋` of its width, every accepted size being 16:9 (`:398-415`), exactly and once, a result past `MAX_COORD` refusing. A type-4 slider's paint moves down by `R · cover · (1000 − lift) / 10⁶`, 0 while SUDDEN+ is off (`skin/property/FloatPropertyFactory.java:139-151`, `skin/SkinSlider.java:116-120`), on top of offset 4 when it lists that; it is play-critical unless proven outside the lanes as an offset-4 image is. A type-6 slider's paint is decorative, with a D4 note (the progress's end, above), and moves by `R · time.progress_ppm / 10⁶` (R3) up, right, down or left for `angle` 0 to 3 (the source's y up, `skin/SkinSlider.java:116-120`), offsets moving it as any image's. `changeable` installs nothing, since the play has no pointer, and an `event` is unused on that path, noted as ignored. The source scales the range at its own destination resolution and truncates there, so the motion can differ by under one source pixel (D1): a 720 px skin's range 5 at 3/8 of the lane moves 1.75 px of a 1 280×720 target, where the source moves 1.875. A play slider with a Lua `value` (`JsonSkinObjectLoader.java:398-402`, `SkinLuaAccessor.java:665-673`) maps at `angle` 0 to 3 with its range scaled as above: its value compiles into the presentation program (S7-06d), converted by `tofloat()` (design.md "beatoraja Lua Skins"), and moves its paint by `R · value`, unclamped, a non-finite value at 0; with no value (an unavailable read, a loading frame) or past 2^40 reference pixels (D24), it is not drawn that frame. `type`, `isRefNum` and `event` beside the value are noted ignored, as the source does not read them. Its base role is decorative, never SUDDEN+'s paint, whatever it reaches or its type: its travel is the skin's own value, and what it covers is none of R6's covers. A read showing a critical part promotes the whole element, which then follows the critical dependency rules (a blend, a stretch or an asset it cannot reproduce refuses; an untimed settling track plays its authored keys, D3); failure presentation refuses; a value that does not compile drops the slider with its callback note, or refuses the skin when its role is critical or its role scan leaves an id or callee unresolved. The callback is kept in the program only once the slider is retained. A `value` that is not a function, and any slider `value` off play, is dropped with a note.
  - **LIFT and HIDDEN+** (`liftCover`, `hiddenCover`). Every destination of either is a cover paint (R6) of its own in its place in the draw order, repetitions kept, with its own track, timer, conditions, blend, colour and placement, as the source makes a cover object per destination (`skin/json/JsonPlaySkinObjectLoader.java:182-210`) and draws objects in destination order (`skin/Skin.java:316-328`). The record's own source rectangle, grid, timer and cycle give its image; it names no image-table id and has no `len` or `ref` selection. LIFT's paint moves with the lift (`CoverMotion::Lift`), HIDDEN+'s with the lift and its own share (`Hidden`) and only while HIDDEN+ is on: the offsets the source adds, 3 on LIFT and 3 and 5 on HIDDEN+, are that motion, consumed however `offset` and `offsets` list them, and an extra 4 follows SUDDEN+ (`Follow::Cover`). `disapearLine` gives the trim, −1 when absent: a negative line none, else `1080 − line · 1080 / h` on exact rationals, `h` the skin's height, rounded half up, rising with the lift when `isDisapearLineLinkLift` (false on LIFT, true on HIDDEN+ when absent, `skin/json/JsonSkin.java:380-407`) and fixed otherwise; static offsets move the destination, never the line. Op 273 compiles as any condition, either sign. The destination maps as an image's, with the accepted stretches and native sizes, stretch 5 on a static track with SUDDEN+'s exact crop, and 3, 7, 10 and a moving stretch-5 track refused. A trimmed paint whose stretch changes its rect keeps the unstretched track as its gate, whose keys are charged after the image is admitted and stay spent when it is dropped; a trimmed destination of negative size cannot be gated. LIFT is play-critical: what cannot be reproduced refuses, an untimed LIFT that moves or changes a colour channel other than alpha and does not settle included (D3). HIDDEN+ is optional for the player, so what cannot be reproduced is dropped with a note naming the field and the skin still imports, unless the destination is failure presentation ("Policy"), which refuses; aggregate limits and range errors keep their policy.
  - **Judge.** Index 0 only, drawn once, as a judgment display in `screen`: the outer destination's timer, conditions and lifetime go onto the element, which gets a neutral color track (white, opaque), since the target does not multiply that destination's color into its children. Grades and combos per slot, combos for slots 0, 1, 2 and 6 (slot 6 falling back to 0), `Ten` with blank padding or `Eleven` with the eleventh glyph, centred (`Align::Center`), `x −= w·digit/2` on the raw values, exactly, and only the drawn edges rounded (the source's integer division, which drops half a source pixel, is not reproduced): a key omitting both `x` and `w` inherits the shifted `x`, and one omitting only one of them is a range error. Each combo becomes `Attach::Bottom` below its grade with `cy = −(combo y + combo h)` in top-left terms, so no keys are rewritten across independently timed tracks. A grade under stretch 1, 2, 4 or 6 whose baked paint moves its left or bottom edge, where an effective state pairs it with a combo (slots 0, 1 and 2, and the full-gauge PERFECT through either fallback), keeps its destination before the stretch, reflected and each edge rounded on its own, as its attachment track: the source attaches the combo to the grade's region and stretches only a copy at drawing (`play/SkinJudge.java:115-125`, `skin/SkinObject.java:649-667`). Its keys are charged before they are built, as ordinary keys counted again in `Usage::grade_anchor_keys`, and spent if the judge is later refused; past the key limit the import stops with `ImportLimit::Keys` at the grade's destination. A grade no combo pairs with, or whose stretch keeps both edges, keeps no attachment. The rounding of exact rational edges may differ from the source's float sampling by a rounding step (D1); trimmed stretches and a stretch changing its branch between keys stay refused. A grade listing offsets 3, 4 or 5 follows the covers; its combo takes 3 and 4 without motion of its own and moves with its grade, as the source skips a relative object's own translation and attaches it to the grade (`skin/SkinObject.java:404-428`, `play/SkinJudge.java:122-125`); offset 5 on the combo refuses. An entry the judge draws must name its record, whatever tables the skin has: an `images` entry of slots 0 to 6, and a `numbers` entry of slots 0, 1, 2 and 6 within the images, refuse without an `id`; an id naming no record leaves the slot empty. Entries never drawn are not read, and fewer numbers than images leave the rest without combos, where the source throws on some of them (`JsonPlaySkinObjectLoader.java:230-232, 243-245`). Quads: the maximum over its states.
  - **Gauge.** Node maps for 4, 8, 12 and 36 nodes, the parts as static frames (frame 0, nearest; the target builds them with timer and cycle 0), the motions with a range of 0 or less still for every motion (an intentional policy; the source's arithmetic on such ranges is not reproduced), at most `MAX_SEGMENTS` parts (16 384) as the source passes the authored count uncapped (`JsonSkinObjectLoader.java:551-554`), the count kept as authored where the source may rewrite it on a mode conversion (`play/SkinGauge.java:134-150`, D10); `parts`, `type`, `range` and `cycle` read as exact integers (`JsonSkin.java:369-377`): a whole number in any spelling or a decimal string, a fraction, `null` or a non-integer string a type error, past `i32`, or a number whose exact rational value overflows (such as `1e-100`), a range error, the defaults 50, 0, 3 and 33 only when absent; a flicker's positive range only enables it, whatever its size; the node array's shape and count are checked from at most 37 entries before any id is read; after the destination's own admission, its quads (parts and an effective flicker's tip) are charged before any node is looked up or admitted, a never-drawn destination charging nothing, and spent if it is later refused; it ignores stretch and filter and clears the angle (the target draws segments unrotated).
  - **Lane approximations** (D9, each accepted by the owner and noted): notes stop at the lanes' top; `dst2` and `expansionrate` are ignored; a long note within one note height of its end draws no body (the source draws it flipped); a bar line stays while any group draws it inside the lanes, through a STOP and after its time, where the source draws a bar only while its time is ahead (`play/LaneRenderer.java:357-359`), and with no scroll span no bar draws (`BarLine`, noted once per group kept). The bars travel from the chart position at the display head and their groups animate on the frame clock, where the source rewrites one draw clock from its own origins and passes it to both (`play/LaneRenderer.java:300-306, 444`, `skin/SkinObject.java:348-357`, `skin/SkinSourceImage.java:73-88`; D2, D3); the travel keeps its fraction where the source truncates it to whole output pixels (D1). Followers (`CoverMotion`) move by this frame's lane geometry, from the first frame and while the lanes are hidden or their timer is off, and not at all in a skin without lanes; the source prepares every object before it draws (`skin/Skin.java:316-328`) but sets the offsets while it draws the lanes (`play/LaneRenderer.java:332-345`), so its followers can show the previous frame's motion, and it keeps the offsets on the controller across states (`MainController.java:124-137`, `MainState.java:146-147`). LIFT's and HIDDEN+'s paint move the same way and are noted with them. A HIDDEN+ that is off draws none of its paint, where the source hides it by an alpha offset that it skips while the colour interpolates (`play/LaneRenderer.java:335-345`, `skin/SkinObject.java:480-519`), noted on each kept HIDDEN+ destination with an animated colour (`HiddenDisable`). Not reproduced and not noted: the source's float steps, its amount's NaN and infinity, its offsets updated a tick late and its stale lift for the trim (`play/SkinHidden.java:60-67`).
  - Tests: a memory fixture with the play objects; deduplication on real ids.
- **Lua** (design.md "beatoraja Lua Skins", and "Threat Model", Lua skins) runs during import, at startup on the import thread S10-07 runs and after it only at an explicit skin change (S11) or an attempt's specialization (S10-07c), and at runtime only on the selection, decision and result screens (S7-07), never from READY through the audio tail or Closing. Lua execution, source analysis, configuration export, import facts, table conversion, callback lowering and compilation live in `skin-beatoraja`; `skin-model` stays free of `mlua` and receives its existing programs, timer references and widget definitions, and from S7-06 the presentation program with its verifier and executor. S7-01 to S7-06 touch neither `game` nor `eval`, and touch `render` and `player` only in two places: S7-06a adds one arm to the `FactId` match in `player::imports`, and S7-06d-3 gives each play frame the state clock (`SkinFrame::state_us`, through `render::FrameInput` and `render::skin_frame`), whose origin `player` stamps where a play state is entered and its render side samples once a frame. A host property that would need new production state is refused, not produced, and the import facts' snapshot is a type the caller fills. S7-07 is render side.
  - **Dependency.** `mlua` 0.12.2 with its defaults off and only `lua54` and `vendored`: no Lua 5.5, LuaJIT, module mode, async or serialization. The graph is locked in both lockfiles (the workspace's and `fuzz/`'s), deny, audit and machete run over both with advisory and index failures failing (docs/setup.md), the vendored C builds on Windows and Linux, and the MIT notices of `mlua` and Lua are kept; both are compatible with this GPL layer.
  - **Sandbox.** Each VM gets a fresh environment built from an allowlist, exposing no filesystem, network or process API, unrestricted package machinery, dynamic source loader, debug access, output, random source, finalizer or coroutine library. Local Lua comes only as text through `skin_model::Root`, by two loaders that resolve virtual paths as the JSON importer does and admit only regular files inside the skin folder, with canonical identity, loader depth and cumulative read limits: `require`, cached within one pass, a cycle refused; and `dofile`, each invocation executed and charged on its own, which loads the selected local parts that three of the five surveyed 7-key play skins compose themselves from, through protected calls. Neither searches the working directory, environment paths or native modules. A required part that does not load is never a successful conversion.
  - **Caps**, regression ceilings that bound crafted input coarsely rather than exact proofs (design.md "Threat Model"), each charged before its work across the candidate's two passes: 64 MiB of Lua-managed memory, compilation included; 10 million instructions, the hook installed on every VM thread the host creates, not only the main one; 100 000 calls into host functions; the existing loader depth 16 (`MAX_INCLUDE_DEPTH`) and 1 024 load invocations (`MAX_INCLUDE_PARSES`); 16 MiB a file (`MAX_FILE_BYTES`) and 32 MiB in all (`MAX_INPUT_BYTES`); the existing depth, node, expanded-value, string and decoded-byte limits on the converted structure; a bounded analysis ledger and capture graph, under R4's source, nesting, operation and stack limits, which stay authoritative; and the existing 4 096 retained notes (`MAX_NOTES`), the rest counted. Generated values are charged before they are traversed or allocated, shared tables at each expansion; cycles, sparse structures, mixed tables where an array, a condition or a choice branch is read, and over-limit captures are refused. A VM cap does not bound Rust-side ASTs or converted tables, which are charged on their own. Instruction hooks miss C work, so a skin reaches only an allowlist of the library, what the surveyed skins use (design.md "Threat Model" lists it), written as the sandbox's own Lua: a shared pattern matcher and raw-access table functions (a table with a metatable refused) run as metered Lua, and no function coerces a string to a number or back, string arithmetic included; each is an ordinary error. The native primitives they keep are called only with arguments of the exact Lua type, checked first, for a result whose size is checked against a small per-call bound and charged in bytes first; an unsupported pattern, format conversion or `tonumber` base is refused with its location. Ordinary errors stay Lua values; host functions return a status, never a Rust error (mlua would wrap one with a traceback outside the VM's memory limit), and a fault is raised in Lua as a fixed marker, and host functions raise no error of their own, `get_path`'s argument checked in Lua and the loaders' names in Rust, names and paths at most 1 024 bytes measured before they are copied. `lua/library.rs` tabulates the bound of every native a skin reaches; one VM instruction can cost up to the size of VM memory, so the hook runs every 100 instructions and a candidate's two passes also stop at 5 s of monotonic Lua time in all (`MAX_LUA_TIME`; the time between them not counted), a cap on the CPU a candidate can use like the others; cancellation is seen within 100 instructions, and the hook's own fault is raised again at every step past a grace of 1 000 steps. The callbacks' analysis holds at most 1 Mi tree nodes (`MAX_AST_NODES`), parses and walks 200 levels deep (`MAX_AST_DEPTH`), copies 65 536 captures (`MAX_CAPTURES`), expands helpers into 1 Mi nodes (`MAX_EXPANDED_NODES`), does 16 Mi units of work (`MAX_ANALYSIS_WORK`, cancellation seen every 4 096 units and every 64 KiB the parser scans), spends 5 s of monotonic time in its phases (`MAX_ANALYSIS_TIME`: closing the capture graph, the effect walk, admission and each lowering, checked as each starts and ends, at each root the walk visits and every 4 096 units; the time between phases not counted) and keeps 64 MiB of rewritten chunks (`MAX_REWRITTEN_BYTES`); a function with more locals than Lua's 200 is refused. A metatable's `__name` is at most 64 bytes, a caught error's message is charged before the skin's handler sees it, and every load invocation counts, cached source included. No skin code runs with the hook off (design.md "Threat Model"). A protected call, a `dofile` and `string.byte` give back at most 16 results, within the free stack of a native call, more refused. Exhaustion and cancellation set a sticky fatal state that the exposed protected-call wrappers propagate instead of returning a catchable failure. A refusal returns no candidate; S10-07's transaction keeps the installed skin, and S7 holds no installation logic.
  - **Cancellation.** S7-02 delivers portable, cooperative cancellation checks through VM execution (the library's loops included), source reads, parsing, analysis and conversion, and owned results and errors that cross threads without Lua handles. Compilation, garbage collection, the at most 100 instructions before the next check (each at most VM memory) and a blocked OS read are documented bounds, not deadlines; the Lua time cap bounds the CPU a candidate uses. S10-07 owns the import thread, dependency tracking and installation.
  - **Two passes.** (1) Bounded source snapshots are read; a fresh VM runs the entry without `skin_config`, host-module handles importable but no live state readable, the header is converted through the common header path (`read_header`'s), and the VM is discarded. (2) The configuration is validated against that header with the existing choice, default and offset rules; another fresh VM exports a pass-local `skin_config` (`option`, `enabled_options`, `file_path`, `offset`, `get_path`; `file_path` holds each file choice's chosen candidate by name, a file or folder in the choice's folder, and `get_path` resolves a path relative to the entry's folder with the same choices substituted; a tagged path substitutes like any other, `file_path` never holds a tag, and neither holds a host path: `get_path` returns the substituted text, which can keep a tag when another choice's name matched first), a writable copy built from the validated header and settings whose writes reach nothing outside the pass (design.md "beatoraja Lua Skins"), runs the entry again and captures the returned definition and its closure bindings before it is discarded, or kept for a selection, decision or result screen's runtime (S7-07). Only immutable source snapshots and accounting pass between them. `main_state.offset` resolves at load only where analysis proves it reads an available, passive configured offset; no invented runtime state is returned to let loading go on. From S7-04 the configured pass also reads import facts (design.md "beatoraja Lua Skins"): `option(n)` with an integer `n` answers autoplay (32 and 33, false outside a play scene, as the source's are), the chart's judgment class (180 to 184, the class the source computes from the chart's own rank whatever the play preset, the one `chart.judge_class` holds at frame time, R3; all false for a chart in no class), course mode (290, false) and `table_song` (1008, false, a scene constant recorded nowhere, as for the table's texts); from S7-06a `number(96)` answers the chart's play level (`SongData.getLevel()`, Java's `Integer.parseInt` of `#PLAYLEVEL`, a known 0 when it does not parse, unavailable only without a chart, pending in a base import) and `text(n)` answers 1001 to 1003, the difficulty table's texts, as `""`, a scene constant recorded nowhere, every other `number` and `text` id refusing as live state; a negative `n` negates; an `n` the source's factory does not know is false and records nothing; a fact the snapshot has as pending (bound to the chart or the attempt, in a base import) stops the import with a pending result recording it, not a refusal; any other built-in, a fact the snapshot has as unavailable, or an argument that is not a Lua integer within `i32` (checked as a subtype and range in the host bridge, before any absolute value is taken) refuses at the read, located and fatal, so neither `pcall` nor `xpcall` catches it. The result records its scene and each fact read with its value, once, in first-read order; it is valid only for a snapshot of the same scene, the scene of its skin's kind.
  - **Common conversion.** The returned table becomes the resolved-table representation, extended with typed callable references and source provenance, through the existing object lookup and duplicate-ID behaviour, geometry admission, note-part validation and fallback reporting, roles, covers, guards, loading availability, cumulative budgets and model checks, each callback located by its origin and its consumer's field. Tables are read without invoking metamethods, an empty table by the expected schema. A table with string keys and others is a record of its string keys, literal (its `include`, `if`, `value` and `values` are fields, never wrappers), its other entries never read (not converted, charged or captured) and noted `Ignored{"non-string keys"}` at the start of the configured import; where an array, a condition or a choice branch is read (an array field, a literal array, a `dst` list, digit offset rows, a condition, its items and alternatives, a branch of a choice list, a note part array, the `lnbodyActive` probe included) it is refused (`LuaWhy::Mixed`) before any include, and the readers with their own array check refuse it with their type error. Numbers retain their Lua subtype and exact value. Integers are never normalized through `f64`; Lua floats are decoded from their exact bit patterns. Decimal formatting, lossy numeric conversion and subtype-erasing integer coercion are not used to make importer decisions. Per-lane note heights follow §3's lanes rule as it stands, until S6c-13's owning PR changes it.
  - **Screen-neutral boundaries.** The conversion keeps the source skin type and provenance in a screen-neutral definition, and 7-key admission and play-widget mapping sit in a play adapter. Typed callables keep their expected result kind, closure identity and symbolic dependencies before they are bound to a schema, with no play assumption in capture analysis. Property bridges and the refusal policy are chosen per screen profile, which tells an unavailable property from a valid zero or false and names its own critical sinks. The report carries the skin type and profile with generic reasons, locations and counters, under one shared privacy allowlist. S7 ships only the play profile; later screens reuse loading and analysis without their APIs, widgets or runtime existing now.
- **Per-frame functions.** Each callback goes to the first backend that represents it exactly (design.md "beatoraja Lua Skins", backends): an expression, with its acceptance proof; on play, the verified presentation program (S7-06); outside play, the bounded runtime (S7-07). Refusal and capture rules follow the backend: the expressions and the program read owned copies of their captures and refuse or freeze by role on play, and the runtime keeps the VM and falls back to typed constants. The expression backend, as built: their captures are copied, then the VM is discarded, then they are classified and lowered during the common import, by analysis of a bounded Lua 5.4 AST with lexical binding resolution and spans, never source-pattern matching, bytecode layout or privileged upvalue inspection. Each constructed closure is tagged with its AST identity and a unique instance identity; compiler-generated capture readers observe the final bindings after the configured pass, not values copied at construction, and only bounded supported captures are copied into owned analysis data before Lua is discarded. Acyclic pure helpers inline under an expansion budget; frame-time writes, mutable captures, unknown calls and unproven alias effects are outside it, except a recognized observer. As built in S7-03, S7-06b and S7-06c (design.md "beatoraja Lua Skins", how closures are found and captured, and classes): the tagger and readers are inserted into each chunk the configured pass parses; host modules are read-only proxies there; every root in the returned definition (a function at a property field or an `op` entry, by the source's schema; any other function is inert, never classified) is walked before the common import against the modeled subset (S7-06b), the first construct outside it, a script, an unread listed entry or a font fallback with a metatable opening the candidate, which voids every capture fold and binding, and a root that reaches a write never lowering; each root is partially evaluated where its field is consumed (S7-06c): helpers inlined with their arguments, `if`, locals and the gated join (a residual `if` merges the locals its arms assign with a select, a possible `return` carried as a condition and a value), each residual node charged, and the tagger's provenance mark on `local d = X.timer_off_value`; one that does not lower follows its role (a decorative one replaced, a critical one refusing the candidate) until S7-06 and S7-07 give it another backend; a number's, an image set's or a text's role covers every id its whole tree and helpers may read, an id or callee left unresolved making it critical; every walk over the trees is charged to one cumulative work allowance; and there is no `/`, `%`, float, string operation or library call in a converted callback yet.
  - **Acceptance proofs**, in checked integer and rational arithmetic, a Lua float being its exact binary value with its subtype, with no general floating-point emulator: integer arithmetic agrees between Lua and every lowered R4 intermediate within their ranges, never relying on wraparound; `/` keeps its float semantics and is lowered only where an exact rewrite proves the result, never replaced by R4's floor division; `%` and floor operations are proven for operand types, divisor behaviour and rounding, negative operands and zero included; `and` and `or` keep their operand values and Lua's truthiness, zero and the empty string true; branches keep observable results, errors and side effects, R4's eager evaluation used only after that proof; strings, coercions and subtypes keep their exact semantics or are rejected, with no numeric-string guessing or subtype-erasing fold. A floating-dependent decision or callback left unproven is a located unsupported-semantics result. A sample execution is a test, not an equivalence proof. As built (S7-06c), a conditional becomes R4's `if … then … else` only when it is int-or-bool, total, pure and range-proven (design.md "beatoraja Lua Skins", the acceptance proof), and an integer folds only when the source holds the same double: a numeral by its provenance, a captured integer within ±2^53, the off value only with a recorded provenance.
  - **Bridges**, initially: the existing integer, boolean and selector properties; `main_state.gauge_type` through the existing projection from `gauge.kind`, guarded by `gauge.valid`, never exposing enum discriminants as source indices (unsupported uses follow their consumer's role; a callback controlling gauge visibility is critical and refuses without the bridge); direct chart-text fields; judgment-count aliases over existing model fields; the supported timer identities, off tests and proven elapsed operations; and the boolean-observer API. A raw absolute timer start (µs) is not an R4 integer (elapsed ms): timer values stay symbolic until a supported operation or a `TimerRef` consumes them, the off sentinel kept for on and off testing only. An observer, recognized by the host API's identity with its predicate and observation semantics validated, becomes one `when` slot per accepted instance, sharing kept, only when its transitions, lifetime and observation agree; identical predicate text does not merge instances, and a clock latch without a false-edge reset stays unsupported unless an existing native timer is proven equivalent. Support is published by API and by property or operation: a selector backed by unavailable note-modifier or speed-policy state, best and target rates, string composition and dynamic string expressions stay outside the expressions, and a text's empty-string fallback is a prepared `TextValue`, not an expression; the program (S7-06) and the runtime (S7-07) take what their bridges back.
    S7-03's bridges: `number(n)` and `option(n)` through the JSON value and built-in boolean mappings (an `option` the factory does not know is `false`, as the source's is); `timer(n)` only against the off value, as `on(t)` or its negation of the JSON timer, the off value read from the host module at frame time or captured where its provenance is recorded; `gauge_type()` only against a known integer, `gauge.kind` against the look it selects under `gauge.valid`, an index past 5 matching no look; `skin_config.option["name"]` folded as any captured value under the capture proof (design.md "beatoraja Lua Skins", built today), in critical callbacks too from S7-06a. `gauge()`, timer arithmetic and the other APIs stay unsupported. Since: `number(425)` and `number(427)`, `combobreak` and `bad_plus_poor_plus_miss` (`IntegerPropertyFactory.java:461-467`), BAD and the scored POORs, with the empty POORs for 427; `judge(n)`, a known integer, the count of judge index `n` and 0 for any other index, 4 the scored POORs, where `ref` 114 keeps `count.poor`, which a custom preset with `empty_poor.counted` makes differ by the empty POORs; and an image set's `value` selecting as a number's value lowers, a bare `gauge_type()` by image index 40's selector, its reads' part joining the element's role through the mapping, one that does not lower following that role and never `ref`. At construction `main_state.offset(n)` answers the configured offset of a declared id, zeros for any other id from 0 to 199, and refuses any other argument (design.md "Import facts").
  - **Fallback.** Roles and dependencies are decided before any fallback, and immutable configuration folding may remove an unused callback first. An unsupported decorative callback becomes an explicitly reported typed constant (false, zero, off or empty text), never obtained by running it: in S7-03 a `draw` becomes `false` (the element dropped with its reason) and a number's `value` the omitted number, and a number reading the EX score or the max combo is critical whatever else it reads; on failure presentation either refuses instead ("Policy"). Aliases and writes into the returned definition are traced: an unsupported callback or write affecting notes, judgment, gauge, combo, score, critical cover behaviour or the failure presentation refuses the skin, even when its immediate consumer looks decorative, and so does an unknown effect that could reach such a sink; a frozen judgment timer or score number would load and look broken, so a fixture keeps such an element present but frozen and expects the refusal. An unsupported state read never stands in as a legitimate zero or false.
- **Widgets** map onto R6's fields:
  - `value` becomes a `number` (S6b-1) of EX score, max combo, the note count, the gauge, the judgment counts, the empty poor count or the score rate (S6c-02, decorative with a D4 note): 102 (`score_rate`) is `rate.current_bp / 100` and 103 (`score_rate_afterdot`) `rate.current_bp % 100`; 115 (`total_rate`) and 155 (`score_rate2`) are `rate.whole_bp / 100`, 116 (`total_rate_afterdot`) and 156 (`score_rate_afterdot2`) `rate.whole_bp % 100`, with no padding forced and no decimal point added. The early and late counts (S9-01, decorative, no note): 410 to 419 are `count.early.<kind>` and `count.late.<kind>` in pairs PERFECT to POOR, 421 (`early_miss`) and 422 (`late_miss`) `count.early.empty_poor` and `count.late.empty_poor`, and 423 (`totalearly`) and 424 (`totallate`) `count.fast` and `count.slow`; 420 (`miss`) stays `count.empty_poor`. The play readouts (S6c-03, decorative with a D4 note, R3): 90 (`maxbpm`), 91 (`minbpm`) and 160 (`nowbpm`) are `bpm.max_x100`, `bpm.min_x100` and `bpm.current_x100` `/ 100`; 92 (`mainbpm`) is `chart.main_bpm` (S10-13); 161 (`playtime_minute`) and 162 (`playtime_second`) `time.elapsed_s / 60` and `% 60`, 163 (`timeleft_minute`) and 164 (`timeleft_second`) the same of `time.remaining_s`; 525 (`judge_duration1`, `IntegerPropertyFactory.java:469, :909-916`) `judge.recent_ms`, signed, with no note (R3); 368 (`chart_totalgauge`, `IntegerPropertyFactory.java:389-394`) `chart.total` under `chart.total_valid`, the song information's total, with no note (R3); 96 (`playlevel`, `IntegerPropertyFactory.java:154, :580-584`) `chart.level`, per chart as in the source, with no note, a negative level showing as the digit layout below shows any negative; 14 (`lanecover1`), 314 (`lift1`), 315 (`hidden1`) and 316 (`lanecover2`) `cover.permille`, `lift.permille`, `hidden.permille` and `cover.absolute_permille`; 10 (`hispeed_lr2`) `hispeed.x100`, 310 (`hispeed`) its `/ 100` and 311 (`hispeed_afterdot`) its `% 100`; 312 (`duration`) `duration.ms` and 313 (`duration_green`) `duration.green`; planned in S10-04b, 1312–1327 the duration variants in four groups (current, MAIN, minimum, maximum), each the configured-cover ms and green then the uncovered ms and green, the green scaled before rounding and the configured cover counted while off, each under its variant's validity. A number whose value may be unknown takes its validity (`bpm.range_valid`, `bpm.current_valid`, `chart.level_valid`, `hispeed.valid`, `duration.valid`) as a guard joined into its visibility before that is compiled and charged, so it draws nothing while the value is unknown, never a 0 standing for it. 15 and 131 are not value properties (the number falls back to its `ref`); 1163 and 1164 read the chart's 1× length (`chart.length_ms`, S10-13); 1312–1327 until S10-04b and 372 and 373 (historical averages) stay unsupported, since they need data of their own. The glyph layout comes from the frame count (a multiple of 24 is `TwentyFour` with `zeropadding`'s padding, else a multiple of 10 `Ten`, else `Eleven`, which forces glyph padding), the first whole set of glyphs kept when they form whole rows; a `digit` above 11 only with blank padding, as 11 cells shifted by the extra cells for the alignment. The cell's position, width and pitch come from each key's exact geometry (the pitch is the width plus the scaled `space`), so rounding may drift by a pixel across cells, and right alignment keeps the last cell exact. The supported values never reach `i32::MIN` or `i32::MAX`, which the target does not draw.
  - `text` becomes a `text` (S6b-1), classified by the current screen's role (decorative on play, unless it is failure presentation, "Policy"). `ref` 10 to 16 map to the fields (12 and 16 to the full ones) and `constantText` to a literal; an empty constant is ignored. The box is the destination moved left by `w·{0, ½, 1}` for left, centre and right. `overflow` 0 is `Spill`, 1 `Shrink`, and 2 `Shrink` (D6). A TrueType font's `size` is ignored (the em is the destination's `h`). Outline, shadow and wrapping are removed with a report line (D6), editable is ignored, and the other refs (1003, 1001, 1002, 3, 1, 2) drop the element. `value` functions go to S7: on play a function that folds at import to a string, a plain `main_state.text(n)` (a direct chart-text field) or a field with a string for when it is empty becomes that prepared value, built in S7-06g's prepared slice; a composed string is outside the expressions: on play the program compiles it (S7-06), outside play the runtime runs it (S7-07), and until then it has no consumer and never falls back to `ref`: the text is dropped with its note, or refuses the skin as failure presentation. Both formats put the cap height at the box's top, so the destination maps with no vertical correction; the remaining metric differences (the target's bearing cancellation and cap probe glyphs) are D5's. A `.fnt` font of type 0 (19 of the 38 surveyed text definitions use `.fnt` fonts) becomes a bitmap chain (S6b-3), one per font record and each file read once, its `fallback[]` paths read as BMFont text whatever their extension: the key's top is kept and its `h` is `size·r` rounded half up (D12), with the shift for alignment unchanged; a `text.size` of 0 is ignored with a note, as the source's scaling throws on it, and a negative one, which the source draws mirrored (`JsonSkinObjectLoader.java:652`, `SkinTextBitmap.java:118-123`), is unsupported: dropped, or refusing failure presentation. Types 1 and 2 are D13's. A primary that fails as the source fails (no `padding`, a broken `common` line, a missing or misnumbered page line, a glyph on a missing page, no glyph drawn with a size, a missing or unreadable page image) is ignored with a note, as the source draws nothing; one refused by our stricter grammar or limits drops the element, and a missing file is a missing asset. A failing fallback is skipped with a note, ignored where the source also fails to load it (a missing or unreadable file included) and dropped where only our grammar, limits or folder confinement refuse it, more than 15 fallbacks is the font limit, and the caps on files, images, frames, font bytes, glyphs and kerning pairs refuse the import. LR2 skins, after stage 3: `#SRC_TEXT` gives the font, string id and alignment, and `#FONT` and `#LR2FONT` the fonts.
  - **Digit offsets** (S6c-09). A `value` record's `offset` table gives the source's per-cell deltas (`skin/SkinNumber.java:196-205`, copied by `JsonSkinObjectLoader.java:127-137, 157-167`, a combo's by `JsonPlaySkinObjectLoader.java:261-271`) to every number and combo drawing it. The tables of the `value` records are resolved once, when the import's tables are, with the wrappers of an array field: an included table, a conditional item's `value` row then its `values`, an included item spliced, an object as a one-row table and `null` as none; a choice that selects nothing, a `null` or non-object row and a field that is not an integer refuse at that row (`ImportError::Type`), so no row moves into another's cell. Each row is counted as an expanded value and against `MAX_RESOLVED_DIGIT_OFFSETS` (65 536 across every table, used or not; `InputLimit::DigitOffsets`) before it is read; `x`, `y`, `w` and `h` are read exactly, a number with a point or exponent only when it is whole (`Q::decimal`) and one outside `i32` a range error, where the source truncates through `f64`. Rows are stored once, as one range of one buffer per value with rows: an empty table keeps nothing, and a value drawn many times is not copied until a strip keeps it. The source adds row `j` to cell `j`'s drawn rect in window pixels, y up, unscaled by the destination, so row `(x, y, w, h)` becomes `(x, −y − h, w, h)` in target pixels, y down: growing the height moves the top edge. Of a `D`-cell value whose leading `E = D − 11` blank cells the model drops, cell `j` takes row `E + j`; trailing zero rows are dropped and inner ones kept, so the fewest offsets keep every cell's row; a kept row outside ±2^20 is a range error at that row. The report's `usage` counts the rows resolved (`resolved_digit_offsets`) and the offsets kept, each copy counted (`digit_offsets`). `main_state.gauge_type()` is S7's to convert, onto the six looks.
- **Screen profiles** (contract from S10-09a; each screen's profile table and essential parts with its importer). Numeric, text, option, timer, image-index, slider, graph and event ids each resolve in their own namespace: numeric, image-index, option and event 42 are four different things. Each profile classifies what a skin reads as **needed** (supported in this cut), **decorative** (a truthful constant or an omission with a report line; an unavailable action also loses its hit target) or **refusal** (the screen's skin is refused and its built-in screen shows). A known unavailable feature gets an explicit constant and never falls through the undefined-option rule above. Lua callbacks (`main_state.number`, `option`, `text`, `timer`, `timer_off_value` and `event_index`, numeric and `draw` functions alike) are classified by their screen role before any conversion, folding, omission or refusal, through the profile's bridges over S7's screen-neutral loading and analysis; on selection, decision and result, a callback the compiled program does not cover runs in S7-07's bounded runtime, its host reads answered by the profile from the screen's snapshot and an unavailable one giving its typed constant with a note. The owner's local skins read, in union per screen: selection 86 numeric, 38 text, 109 option (95 built-in, 14 configured), 4 timer, 34 image-index, 4 slider, 11 graph and 19 event ids; decision 5 text, 1 option and 1 timer id; result 80 numeric, 17 text, 81 option (62 built-in, 19 configured), 3 timer, 11 image-index and 1 event id.
  - **Per-kind contract** (S10-09a). Each table's code comes with its first consumer.
    - Import snapshot: S7-04's import facts for every kind, with the skin's kind as their scene (design.md "beatoraja Lua Skins", import facts). Fact availability per kind:

      | Fact | Play | Selection | Decision | Result |
      | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
      | Autoplay, options 32 and 33 | Known in the base snapshot, from the session | false | false | false |
      | Judgment class, options 180–184 | Pending in the base snapshot, known per attempt from the drawn chart | Unavailable | Pending in the base snapshot, known at the choice, or unavailable when the draw decides it | Pending in the base snapshot, known per attempt |
      | Course mode, option 290 | false | false | false | false |
      | The result profile's numbers and options | Unavailable | Unavailable | Unavailable | Pending in the base snapshot, known per view (shown, hidden as `i32::MIN`, or unavailable) |

      Building the decision snapshot as unavailable when the draw decides the class is S10-07's.
    - Presentation snapshot (the player fills it, render reads it; design.md "Rendering and Skins"):

      | Kind | Snapshot | Type lands in |
      | --- | --- | --- |
      | Play | `PlayView` with `SkinFrame` and `LoadingFrame` | exists |
      | Selection | A bounded window of visible rows around the cursor (row identity, kind, text, level and typed own lamp), the cursor as an exact ratio, the folder and the query; the bounded typed projections the selection table below needs (a selected-chart summary such as BPM, selected-score projections such as EX and BP, backed settings and capabilities); selection UI events | S10-09c (data), S10-09d (model input beside `song list`), S10-09e (settings, events) |
      | Decision | The chosen chart's labels, copied from the select screen's (`skin_model::ChartLabels`) while the cursor rests on it, prepared then and pinned at the choice (`AttemptContext` exists only after entry) | S10-09f |
      | Result | Result and best projections, exact timing statistics, timeline bins | S10-09g, S10-09h |

    - Screen lifetime: each screen's own, apart from any attempt; at its start every timer is off and `screen` starts; the play lifetime's `screen` is the exception and runs on from the play state's screen origin (D3), and the result's starts at the first committed result view's sampled frame instant (a built-in view included), kept through frames not presented and through recovery, its `input` and `scene` times counting from there too (S10-33). Its code comes with each frame adapter (S10-09e, S10-09f, S10-09i) and the per-kind installation keying (S10-09b).

      | Kind | Starts | Ends |
      | --- | --- | --- |
      | Play: loading | The choice, each practice Start and each retry's loading detour | READY; or a quit or a refused load or preparation, back where the chart was chosen: selection on the play path, the practice page from a practice Start (design.md "Exits from the loading state") |
      | Play: play | Each attempt's `ready`, its `screen` running on from the play state's screen origin (D3) | The result, a retry, a quit at READY (back where the chart was chosen) or the next practice round's automatic transition; kept through READY, Playing, AudioTail (its hold and its fade-out, which starts the `fadeout` timer) and Closing (Closing keeps the play frames and the `failed` timer) |
      | Selection | Each entry into selection | A choice or the exit |
      | Decision | The choice (lasting the skin's `scene` plus `fadeout`, or 1,500,000 µs built in, design.md "Play Lifecycle") | The loading state, the practice page, or selection after a refused load |
      | Result | Its entry after ResultPreparing | A retry, back or quit |

    - Profile vocabulary (`skin-beatoraja`, with each importer): id spaces numeric, text, option, timer, image index, slider, graph and event; a treatment of needed (with its bridge), decorative (a typed constant and a note) or refusal; and each kind's essential parts, the counterpart of play's critical parts: selection's song list, selected-row identity and essential text, the result's current result and status, none on the decision screen (only limits and model checks refuse it). A refused play skin has no fallback (Q4-A); a refused skin of another kind leaves its built-in screen. The play profile is the existing play mapping.
  - **Selection** (S10-09c to S10-09e):

    | Group | Ids | Treatment |
    | --- | --- | --- |
    | Rows, cursor and position | `songlist`; slider 1 | Needed: the `song list` widget and slider over a typed visible-row snapshot (R6) |
    | Folders | numeric 300 (with a defined count); row-kind options 1–3, 5, 21–23 | Needed, for #179's minimal folders |
    | Chart and query | numeric 74, 90–92, 96, 350–354, 360, 362–365, 368, 1163–1164; text 10–13, 16, 30, 1000; chart options 160–164, 170–171, 174–177, 180–184, 191, 1160–1161, 1177 | Needed where a fact exists: notes, BPM, level and length from bounded summaries, the query text with native search and IME kept; density metrics and metadata tapestry lacks are decorative |
    | Selected score | numeric 71, 72, 75–89, 100, 102–103, 105, 110–114, 150, 154, 174, 410–427; graphs 140–145; lamp and grade options 100–105, 200–207, 300–307, 1100–1104 | Needed: EX, BP, play and clear counts, the best max combo, the best EX play's breakdown, valid derived rates and ranks, and the typed own lamp; details the bests do not keep are decorative (design.md "Personal Bests", skin projections) |
    | Settings | numeric 12, 57–59, 312–313; image indices 11–12, 15–16, 19, 40, 42–43, 54–55, 72, 75, 77–78, 301–308, 315–318, 330–332, 340–342, 370–371 | Needed where a native setting or lamp backs it, read-only and live (the system volume, and the key and background volumes and the display offset as F3 to F6 leave them, at the next frame); a truthful constant for a feature tapestry lacks; otherwise unavailable with a report |
    | Timers and events | timers 11, 21–23; events 12, 15, 315, 330–332 | Needed: typed UI events for the selection change and panels; sort, play, practice and the cover actions as native commands |
    | Other actions | events 11, 13–14, 16–17, 19, 308, 316–318, 340–342 | Decorative unless a native contract exists (per-choice autoplay, replays, mode filters, configuration pages, documents, other LN modes, judgment and gauge policies): each element a target that takes a press and does nothing |
    | Volume and ratio graphs | sliders 17–19; graphs 17–19, 140–144, 147 | A valid EX-rate bar, and the system, key and background volumes on sliders and graphs 17, 18 and 19, on the `ratio bar` widget, as the source reads them (`FloatPropertyFactory.java:261-275` at `0.8.9`), each on its own slider, graph and number |
    | Separate features | numeric 17–19, 30, 31, 333 (global statistics), 179–180, 227, 241, 271, 283–284, 380–384 (rivals, IR); text 1, 3, 120–124, 150–154, 201–218, 1020; options 50–51, 60, 126–131, 197, 352–354, 606, 624–625, 1002–1007, 1010–1017, 1128–1131, 1197, 1200, 1203, 1205–1208; graphs −2, −1; `judgegraph` type 0; `bpmgraph` | Decorative: targets, rivals, IR, courses, replay slots, transform history, folder distributions and chart previews are features of their own; no history is invented from the current configuration |
    | Missing essentials | No usable list, selected-row identity or essential text | Refusal |

    The importer's profile (S10-09d, `skin-beatoraja`'s `import::selection::profile`) classifies every id the surveyed selection skins read, one explicit `match` per id space; a JSON field's `ref` or `op` and a Lua `main_state` read of the same id go through the same function, so they get the same treatment and draw the same frame. Classes: **N** bridged from the selection schema, the player's settings among them (`SelectionSettings`, applied apart from the view each frame); **E** an action, or a setting with no native equivalent, unavailable with an `Unavailable` note (a setting's element draws nothing; an action's element draws and takes a press that does nothing); **D** a truthful constant (the source's value for "off" or "none" where tapestry lacks the feature, or the image's entry 0 where the source has no property), or omitted with a note; **configured** the skin's own options, folded. An id outside the profile follows its space's existing rules: an option the source's factory knows is unsupported, one neither built in nor declared never draws, and an unknown number, text or image index is unsupported by its role or resolves to nothing.

    | Space | N | E | D |
    | --- | --- | --- | --- |
    | Numeric | 71 and 150 (EX), 72, 74, 76, 77, 90, 91, 96, 102, 103, 154, 300, 1163, 1164, each drawn only while its fact exists; 75, 105 and 174 (the best max combo, of any counted play, `IntegerPropertyFactory.java:627-631`), 78 and 79 (the clears and the plays less them, `:140-152`), each drawn with a score row; 80–84 (the best EX play's PERFECT to POOR, drawn with a score row, `:500-504`) and 85–89 (their whole percent of that play's own notes, drawn while it has notes, where the source divides by its stored score's notes, the chart's as last counted for a best, `:505-511`, `PlayDataAccessor.java:208-210`, the same unless the chart draws); 100 (that play's point score from its own max combo, where the source takes the best max combo, `ScoreDataProperty.java:75-78`); 110–114 and 410–427 (that play's counts, early and late splits, MISS as its empty POORs, the early and late totals, and the sums 425–427, 0 without one, `IntegerPropertyFactory.java:444-467, 512-519`); 12 (the display offset in whole ms, of the source's judge timing's sign); 57, 58 and 59 (the system, key and background volume's `(int)(gain · 100f)`, the `f32` product truncated as the source does, so 0.01 shows 1; `IntegerPropertyFactory.java:122-124` at `0.8.9`); 312 and 313 (Floating's `⌊5 · G / 3⌋` ms and the target green number `G`; nothing in Classic); 20–29 (the application's readouts as play reads them: the presents in the last second, the local date and time, and the uptime's whole hours, minutes and seconds, from one sample per frame, D28, D29) | | 17–19, 30, 31, 92, 179–180, 227, 241, 271, 283–284, 333, 350–354, 360, 362–365, 368, 380–384: omitted |
    | Text | 10–13, 16 (the cursor's row and chart), 30 (the query), 1000 (the place, each name followed by `" > "`) | | 1, 3, 120–124, 150–154, 201–218, 1020: omitted |
    | Option | 1, 2, 5, 21–23, 100–105, 1100–1104, 160–164, 176–177, 180–184 (the selected chart's judgment class, from its library entry), 200–207, 300–307, 1160–1161 | | 50 and 624 true; 3, 51, 60, 126–131, 170–171, 174–175, 191, 197, 352–354, 606, 625, 1002–1007, 1010–1017, 1128–1131, 1177, 1197, 1200, 1203, 1205–1208 false |
    | Timer | 11 (`select.change`), 21–23 (`panel1..3`) | | |
    | Image index | 12 (the source's sorter index: title 0, artist 1, level 4; nothing for the modification order, which it has no sorter for); 40 (the chosen preset's displayed gauge's look, assist-easy 0 to hazard 5); 55 (Classic 0; Floating 1 to 4 by its reference tempo: start, maximum, main, minimum); 78 (the gauge auto shift as `player.toml`'s failure handling: none 0 when a failed play closes, continue 1 when it plays to the end; the label names tapestry's failure handling, not an evaluator equivalence); 330–332 (SUDDEN+, LIFT and HIDDEN+ on), 342 (the hi-speed auto adjustment: 1 in Floating, which re-solves at the tempo at the head, 0 in Classic) and 340 (the judge algorithm of the chosen preset's press assignment: combo 0 for combo priority; duration 1 for the nearest note, earlier at a tie, whose candidate order follows the source's Duration comparator, though the source's candidate loop also admits judged and POOR-only candidates; nothing without a preset), on every row, as the source reads them from the selected bar's play config, a folder's being the mode filter's; 370 (the selected row's clear type, the source's lamp index) | — | 2 for 11 (7-key only) and 72 (no BGA); 0 for 42–43, 54 (no lane option), 75 (no display-timing auto adjustment), 301–307 (no assist option), 308 (LN), 341 (the bottom-shiftable gauge at its default, assist easy, which acts only under an automatic gauge shift tapestry has none of), and 15–16, 19, 77, 315–318 (no property on selection: the image's entry 0); 371: omitted |
    | Slider | 1 (the list position); 17, 18 and 19 (the system, key and background volume: its `f32`'s exact binary value in lowest terms, 0 only where that denominator would pass 2^63, below 2^-40) | | |
    | Graph | 147 (the EX rate); 140–144 (the best EX play's PERFECT to POOR over its own notes, where the source divides by the chart's, `FloatPropertyFactory.java:303-307, 542-561`); 145 (the best max combo over the summary's notes, every chart included, as the source's, `:155-176, 308`); 17, 18 and 19 (as sliders 17, 18 and 19) | | −2, −1: omitted |
    | Event | 12 (the order: Right for a positive click argument, Left for a negative one), 15 and 315 (Enter and the practice key, on a chart row only), 330–332 (the SUDDEN+, LIFT and HIDDEN+ switches turned over, on any row) | — | 11, 13–14, 16–17, 19, 308, 316–318, 340–342: ignored, the element a target that takes a press and does nothing |

    Planned moves out of D and E: S10-10 bridges numbers 92, 350–354, 360–365 and 368 and the graphs `judgegraph` type 0 and `bpmgraph` (§1, owner decision); S10-12 the chart's stage file and banner; S10-15 the local statistics 17–19, 30, 31 and 333. Rivals, IR, courses and difficulty tables stay D: features tapestry does not have yet (§1).

    Any other timer id, play's among them, is undefined on selection, and play's run-time offsets (1 to 5) are ignored with a note; the play loader's own objects (notes, judge, covers, BGA, practice) are never looked up, and a gauge is dropped as unsupported. `customEvents` and the song list's player and rival lamps, trophies and distribution graph are noted and unused; its `clickable` slots are the rows the pointer hits, tried in that list's order (an entry outside the slots is dropped with a note).

    Essential parts, judged after the configured options are folded and only over the active arms (`liston[center]`, and `listoff` elsewhere) and the row kinds tapestry lists (chart, folder, "all charts"); each missing or not reproduced refuses the import with `ImportError::Essential`:
    - **Song list**: a `songlist` that a retained destination names, each active arm given and reproduced (its track, image set and blend, with no stretch); placements past 60 are ignored with a note. Several destinations naming it share one definition and one row text table, each charged its own quads and its parts' visibilities once per slot.
    - **Selected row**: the center slot's `liston`, drawn after folding, its image set's image 0 present and loaded; each row kind's bar image is its own index (chart 0, folder 1, "all charts" 5), image 0 when that index is past the set's end, as the source's (`SkinImage.java:133-135`), or when this importer alone dropped that image (a cap, a glob, a source rectangle). A placement's images run on its set's timing as the source takes it: the first timer given and the first cycle other than -1 among the images that load.
    - **Row title**: each row kind's title style by its chain (chart 2, else 0; folder 4, else 0; "all charts" 9, else 4, else 0); the first that is given (an entry naming a text) must import, never replaced by the next. A row text ignores its stretch, as a text does.

    A missing image file refuses none of these parts except the center slot's image 0, which still refuses, where the source draws an empty center row. As the source draws a set entry that failed to load (an entry naming no image, or a file the source cannot load either: missing, unreadable, a movie, or one whose whole decode fails): a non-center slot whose image 0 is missing draws nothing, its note kept; a folder or "all charts" row whose own image inside the set is missing draws its title without its bar, noted (those rows have no lamp, level or label to draw); the set's timing comes from the images that load. Whether such a slot or row takes a click is the pointer input's, still to come (S10-09e). A file only this importer refuses (a cap, a glob some file matches, a source rectangle over a file that decodes) is no missing file and refuses or drops as before; the source fails a texture before it reads the rectangle, so a refused rectangle is still checked for its file. Each file is decoded once in an import to check it, and a JSON import observes the stop its caller gives between checks, as a Lua one does. ECFN's `select.json` stays refused, its bar images' one file absent from its folder; the source refuses it too, at a number whose file is absent (`skin/json/JsonSkinObjectLoader.java:99-103` at `0.8.9`).

    A Lua visibility that lowers to a constant false folds as the JSON condition of the same id does, so an essential part under it is missing. Every other part (an inactive arm, a level, lamp or label style) that cannot be reproduced is dropped with a note: a lamp or label keeps a stretch that bakes into its keys and drops one that keeps a native size or trims rows. A level takes the selection loader's strip: ten glyphs padded with blanks, or eleven padded with the eleventh, with no digit offsets. The source's seventh level style and its lamp styles 2, 9 and 10 are noted and never drawn.

  - **Decision** (S10-09f): static images, text, tracks and blends on the existing primitives; text 10 to 16 from the chosen chart's labels prepared before entry, never read from files while drawing; text 1003 omitted and course option 290 false (so −290 is true), both decorative; timer 1 the input time, timer 2 the fade-out. Unlike play's D14, the profile reads the skin's `scene`, `input` and `fadeout`; the flow honours `scene` and `fadeout`, and timer 1 starts at the choice plus `input` and is on once more than that has passed (off at the choice plus `input`, on 1 µs later; S10 item 9.4b); no command acts, since the whole screen stays inside the input epoch (design.md "Play Lifecycle", the decision screen). Skipping a skinned screen after its input time, as the source allows, is planned (https://github.com/shuymn/tapestry/issues/478). The two Lua decision skins checked against the source draw their labels in bitmap fonts and fit the profile; one surveyed JSON skin shows its labels on timer 1, so it draws them once timer 1 starts. Fonts come through S10-08a with its fallback.

    The importer's profile (S10-09f, `skin-beatoraja`'s `import::decision::profile`) classifies every id, one explicit `match` per id space, over the decision schema (`skin_model::DECISION`), which has no field: every value a decision skin shows is a constant folded at import or the chart's labels. The decision loader reads the common objects alone: images, image sets and texts; any other object is dropped with a note. A JSON field's `ref`, name or `op`, a Lua callback's `main_state` read and a Lua read while the skin loads go through the same function. Classes: **N** the chart's labels; **D** a truthful constant for a feature tapestry lacks, or omitted with a note; **configured** the skin's own options, folded. A built-in wins over a configured option of the same id, as the source's factory answers built-ins first, and an undefined option follows §3's undefined-option rule.

    | Space | N | D |
    | --- | --- | --- |
    | Text | 10–16 (12 and 16 the full strings, as play builds them) | 1003 (the table's level and name, the source's empty string without difficulty tables): omitted |
    | Option | | 50 and 160 true; 32 and 33 (no autoplay off the play screen, as the source answers them), 51, 161–164, 280–283, 289–290 and 1008 false; 180–184 the judgment class, a load-time fact (the decision schema has no field for it, so a frame condition on it is dropped with a note); 190–195 unsupported until S10-12; any other built-in unsupported, never a constant |
    | Timer | 2 (the fade-out); 1 (the input start, from the choice plus `input`, on strictly after it: S10 item 9.4b) | 0: never set |
    | Number, image index, slider, graph, event | | none: a number, slider or graph is dropped with a note, an image group or set choosing by a known image index is dropped with a note (one naming no known id shows its first image), and an image's `act` is ignored with a note |

    Essential parts: none; only limits and model checks refuse a decision skin, and, when a chart's labels are prepared, a label set whose rows do not fit the installation's label slots at the output size (design.md "Rendering and Skins"). The importer reads the top-level `scene`, `input` and `fadeout` in ms, JSON and Lua alike, as µs (`Skin::decision_timing`): an absent or negative value 0, `scene` and `fadeout` each past 10 s clamped to 10 s with a deviation note (a provisional interaction limit), and a field the skin's integer reader refuses refuses the import. A Lua decision skin whose load-time read reaches a pending fact (the judgment class) ends pending and stays refused at startup: the built-in presentation draws, and the select screen's notice says why.
  - **Result** (S10-09g to S10-09i):

    | Group | Ids | Treatment |

    | Chart | numeric 74, 90–91, 96, 368, 1163–1164; text 10, 12, 14, 16; chart options 150–155, 160–164, 177, 180–184 | Needed from the loaded chart and its presentation data, where a fact backs it |
    | Current result and timing | numeric 71, 75–76, 102–103, 107, 110–114, 171, 183–184, 372–375, 407, 410–425 | Needed: exact statistics from the recorded events under the viewed preset; 374–375's mean from exact deviation sums and counts, which the histogram cannot give; best-based rates under validity guards |
    | Own best and change | numeric 150, 152, 154, 170, 172, 176, 178, 370–371; options 90–91, 300–307, 320–327, 330, 332, 335, 2244–2245; image indices 370–371 | Needed, by exact comparison of the same saved play's before and after bests |
    | Widgets | `gaugegraph`, `judgegraph` types 1 and 2, `timingdistributiongraph` | Needed (R6, S10-09h). `judgegraph` types 1 and 2 are imported (S10-09h-2) from JSON and Lua alike, with `backTexOff`, `delay` (500 by default), `orderReverse`, `noGap` and `noGapX`, a flag on only at 1; type 0 (the note distribution) and any other type drop with a note. Its destination keeps its track, timer, visibility, blend, colour, alpha and angle, and its stretch fits the whole graph as the source fits a texture of 5 texels a bin and a cell (every mode, the trimmed ones cut to the rect); a `filter` is ignored with a note. An id a `judgegraph` shares with a later common table (`bpmgraph`, `hiterrorvisualizer`, `timingvisualizer`, `timingdistributiongraph`, then the `gauge`) is that table's, as the source's lookup does not stop at a judge graph. A `gaugegraph` and a `timingdistributiongraph` are imported (S10-09h-1) from JSON and Lua alike: the gauge graph with its 24-entry `color` array (a missing entry `000000`) or its 14 named colours, assigned per look as the source assigns them; the distribution with `width / lineWidth` columns (`width` 301 and `lineWidth` 1 by default, `lineWidth` clamped to `1..=width`), its eight colours and `drawAverage` and `drawDev` (on only at 1), dropped with a note past 4 096 columns. A colour is an optional `#` and exactly 6 or 8 hex digits; any other draws opaque red with a note, the graph kept. Both keep their destination's track, timer, visibility, blend, colour, alpha and angle and ignore a `filter` with a note; a distribution's stretch fits it as the judge graph's does, against its `gx` × scale texture, and a gauge graph's texture is its own rect, so its stretch changes nothing. |
    | Lane options | image indices 42 and 450–457, `main_state.event_index(42)`; event 42 | 0, as the source shows with no lane option or shuffle; event 42 is a select-screen action that does nothing on the result, so it has no hit target |
