    The importer's profile (S10-09g, `skin-beatoraja`'s `import::result::profile`) classifies every id the surveyed result skins read, one explicit `match` per id space, over the result schema (`skin_model::RESULT`); the result loader reads the common objects alone. A JSON field's `ref`, name or `op`, a Lua callback's `main_state` read and a Lua read while the skin loads go through the same function. Each value is shown, hidden or unavailable: a bridge is an expression and the validity it is drawn under, joined into the visibility of every element that reads it, outside any negation or callback expression, so an unavailable value draws nothing and neither does a negated option on it (`-90` on a view without a result); a hidden value (the BP and its difference with no previous BP, the source's `Integer.MIN_VALUE`) reads `i32::MIN` in every expression, which only a number does not draw. Classes: **N** bridged from the viewed result, its timing statistics, the best before this play or the chart's facts; **D** a truthful constant for a feature tapestry lacks, or omitted with a note; **configured** the skin's own options, folded.

    Essential parts, judged after the configured options are folded and over the retained elements; each missing refuses the import with `ImportError::Essential`, and an element of one that cannot be reproduced refuses it too, where any other is dropped with a note:
    - **EX score**: a number showing 71 or 171, by `ref`, name or a Lua read; an image set choosing by them is not one.
    - **Clear status**: an element whose visibility reads option 90 or 91, or that shows image index or number 370 (an image set, an image group or a number). An element that only reads 90 or 91 in its visibility (a background, a stage image) counts when retained but is dropped with a note when it cannot be reproduced, as the source's bundled result skin conditions an optional stage image on them; the import refuses only when no status remains. One showing 370 that cannot be reproduced refuses.

    While a Lua result skin loads, its reads of result values answer from the viewed result through the same profile: options 50 and 51 the offline constants; a shown value itself, a hidden one `i32::MIN`, and an unavailable one refusing the pass (an option is unavailable without its group's validity). Each read is recorded with its value, and the base snapshot has the result pending. The player imports the result skin and specializes it (S10-09g: images in Preparing, the layout after the save and on each switch), publishing each outcome on the CPU; until S10-09i draws result skins, the built-in result screen is what shows.

    The native strip of design.md "Play Lifecycle" (a result skin) draws over a result skin only while a fact the skin cannot express applies (S10-09i); otherwise the skin owns the whole screen. The result's outro, which retry, back and the quit key wait for, is the skin's declared one: the importer reads its top-level `fadeout`, `input` and `scene` in ms, JSON and Lua alike, as µs (`Skin::result_timing`): an absent or negative value 0, a `scene` of 0 or less none, and a `fadeout` past 10 s clamped to 10 s with a deviation note (a provisional interaction limit); a field the skin's integer reader refuses (`null`, a string that is no number, a value past `i32`) refuses the import, as any integer field of a skin does. The flow honours them as design.md "State Model" (Result) says (S10-09i).
  - **Text limits.** R6's 64 text elements stay: a surveyed selection skin declares 65 text definitions but draws at most 64, and at most 39 once its decorative texts drop (S10-09d's measurement). Visible-row expansion does not count against it: the song list's row text is its own table, one line per placement, at most 60.
- **The conversion report** tool runs the importer over a set of skins on the developer's PC (none are in the repository) and lists what did not convert. It decides which expressions and patterns to add. It is developer-only: a binary inside `skin-beatoraja` with `required-features = ["dev-tools"]`, since `xtask` must not depend on this GPL crate. It prints only allowlisted API, construct and reason counts, outcomes and numeric locations under anonymous skin and module indices, never source, generated definitions, captures, expressions, private names, paths or raw Lua errors; mapping a location back to its file stays local and transient. The one exception is its explicit `--local` flag, under which each callback record also names its module file relative to the skin root, its element's kind, destination index and `id`, and a lowered callback's generated expression, its generated guards and whether the expression is a constant, and the report adds the options and file choices as resolved, by the names the skin gives them, and one line per destination in the skin's list, with its index, `id`, the kind its `id` names, what the import made of it (retained with its elements, folded, dropped, ignored, no element, refused, not reached), and its notes with their files and locations: that output stays on the developer's PC and is never pasted into the repository, issues or PRs.
- **Fixtures** in the repository are small self-made JSON and Lua skins; private skins are never fixtures or fuzz inputs. That a real skin plays is checked on the developer's PC. The Lua fixtures cover header and configuration divergence, local modules, selected parts and pass isolation; the critical gauge callback and pure helper and capture lowering; fractional division, zero divisors, negative modulo, truthiness, coercions and subtypes; shared against independent observers, and a latch that must not become `when`; decorative freezing and the refusal of a critical value, visibility, timing or side effect; generated-table cycles, alias expansion, sparse arrays and every cap; and infinite loops, exhaustion inside protected calls, recursion, memory exhaustion and an attempted coroutine escape.
- **`skin_lua`**, a fuzz target of its own from S7-02's first executable support, extended with each lowering feature: it reuses `skin_json`'s drawing and usage assertions on a successful import and keeps malformed Lua, configuration variation, module confinement, pass isolation and closure analysis visible as its own boundary, wired into the seeds, smoke tests, dictionary, runner and scheduled fuzzing.
- **Lua evidence.** Memory is witnessed over the source and AST, each VM, captures, conversion, the common import and teardown, with their overlaps and failure paths; a positive control proves the allocation counter sees VM allocation, and process memory is recorded too. The JSON bound (153 286 620 bytes) is not Lua evidence. The header pass, configured execution, analysis and common import are benchmarked apart, with target, toolchain and build conditions and the refusal latency at each cap. The model's first and later play and loading builds, observer transitions, refusal and rearm allocate nothing, Lua being destroyed, or a non-play screen's VM quiesced, before those spans; a play following a Lua-driven selection screen allocates nothing (S7-07).

## 4. The authored format (`skin-toml`)

**Concepts an author learns,** each with its payoff:

| Concept | What it gives the author |
| --- | --- |
| image | A file with optional grid frames, a frame cycle and its own timer (R2), as authors make sprite sheets today |
| element | One placement model for images, numbers, text, bars and widgets, in reference pixels |
| region | 21:9 support and player adjustment, by tagging HUD elements (the playfield and judgment are automatic) |
| expression | Conditions and values by name (`combo >= 100`) instead of numeric IDs |
| timer | What starts an animation: a built-in event or `when(<condition>)` |
| keyframes | Animation, with the semantics of R2 |
| option | Variants the player picks, folded at load |

`per_lane = true` repeats an element over the lanes with the lane's x and width; it is an attribute, not a concept. Cut: generic templates, includes, file choosers, free offsets, expressions that move or color things, subtract and multiply blends.

**Files.** `skin.toml` (TOML 1.1 through `toml` with `serde_spanned`) beside the images. `[meta]` (name, author, version, format version), `[options.*]`, `[images.*]`, then elements in draw order. A file of another format version is refused (AGENTS.md). Errors carry spans and are rendered with `annotate-snippets`: unknown names with a suggestion, type and range errors, and export warnings. KDL reads better for trees but has no maintained derive layer; YAML's implicit typing hurts hand authors.

**How the export always works:**
1. A construct enters the format only together with its export rule.
2. Expressions decide only visibility, numbers, text, frame index and bar ratio; geometry and color come from keyframes and regions, because beatoraja's keyframe geometry is plain integers and takes no Lua.
3. Expressions stay in a Lua-translatable subset (R4); division, remainder and conditionals become prelude functions (a conditional because `c and a or b` breaks when `a` is false).
4. Every state field states its beatoraja mapping or is tapestry-only. A decorative element that reads a tapestry-only field is left out of the export and listed in the report; `--strict` fails instead. The elements the play depends on (the lanes, judgment and gauge widgets, and the score and combo numbers) may depend only on exportable fields, in every option configuration: `check` refuses a skin that breaks this, so `build` never writes an export without notes. A fixture tests the refusal.

## 5. The beatoraja exporter (`skin-export`) and the CLI (`skin-cli`)

- **Target:** a generated `.luaskin` at exactly 1920×1080 (a test asserts the size: an unlisted size silently falls back to 1280×720 in both loaders), using only the fields and runtime API that upstream and ED share. Conditions are `draw` Lua strings, because ED's `op` takes integers only. The loaders compile each such string on its own in the skin's global environment, so the prelude helpers are installed as globals with a reserved prefix, not as locals of the generated file. A flag for upstream-only fields can come later. JSON was rejected: it cannot compute geometry and expands long.
- **Regions** become one user offset per region. The Lua reads the configured values at load (ED exports them before the configured pass, `LuaSkinLoader`, `SkinLuaAccessor`) and bakes each region's transform into its elements' keyframes; no destination offset applies them a second time. The built-in offsets are used only where their meaning matches: lift and lane cover for the covers. The whole-skin offset scales the lanes and BGA too, so it does not stand in for a HUD region.
- **Text:** literal text is baked into images at 1x. Chart-bound text exports as `text` plus `font`, copying the author's font file, which must be redistributable and cover the needed glyphs: `size` is the largest key `h`, `Shrink` is `overflow` 1 and `Spill` 0, and the destination x moves right by `w·{0, ½, 1}` for left, centre and right. Chart-bound text in the default font cannot ship a redistributable font, so it is left out with a report line. A title cannot be baked into a reusable skin.
- **Judgments and gauges** map by a table: tapestry's grades, empty POOR and the preset's gauges to beatoraja's grades and gauge types; a gauge with no counterpart maps to the nearest type with a report line. A `gauge.kind` comparison is remapped explicitly onto the target's gauge types, never exported as the enum's storage, and one the target cannot express is reported.
- **Score rates** export by recomputing the guarded integer formula of R3 from the target's own score and note counts, never by rebuilding basis points from its rounded rate properties (102 and 103, 115 and 116); a rate expression the target cannot compute that way is reported.
- **Widgets** export as the target's `value`, `judge` and `gauge`, and what differs is reported:
  - Numbers: a `Ten` image whose frame count is a multiple of 24 is rewritten, since the target tries 24 first: a cycled strip is split, keeping its set boundaries and wrap, and padding is allowed only for one set or no cycle. `Eleven` with blank or zero padding is reported (the target forces glyph padding) or rewritten to `Ten`. The target hides `i32::MIN` and `i32::MAX`, which is reported.
  - Judgment displays: a `TwentyFour` combo is not exportable (the target's judge takes 10 or 11 glyphs) and is reported. A `Bottom` combo exports directly; a `Top` combo exports only where, per effective state (each slot and the full-gauge PERFECT, grade and combo resolved), every grade key has one height, giving `combo y = grade h − cy − combo h` per key; when a fallback-shared `Top` combo needs different offsets in different states, separate combo entries are written (grade 0 of height 80 and grade 6 of height 100 sharing a `Top` combo at y 10, h 20 give offsets 50 and 70). Otherwise it is reported as not representable.
  - Gauges: a part with an animated image or a non-zero angle is reported (or baked to frame 0 and angle 0 with a report line). Each motion exports as the target type of its name, `Still` as random with range 0, and the report states the motion differences (R6).
- **What degrades:** aspect (16:9 only), the side inset, mipmaps and @2x (the 1x image), nine-slice (baked per size, edges rounded so seams do not open).
- **CLI `tapestry-skin`:** `check`; `preview`, which draws the compiled quads with `tiny-skia` into PNGs at 16:9 and 21:9 for a fixture state, so authors preview on macOS and Linux; `export beatoraja`; `build`, which does all three in one command; `watch`, which polls file times as preset files are polled (design.md "Tuning Without Restart"). The preview lives in the CLI, so the player does not link it.
- **In the player** a changed skin is reloaded between plays, built off-thread; a failed reload keeps the current skin, and nothing is polled during play.

## 6. Crates, render, tests and PRs

### Crates

| Crate | Contents |
| --- | --- |
| `skin-model` | The runtime model, state schema, expressions, tracks, timers and reducer, regions, widgets, text requests and font files, scroll math, atlas pages and mips, `Quad` and `QuadBatch` |
| `skin-beatoraja` | The JSON and Lua importer |
| `skin-toml` | The authored format, its compiler and diagnostics |
| `skin-export` | The beatoraja writer, from the published format only |
| `skin-cli` | The authoring CLI and its preview |

All are `GPL-3.0-or-later` (design.md "Licenses") and portable: each goes into `REQUIRED_PORTABLE`, the baseline workspace of `xtask/src/fixture.rs`, the license table of `check-arch`, and its in-play functions into `IN_PLAY`. A skin that tapestry exports is the author's work and data, not a derivative of tapestry.

### Render integration

- `SpriteBatch` is one ordered list with runs broken at each blend or clip change, drawn in order with a pipeline per blend (S10-01) and each run under its clip's scissor (S10-02). The clips are the skin's fixed table (`render::ClipTable`), installed with its atlas; their scissors are projected once per target size (design.md "Rendering and Skins", D1), an unclipped run sets the whole target back, and a clip that keeps no pixel draws nothing. A `QuadBatch` run may also carry an inline clip (`Run::clip_rect`, a cover paint's trim, R6) in canvas pixels: render draws the run under the intersection of its fixed clip and that rectangle (S10-05), projected with D1's rounding and clamped to the target as a fixed clip is, an empty intersection drawing nothing and the next unclipped run setting the whole target back. Imported destination `clip_*` fields stay outside this.
- A sprite is staged into a 64-byte `Instance`, converted field by field: rect, uv, colour, the quad's pivot and angle, turned in the vertex shader, and the atlas layer and filter packed into flags (S10-01). The atlas is a texture array of one page side (S10-02), the bitmap font at the top left of reserved layer 0, always read at its nearest level-0 texel by integer texel (its own uv times 128), whatever the page side, and the skin's pages after it (R7), uploaded with their mips as `Rgba8Unorm`, then the skin's text layer when it has rasterized text (S10-08a), with a linear and a nearest sampler chosen per quad. `Renderer::atlas_limits` gives `AtlasLimits` from `Device::limits()`; `Renderer::install_atlas` checks the pages before allocating and keeps the atlas and clips in use when it refuses them. Planned for S10-03: the uv table and the uploaded pages have one owner, and the memory budget counts the old atlas alive during a reload and the upload staging.
- Premultiplied alpha throughout the sprite pipelines, the bitmap font and `text_screen` included (S10-01): staging multiplies a tint's RGB by its alpha once; alpha blending is RGB 1, 1 − source alpha, additive RGB 1, 1, and the alpha channel composites over in both. Multiply (S6e) is a third pipeline: its fragment divides the premultiplied colour by its alpha, and blending is RGB 0, source colour, the alpha channel kept. Menu text keeps its straight colour (`TextStyle.color`), blended by glyphon.
- The canvas projection replaces the letterbox for play frames (S10-02): `PlaySkin::canvas` places the skin's canvas on the target as the viewport, built-in menus and results keep the fixed letterboxed canvas (a skinned selection, decision or result screen uses its own compiled canvas, S10-09b), and `FrameInput::target` carries the size the renderer applied. The window's size reaches the render thread through a slot of the newest size, applied between frames by `Renderer::resize`; a minimized window presents nothing until a nonzero size comes back (design.md "Rendering and Skins").
- The screen's recovery and an attempt's preparation are separate (S10-03a, design.md "Play Lifecycle", preparing each attempt). `RenderMsg::Prepare` builds a failed screen again, without a chart, and prepares nothing; `RenderMsg::PrepareAttempt` carries the owned `AttemptContext`, from which the render thread makes the borrowed `PlayContext` for the one call `Screen::prepare_attempt`, and answers `Preparation::Ready`, `Preparation::Refused` (a skin's refusal while the device is healthy: the screen is kept and no failure is reported) or an error (the device or the surface: the screen is dropped, and the next recovery builds it again). The game thread waits for the answer in 10 ms slices within the render answer bound, and a quit key, a close or a failure of the input or audio thread stops the wait, the failure then ending the run with its cause. `RenderMsg::EndLoading` answers for the loading lifetime it ends whether the screen is still there and whether the skin refused a loading frame of it: `ScreenError::Lost` refuses the opening when a loading frame dropped the screen after the preparation, and `ScreenError::LoadingRefused` when the skin refused one (S10-03c-2); the game also checks the input thread, the audio output and the screen just before recording opens. Each loading page travels with its `LoadingUpdate` in the same slot and loading lifetime.
- One batch for every play skin (S10-03b-1): `SpriteBatch` is the runtime model's `QuadBatch`, which `SimpleSkin` pushes into and a model skin writes into directly, with nothing copied through per-blend lists; after the skin's sprites the staging appends the measurement overlay from a reserve of 1 024 sprites in one run that the skin cannot fill. `PlaySkin::build`, `Renderer::build` and `Screen::build` return `Result<(), skin_model::BuildError>`: a refused frame submits nothing, the renderer builds that attempt no more until the next attempt's preparation, and the render thread stops the play as a screen failure while keeping the screen, its atlas, clips and device for the next preparation; device and surface failures keep the screen-dropping recovery (design.md "Rendering and Skins", refused frames).
- The declarative skin on the play screen (S10-03b-2, design.md "Rendering and Skins", declarative skin installation): `PlaySkin::prepare` takes the attempt (`render::AttemptInput`: the chart, the borrowed `PlayContext`, the five chart labels and the applied target) and `PlaySkin::load_chart` a chart's load, which prepares nothing preset-dependent. `render::SkinBundle` is the immutable CPU description of an installation, checked when made against its skin's frames, fixed clips and the renderer's reserved layers (`render::BundleError`), and `render::DeclSkin` the `PlaySkin` drawing it, a fresh `Play` per attempt with its bitmap lines and the text an `AttemptText` producer prepares; `render::skin_frame` is the frame adapter. `Renderer::stage` uploads and validates a bundle's atlas, clips, staging and instance buffer beside the installation in use and prepares the fresh attempt before it returns a `PreparedToken` (request and device generation); `Renderer::commit` swaps them together and refuses another request's or device's token; `Renderer::discard` and a refusal drop them whole; `Renderer::restore` installs a committed bundle on a renderer built again; `Renderer::memory` witnesses the peak, both attempts' state included. A device refusal of the staged uploads or instance buffer is a refusal inside one error scope. On the render thread, `RenderMsg::PrepareAttempt` carries the link's request number and is answered between frames (`Screen::prepare_attempt` returns `None` until it answers), `RenderMsg::Commit` and `RenderMsg::Discard` finish or drop it (`RenderLink::commit_attempt`, `discard_attempt`, behind `Rig::commit_attempt` and `Rig::discard_attempt`), a commit the link stopped waiting for being withdrawn so it never installs later, and the committed bundle is kept apart from the screen for `Screen::restore`. An unconfigured play screen draws `SimpleSkin`, whose preparation stages nothing, so its commit only checks the device; a play skin configured in `player.toml` is selected as a declarative bundle at startup (`RenderLink::select`, S10-07b; design.md "Rendering and Skins").
- The loading presentation (S10-03c, design.md "Rendering and Skins", loading state and declarative skin installation). The model's loading runtime is portable (S10-03c-1, R3). Render draws it (S10-03c-2): `render::LoadingFrame` (the loading lifetime, `now`, `LoadState`, the target, the layout, the lanes, the covers, the gauge descriptor and the text revision) goes to `PlaySkin::build_loading`, which returns `Result<(), BuildError>`; `SimpleSkin` draws its lanes, judgment line and gauge frame from it as before and never refuses; `DeclSkin` builds its installation's `render::LoadingPresentation`, never an attempt's `Play`. A `render::Installation` is a bundle with its presentation, staged, committed, discarded and restored together; staging the installed bundle, an attempt's included, keeps the presentation, another bundle gets a fresh one, and a new loading lifetime starts one that belonged to another lifetime afresh, drawn or not. Loading text installs through `Renderer::install_loading_text` under a `render::LoadingTextKey` (`Renderer::loading_text_key`: the installed presentation, the renderer and its resource generation), and a key from another installation, renderer or resource generation installs nothing (`LoadingTextError::Stale`). `Renderer::install` stages a bundle's chart-independent resources without an attempt under the same request and device generations as `Renderer::stage`, and the renderer refuses a staging or commit older than one staged or committed (`RenderError::Superseded`, `CommitError::Superseded`). `Staging::build_loading` clears the batch, the overlay's reserve included, on a refusal; `Renderer::loading_frame` then submits nothing and builds that lifetime no more, and the player's `Screen::loading` returns the refusal apart from `RenderError`, so the screen stays and the end of loading reports it. `MemoryWitness` counts the installed and staged presentations (`loading`, `staged_loading`), the recovery copy being the installed one, shared. On the render thread a selection is a request (`RenderMsg::Select`) answered ready, refused or failed, polled by `PendingSelect` and withdrawn when cancelled, timed out or dropped, the commit checking the withdrawal; the committed `Installation` is restored on a screen built again. The player admits a chart choice (`Selection::admit`, in `PlayerRig::look` before the choice's side effects) and a practice Start (`AppRig::start_ready`) only while `SkinState` is ready, a refusal showing its located reason and admitting nothing. The player bridges each lifetime into `LoadingFrame` (S10-03c-3): `RenderLink::begin_loading`, at a chart's choice and a practice Start, stamps the lifetime's origin once on the clock the game and render threads share and starts its state (`player::loading_state::LifetimeState`) on the producers' side; typed inputs (`LoadInput`: the stage, the bank's completion, readiness after the last checks and before the commit, the descriptor refreshed with the preset by `begin_play`, the chart's fields) each publish the whole state numbered (`LoadingUpdate`), which the slot and then the render thread take only when newer. The progress is 0 at the start, `⌊90 · min(done, files) / files⌋` of a known sound count, kept by an unknown count and every other stage, 90 at the bank's completion and 100 only at readiness. The descriptor comes first from the preset selection validated (`SelectScreen::bests_preset`), `None` without one. Inside each loading frame's call, before the frame is built, `MenuScreen::loading` brings the chosen chart's text to the lifetime's revision through the installation's producer (`Renderer::prepare_loading_text`, staged, installed at the next frame by `Renderer::install_staged_loading_text` after its key, lifetime and revision are checked; at most one table staged, counted as `MemoryWitness::staged_text`), every slot cleared and every field asked for another lifetime, installation or size or after a refusal, and only the changed fields otherwise, their slots cleared until the replacement; a refused request is not asked again. The covers stay interim until S10-04a/04b: the author's layout, covers 0 and off, not adjusting, and the tuning snapshot's lane time read once per frame.

### Tests

**macOS and Linux:**
- Expressions: unit tests, range and bound errors, and a property test against a reference interpreter.
- Tracks: the endpoint table of R2.
- The reducer: effect scripts split across drains at every point, long-note feedback, `failed_at`, retry.
- `lanes`: `SimpleSkin`'s long-note, culling and density tests ported with their literal values unchanged; the scroll copy against a reference copy of render's `Scale` (equal bits) with a drift guard on render's source, removed in S14.
- Golden quad lists and preview PNGs at 16:9, 21:9 and 16:10 with a bundled test font, a resize of one prepared play, adjusted regions, an alpha/add/alpha overlap that fails if blend order is lost, and two same-blend elements with different clips followed by an unclipped one, checked by expected pixels.
- `text`: the load checks and the font file checks at their bounds, the requests (the fields' join, cleaning and cutting, the em), the geometry table with overhanging ink, and a property over valid lines (a squeezed line inside its box, a spilled one at its width, each edge on the box).
- An allocation test around `build` on a dense chart, in dev and release (design.md "Scope of No Allocation"), with every follower kind under changing cover amounts, timer-off lanes, a resize and both scratch sides, both rates and gauge-kind conditions in `when` timers under both empty-POOR policies, a refused inconsistent snapshot, and the play readouts known and unknown through a STOP, background sounds, READY and two rounds each of a full play and a section, each round prepared outside the count, with refused and poisoned builds; and the source lanes drawn wholly with generated fallback paint, LN then CN, with holds, covers, every target and a half-size batch, then poisoned.
- Score rates and gauge kind: the arithmetic table (7 500 and 3 750 bp for EX 3 over 2 judged and 4 attempt targets, 1 950 for 39 over 100, the empty denominators, custom weights past 10 000, the largest counts), both empty-POOR policies on the real evaluator's snapshots, section targets, a snapshot ahead of its effects, `BuildError` with the poisoned `Play` and its reset at `prepare`, every look and the custom fallbacks, and an invalid shown gauge.
- Play readouts: the remaining time to a background sound after the last note (17 s, not 10 s); the clocks at 0, 1, 999 999, 1 000 000 and 1 000 001 µs (0, 1, 1, 1 and 2 s); a section's lead-in and the reset each round; a section of 10 s loaded at half speed counting from 20 s; the display offset and completion moving neither clock; the progress at READY, at the start, at 15 and 16 µs of a 16 s attempt (0 and 1 ppm), halfway, at its end and past it, over a half-speed section and an empty one, at 0 while loading, and a slider of each direction moved by it, also through `Logic` with manual and autoplay input, a practice speed, a rearm and a retry; readouts supplied through a STOP; configured covers of 500 ‰ and 250 ‰, both off, showing 500, 250 and 375 while the lanes paint nothing; a switch on at 0; durations of 667.5 ms (668 and 400) and 600 ms (600 and 360); a known duration with the hi-speed unknown; inverted ranges refused by `prepare`; and each invalid frame refused with `InvalidReadouts`, the `Play` poisoned until `prepare`. In the importer: each mapping by number and name, the guards joined before compiling, 15, 131 and 92, the cover conditions, and the hidden cover's negated 273.
- Followers: this frame's geometry at fixed amounts (Source and Simple lanes, fractional and capped), the transform order under scratch side, lane width, region scale, pivot and clip, placement identical to a skin that follows nothing under region scale and the clamp, and grades carrying their combos once.
- Cover trim: at H = 600 with lift 300 ‰ and HIDDEN+ 500 ‰, LIFT's paint up 180 px and HIDDEN+'s 390 px, a linked base of 800 at 620; repeated implicit offsets moving once, an extra 4 adding SUDDEN+'s slide once; a raw `[100, 110]` fitted to `[55, 155]` drawing nothing at trim 90, and under stretch 9 a 32×32 paint over `[0, 100, 0, 10]` drawing nothing at trim 105 and whole at 120; rotated and native-sized paint over nine pivots and three sizes keeping its quad under an upright clip; draw order across blends and clips; the coverage unchanged at 0 and 1 000 ‰ and with absent, transparent or timer-off paint; a disabled HIDDEN+ through exact keys, interpolation and steps; the load refusals; in the importer, every destination, the trim's conversion and rounding, the implicit offsets, op 273, the gate's charges, the drops and refusals, and the F10 memory fixture.
- Bar groups: travel at the display head and 100 px ahead, a `Lift` follower moving by both, a STOP holding the bars while their animation runs, READY with `ready` and `play` groups, groups differing in rect, tint, blend, timer and cycle drawn in chart bar then group order, tall, displaced, rotated and native-sized bars drawn whole and a touching one not, hidden and timer-off groups visiting nothing, the shared budgets running out with one drop, an exhaustive small-chart oracle against the heap's order and windows, the variant by binding and visibility, and the cap and its refusals; in the importer, every mapping and drop above, the charges before the reads, and a model error located at its group.
- Digit offsets: a cell `[100, 200, 20, 30]` moved to `[103, 189, 24, 36]` by the row `(3, 5, 4, 6)`, the same delta at 1280×720, under a region's scale, a lane binding and a follower; a 20×30 frame under stretch 9 fitted into the moved rect at `[105, 192, 20, 30]`; fixed cells under every alignment, signs, padding glyphs and blanks, a 13-digit value's visible digits reading rows 11 and 12; overlapping cells in cell order; zero offsets drawing as none; a delta that `f32` would round, added in `f64`; a combo's cells moved and its grade shifted by the baseline width, full-gauge fallbacks keeping their offsets; the logical box and the variant unchanged; the limits and the skin-wide count over unreferenced definitions (5,957 eleven-offset strips and one of nine), an undrawn invalid strip counted and not checked; no allocation in builds with offsets; in the importer, the reflection and the unscaled delta, wrappers inserting and removing rows, every refusal located at its row, short tables and trailing zero rows, the aspect check of numbers and combos, both caps, and memory fixtures of a 65,536-row table (alone and near the node cap), a million empty tables costing no more than an unknown field, and 65,536 one-row tables.
- Grade attachment anchors: a square grade fitted inside `[100, 500, 80, 20]` painting `[130, 500, 20, 20]` with "12" in three centred 10 px cells at x 100 and 110 on y 500, its paint shifted to 120; the grade's attachment and the combo each on its own clock through every ease and end; both full-gauge fallbacks; a follower moving paint and combo once; native sizing, rotation and a region's scale changing the paint alone; the logical box from the attachment's corner without its own box; attachment timing, coordinates and follow ranges refused on referenced judges and unchecked on unreferenced ones whose keys still count; no allocation in builds with attachments; in the importer, stretches 1, 2, 4 and 6 attaching to the unstretched destination, timed attachments looping with their grade, a static offset, edges rounded on their own at 1280×720, no attachment for an unpaired, never-drawn-combo, edge-keeping or native grade, trimmed and branch-changing stretches refused; memory fixtures F14a (8,192 judges at the key and widget quad caps, 24,576 attachment keys, and a key more refused at the last grade) and F14b (16,384 empty judge definitions).
- Large gauges: 16 384 still segments and 16 383 flickering ones with their tip at the widget budget, 16 384 flickering ones refused on it; two elements of one 8 191-part flickering definition, one never visible, reserving the budget whole and a third refused; 5 000 000 and 5 000 611 of 10 000 000 lighting 8 192 and 8 193, the `i64` extremes, the border met exactly and a maximum dividing no count; a fall over every segment wrapping at 16 385 steps and repeating at a repeated time; narrow, empty and negative widths finite; a short batch keeping the ordered prefix; no allocation in play and loading builds at the budget; in the importer, parts up to 16 384 with one D10 note, 16 385 refused, the flicker past the budget refused at its destination and a range of 0 fitting, exact scalars, a node array refused from its count before its ids are read (100 000 entries collecting no list), the quads charged before an unresolved node after the destination's own checks; memory fixtures F15a–F15c and their budget refusals.
- Note order: both styles in `LaneMajor`, the source timeline order and the reversed one over coincident keys, each frame's notes in the order's sequence and in no other's, the same notes in every order; an eight-lane chord in each order's ranks; a long note's parts together before a later tap on an earlier lane; 8 800 taps past the quad budget keeping the order's prefix with one drop; a long note cut after its body, or its body and tail, and the budget's taps exactly with no drop; duplicate and out-of-range orders refused; no allocation in timeline builds over overlapping lanes, held notes, a STOP, a section, no scroll left and short batches; in the importer, overlapping, coincident and unsorted keys imported with their rects and the lanes' usual reports, a chord painted keys 1 to 7 then the scratch, an earlier note under a later one on an overlapping lane, and a scratch reaching a key refused; memory fixtures F16a (the ordered prefix past the quad budget) and F16b (the visits spent exactly, then one drop), and F5's overlapping key and two chords in the source's order.
- Importer: self-made fixtures, and the sandbox tests of §3.
- Export: both load passes, run under a Lua stub with and without `skin_config`; the generated properties executed against known states, checking values and visibility as well as geometry, with stated pixel and millisecond tolerances; the export size.
- Fuzz targets for the skin loader, which also build each import's loading state (S10-03c-1): `skin_json`, and `skin_lua` with S7 (§3).
- The loading runtime (S10-03c-1): `loading` and `screen` from the origin (250 ms at `now − since = 250 000 µs`) with every other built-in off and the progress clamped; a structural skin drawing its background, the judgment line where a play draws it and the gauge's empty frame on either side of its border, with nothing on `ready`, no notes, bars or judge; an animation, a condition, a number and a `when` on the loading clock and the progress through unchanged and changed progress; programs reading the tally, score, combo, gauge value or tempo, in either arm, drawing nothing and starting no `when`; descriptor programs from the frame the descriptor arrives, negations drawing nothing without one and a started `when` clearing; text slots cleared with their composites and installed again with no timer restarted; a refused state building no more; a play keeping `loading` off and `load.percent` at 100. Imported skins through it: untimed destinations and cycles on `screen` while those on `ready` stay off, and in a play again from `ready`; the gauge-type conditions, image index 40 and the gauge's empty frame following the descriptor.
- The attempt's commit (S10-03b-2) on scripted rigs: after the attempt and a round's restore check and before the end of loading, for the first play, a retry and a practice round without a loading state alike; never after a refused, cancelled or failed preparation, which is discarded instead.
- The play lifecycle (S10-03a) on scripted rigs: each attempt prepared after the preset's refresh and before loading ends; a retry and a practice's first round using a preset edited before them; a refusal returning to selection or the practice page, which stay usable, with the attempt's number given back; a round's initial gauges following their gauges by ID through a reordered preset with a gauge added, and the page showing that gauge and value for the next Start, which plays them; a Start's first round, a repeat and a retry not opening on a reset of a value set (a set gauge lost after its start moved off the 1 % steps, too), their number given back and the page showing the reset and stating it, the next Start playing it; a sync-only edit keeping the repeat; a reset staying reset when the rule is restored before the next Start; a preset repaired on the page playing at the next Start; a reset stated beside a section refusal of the same edit; a gauge moved and then lost giving way, on the page, to the gauge the reset names; a refusal giving way to an input or audio failure that came with it; a cancellation, during a slow preparation too, never opening recording; an input, audio or screen failure after the preparation, or one that stopped it, opening nothing and starting no play; a failed opening giving its number back; each way of playing's note end; fresh attempt data each play; and `playing` on READY, play, tail, stopped and gauge-failure steps.

**Windows:** the loading presentation (S10-03c-2) on WARP: a declarative fixture's `loading`-timed animation advancing while the load stalls, over its untimed background; `SimpleSkin`'s loading pixels unchanged; a refused loading build submitting nothing and building that lifetime no more, the next lifetime drawing again; `when` starts kept by the same bundle's attempt staging and restoration and fresh for another bundle or a new lifetime; text installed for a lifetime that never drew not reaching the next; a selection and an attempt keeping the newer request; a failed replacement keeping the old bundle's loading frames; loading frames following a resize and a minimized window; a replacement's memory counting both presentations; loading text installing only under the key of the installation in use, kept by the same bundle's attempt and stale after another bundle's commit or a restoration; `render --test alloc` with a committed presentation's first, later and refused loading builds. On the render thread and in the player: a selection cancelled or timed out never installing when it completes, a newer selection discarding an older attempt, a refused selection keeping the installation before it, a refused loading frame refusing the end of loading, a loading lifetime's origin and `when` start outliving a renderer built again (a real menu screen, its files deleted) and the next lifetime starting afresh, a minimized window's loading frames and handshakes, and a chart admitted only once its skin is installed. The bridge (S10-03c-3), through the render thread, a real menu screen on WARP and a declarative fixture: B chosen after A's lifetime, its load stalled at 4 of 9 sounds, drawing its background, its animation on B's own clock, its `when` start once, the judgment line, the gauge from its descriptor, B's title only and progress 40 throughout (`load.percent` reading 40, its timer started once and ageing), no play frame built; the descriptor refreshed in one frame and kept by later pages, a field's change clearing its slots on the frame it arrives and drawing the replacement from the next, an unavailable field cleared, the staged table counted until installed; one origin and `when` start across progress, a page, the same bundle's attempt, a resize and a renderer built again, a different bundle keeping the origin with a fresh `when` start and its text installed again, late reports of a cancelled lifetime drawing nothing, the next lifetime fresh, 100 at readiness and no loading frame after the end of loading's answer; the slot and the render thread taking only newer states, a coalesced bank completion still giving 90; `render --test alloc` with the descriptor and installed text; the staged text's bound and memory, and text of another bundle, an older revision or another lifetime refused (render tests). The render integration, `render --test alloc` with `DeclSkin`, scripted plays with no render allocations, and the WARP render of a fixture against its CPU preview. The player's attempt preparation (S10-03a): a refused attempt keeps the screen, reports no failure and the menus still draw, a device failure while preparing drops the screen for the recovery, each attempt prepares with its own section, a slow preparation stops when the player leaves or the input or audio thread fails, the end of loading reports a screen a loading frame dropped after the preparation, and typed loading updates reach the screen with their page and never from an ended lifetime. A refused frame submits nothing, the skin is built no more in that preparation, and the next preparation draws with the atlas and clips kept; the overlay draws over a skin that fills its batch; the render thread stops a play whose frame was refused without building or drawing it again, keeps the screen and counts no app-region allocation after the refusal; `render --test alloc` counts the fullest overlay, a refused frame and a full batch (S10-03b-1). The declarative skin (S10-03b-2) on WARP: a commit switching the skin, the uv table, the atlas, the clips and the capacities together, the installation in use drawing on through frames while another is staged; a staging refused by its text producer after its resources were made, dropped whole, the installation in use drawing the next frame; a commit refused by request and by device, each alone; a retry of the installed bundle with another preset (more gauges, empty POORs counted apart) reusing the resources with a fresh attempt after the first attempt's refused frames, its poisoned `Play` never built again; a committed bundle restored on another renderer with its image files gone; the peak memory of a replacement, both installations and the upload's staging, and what remains after a commit or a discard; the frame adapter reading one view; `render --test alloc` with a real prepared `DeclSkin` with clips and prepared text: its first and later frames with effects and changing gauges, a refused frame, a resize, and a full batch with the overlay kept whole, beside the simple skin's. On the render thread: an attempt installing only by its own commit, a superseded or discarded one never, a stale commit changing nothing; loading frames presented while a preparation is held unanswered, and a preparation cancelled or timed out while held never installing when it answers later; a screen built again restoring the very bundle committed and refusing a preparation of the failed screen at its commit by device; effects reaching the frame adapter in order across drains with the frame's own `playing`.

**On the developer's PC:** for every change to the format or the exporter, the standard skin's export loads and plays in pinned releases of upstream and ED (0.8.9 and 0.4.0 at the time of writing; the pins are bumped deliberately); for every change to the importer or to runtime semantics it relies on, the conversion report over the local skin set has no new failures (design.md "What Humans Verify").

The screen PRs keep each screen independent: neither decision nor result waits for selection. S10-07 gives every kind its production choice, import, packing and report at startup, but no non-play screen shows before its own data, widgets and player bridge exist. Foundation PRs (S10-09a to S10-09d, S10-09g, S10-09h) may land before S7; activating a real Lua skin of any kind may not, but for a Lua selection, decision or result skin whose import completes, activated by its screen's bridge (S7 row). S12 to S14 stay play-only, every non-play kind keeping its built-in screen beside its imports, and the screen work finishes before S12 starts the authored format.

S6c, in order (each a planned contract until its PR lands, except where marked done):

1. **Shared cover motion and followers** (S6c-01): the per-frame lane geometry (R3), `Follow` on images, numbers, gauges, text and grades (R6, "Followers"), offsets 3 and 4 mapped to it, D9 `CoverMotion`. Done for the model and importer.
2. **Score rate and gauge kind** (S6c-02): basis points, both denominators and the empty-denominator rule, the empty-POOR correction and `BuildError` (R3), `gauge.kind` and `gauge.valid` (R3, R4), numbers 102, 103, 115, 116, 155 and 156 and conditions 42, 43 and 1046 with D4, ref 40 resolved as an image index. Done for the model and importer.
3. **Play readouts** (S6c-03): the BPM range and current tempo, the clocks, the configured covers and their switches, the hi-speed and the duration with their validity, `PrepareError::InvalidSectionRange` and `InvalidBpmRange`, and `BuildError::InvalidReadouts` (R3, R4, R6); numbers 10, 14, 90, 91, 160–164 and 310–316 and conditions 270–273 with D4 (§3). Done for the model and importer; S10 supplies the inputs.
4. **Expression-selected images** (S6c-04): image choices with independent clocks, per-entry tracks and native sizes, and the entry cap (R6); image groups and image sets by value and image index, 40 and 330–332 with D4 (§3); selectors 500–507 with the lane visual states (fidelity item 5, R3). Done for the model and importer.
5. **Independent SUDDEN+ paint** (S6c-05): cover paints in the draw order with their own attributes, wider, split and above-lane, several of them, image selections among them, and a narrow `slider` type 4 without interaction; the paint independent of the logical coverage, which may reach 100 %; LIFT's and HIDDEN+'s paint as S6c-07 gives it, with the single reservation for the judgment line (R6, §3). Done for the model and importer.
6. **Bar groups** (S6c-06): bar line groups with their own colour, timer, geometry and placement, as many as the skin has up to `MAX_BAR_GROUPS`, drawn in chart bar then group order on the lanes' budgets through a bounded heap (R6); every `note.group` record mapped, its image's `len` and `ref` unused, its clip ignored, the D9 bar lifetime kept (§3). Done for the model and importer.
7. **Cover trim and paint** (S6c-07): LIFT's and HIDDEN+'s paint as independent elements, every destination with its own attributes, geometry and multiplicity, moved by its cover; the trim line gating the unstretched destination and clipping it at the line through an inline run clip, `LANE_RESERVED` 1 (R6); `disapearLine` converted exactly, the implicit offsets, op 273 compiled normally, D9 `HiddenDisable` (§3). Done for the model and importer.
8. **Optional note parts** (S6c-08): generated images in the sheet (R7); unresolved part ids drawn with the source's fallback colours, reported as `Fallback` and counted; malformed selected arrays, an unsized unresolved tap and broken resolved images refused; `size` read exactly by a bounded stream; tail-less LN kept with an unresolved end (§3, "Note parts"); memory fixture F11 and a `size` array near the node cap. Done for the model and importer.
9. **Digit offsets** (S6c-09): per-cell paint deltas on numbers and combo strips in target pixels, before native fitting and the pivot, on fixed cells, the baseline width kept for the grade's shift (R6); the importer's reflected rows, retained-cell indexing, strict rows, aspect check and both caps (§3, "Digit offsets"); memory fixtures F13a–F13d. Done for the model and importer.
10. **Grade attachment anchors** (S6c-10): separate grade paint from its combo attachment for accepted stretches 1/2/4/6, preserving the unstretched destination. Attachment follows the grade's clock and cover motion; the combo retains its own clock, and baseline-width shift moves grade paint only. Attachment keys share the skin-wide key limit, including unreferenced definitions; timing, coordinates and Follow ranges are checked for referenced judges. Preserve native 8/9, digit offsets, bounds and draw/clip order, with trimmed and branch-changing stretches still refused. Include F14a/F14b and allocation coverage. Done for the model and importer; no S10 dependency.
11. **Large gauges** (S6c-11): separate authored part count from the per-mille gauge field; allow up to 16,384 parts within the unchanged aggregate widget budget, reserving one additional quad for effective Flicker. Read gauge scalars exactly, bound node collection, and admit gauge quads after common destination checks and before node lookup or assets. Preserve exact raw-value arithmetic, deterministic motion, placement and draw order. Include F15a–F15c, allocation and fuzz coverage, and memory figures labelled by target. Model and importer scope; no new S10 inputs. Done for the model and importer.
12. **Overlapping key lanes** (S6c-12): preserve authored key-key overlap and coincidence, with scratch wholly outside every key. Add validated LaneMajor and Timeline note order; imported lanes merge resolved heads by position, time, source lane rank (native 1–7, then 0) and lane-local index. Use at most eight stack cursors within the unchanged shared visit and quad budgets, preserving long-note part grouping, bar/line order and native LaneMajor output. Exact original timeline identity remains S6c-13. Include F16a/F16b, both styles and order modes, allocation and fuzz coverage, and memory figures labelled by target. Model and importer production scope; no new S10 inputs. Done for the model and importer.
13. **Exact anchoring, per-lane note heights and tempo sidecar** (S6c-13): coordinated `bms-model`/`bms-parser` timeline identity, endpoint and parallel bar links, exact original tempo metadata and speed provenance, under one bounded construction charge and loader-version change. Keep base validity, structural presentation links and chart-wide endpoint compatibility separate; game admits only presentation-valid charts at their loaded speed, while analysis reads retain structurally valid charts. The portable skin uses every timeline bucket for exact shared note/bar/beat positioning and exact-ID Timeline ordering, with per-lane Source heights and existing traversal budgets. Include evaluator-preservation, admission, allocation, fuzz and target-labelled memory evidence. Deliver the reusable exact playhead, tempo data and practice bar IDs to S10-06; default-renderer adoption and production provider wiring remain there, before S10-04b activation. Done, in three PRs, each with its acceptance checks: (1) the model and parser metadata, builder, bounds, hashing and loader version 9, game admission, producer migrations and evaluator-preservation evidence (design.md "Chart Model", "Timeline"), done; (2) the shared exact playhead (`skin_model::ExactPlayhead`, public for S10-06), exact-ID ordering, beat integration and rearm/allocation integration, done; (3) per-lane heights, importer admission and reporting, and the final integrated memory, fuzz and completion evidence, done. `SimpleSkin` adopts the shared playhead in S10-06a, which closes https://github.com/shuymn/tapestry/issues/167; connecting the tempo provider and consuming the practice bars' timestamps from the 1× practice chart remain S10-06b's. No source behaviour is reproduced without a player-facing or skin-authoring purpose; the arithmetic stays exact.

Deferred beyond S6c: `dst2`, `expansionrate`, and the invert blend (its one consumer among the real skins used for testing is GenericTheme's load bar, graph 102, dropped for it in its 54 play combinations); blend 3 stays additive. The multiply blend (4) maps on images, image choices and player-drawn images (S6e, D23); on a text it is ignored (D5), on a play widget's outer destination ignored, and on any other object dropped (refused when critical): numbers are deferred, and graphs need their own mapping, since their empty areas are no quads where the source multiplies them.

Remaining beyond S6e, each taken when a real skin needs it (the limits rule): a trimmed stretch (3, 5, 7) whose keys change aspect or mix the fitting and cropping branches, dropped with its stretch note, or refused when critical; a source cut that is mirrored or starts left of or above its file, refused with `SourceRect` on a critical or essential element and dropped with that note elsewhere; blend 4 on numbers and graphs (above); and a slider `value` that returns a table or a function, which the source reads as 0, dropped as an unsupported type (refused when critical). The decide skins import and carry no S6e note; select.json still refuses for its song list, not for these.

S7, in order (§3, design.md "beatoraja Lua Skins"; each a planned contract until its PR lands). Every executable PR already meets the whole sandbox contract; S7-04 to S7-07 widen the inputs, the compiled behaviour and the evidence and repair no earlier unsafe support.

1. **The contract** (S7-01, docs only): design.md first ("beatoraja Lua Skins", the import's placement in "State Model" and "Load Pipeline", "Threat Model"'s Lua row with the confined `dofile`), then §3, this table and the order against S10. **Gate:** When the contract is compared with the approved plan and follow-up, design.md and stage3.md shall agree on placement, limits, refusal rules, scope and landing order, with no contradictory retained wording or private skin content.
2. **Bounded Lua loading and common conversion** (S7-02): the vendored VM, the two fresh passes, the `skin_config` bridge, local `require` and the confined `dofile`, data-only Lua skins through the common mapper, typed callables, every one a located refusal at its definition wherever it is (acceptance, dependency and effect classification and typed fallbacks are S7-03's), the cancellation checks and thread-crossing results, the developer report and the first `skin_lua` target, with the confinement, exhaustion and memory tests in this PR. **Gate:** When a cap would be exceeded, cancellation is observed, or a confinement escape is attempted, including inside a protected call, the import shall refuse with its located reason and return no candidate.
3. **Exact callbacks and the gauge bridge** (S7-03): AST captures, bounded pure-helper lowering with its acceptance proofs, the existing property and timer bridges and `main_state.gauge_type`. **Gate:** When an ECFN default configuration is imported on the owner's PC, the import shall succeed; when model frames vary the gauge value and supported gauge kinds, the gauge output shall match the mapped fill and visibility rules, with no frozen critical callback. A static prediction does not meet this gate. Done for its callbacks: in both ECFN play skins every callback converts, is replaced or is never consumed, and the gauge's pulse shows at the ex_hard and hazard looks only while the fill follows the value. Both ECFN play skins now import in their default configuration.
4. **Import facts and their dependencies** (S7-04): the typed snapshot of a scene's facts that the caller supplies to the configured pass, `main_state.option` answering the first facts (autoplay 32 and 33, the chart's judgment class 180 to 184, course mode 290) during construction, the scene and recorded facts on the import's result with the check that a snapshot of the same scene still matches them, the pending result of a base import that reaches a chart or attempt fact, located refusal of an unavailable fact or a property that is not one, and the report's fact counters; per-frame lowering unchanged; S10-07c supplies the snapshot from `player` (`imports::facts`) and owns reuse and invalidation. **Gate:** When the configured pass reads an available import fact, the importer shall return its snapshot value and record the dependency; when the fact is unavailable, it shall refuse at the read without inventing state; when a base import reaches a pending fact, it shall return a pending result recording that fact, not a refusal; when the argument is not a Lua integer within `i32`, it shall refuse fatally, inside `pcall` and `xpcall` too; when a recorded fact changes or becomes unavailable, or the scene differs, the result shall no longer be valid for the snapshot; when only an unread fact changes, it shall still be valid. Done for `main_state.option`: autoplay, the judgment class and course mode are answered and recorded, and on the owner's PC the two surveyed 7-key play skins that read the class at import now pass it and stop at their next live-state read, a `text` read; `number(96)` and texts 1001 to 1003 are answered from S7-06a, and `event_index` answers no fact yet.
5. **Paths and choices** (S7-05; in-root parent paths for images and fonts are already resolved, §3 "Resolution"): the file-choice tag syntax (`|tag|`) that the owner's Starseeker skin uses, for JSON and Lua skins alike, its substitution key (the whole name) kept apart from its listing pattern (§3 "The header"); directory choices, which the header lists beside files and a skin loads a part from with `dofile(get_path(key) .. "/part.lua")`, documented and tested; the selected-part and observed configuration conveniences are those S7-02 and #280 ship (the confined `dofile` and `require` relative to the entry's folder, `get_path`, and `file_path` holding entry names). An optional-part probe (`pcall(dofile, missing)`) stays a fault, as any failed loader is, until S7-06's option-value gate (item 6) removes it: a confined `dofile` of a name that passes every name and confinement rule but names no file inside the skin folder raises an ordinary Lua error, which a protected call catches as the source's does, so the skin goes on without that part; a name the rules refuse (rooted, escaping, a link, too long) stays a fatal refusal, and confinement of the existing part of the path is established before a name is classed as missing (so a link escaping the folder is refused whether or not its target exists), so a probe learns nothing outside the folder, and an unprotected missing load still refuses the skin. Measured: on three surveyed play-skin option values (two judge-font candidates without their sidecar file, and a full-combo candidate that is a file where the skin expects a folder) the source loads the skin and tapestry refuses at such a probe. design.md and §3's header grammar first. **Gate:** When each surveyed play skin's default configuration is imported on the owner's PC, no refusal shall come from a path or file choice the source resolves inside the skin folder. Done for tags and directory choices: Starseeker play7's header lists all 19 choices, the three tagged ones over `parts/lanecover_lift/`; its configured pass loads its selected parts through `get_path`, and it imports (item 6), so its image and font paths are checked; both ECFN play skins import. GenericTheme play7's 14 choices and Rm play7main's 79 all list candidates, and GenericTheme's judge sidecar loads through `get_path` and `file_path`; GenericTheme's object table holding a positional value now converts, and with effects by role (S7-06b) GenericTheme play7 imports closed (`--level 12`), its image paths checked: `play.lua:2487` lowers and the decorative `:1267` and `:2657` are replaced with notes (S7-06c lowers them). Rm play7main imports (item 6), so its image paths are checked. Their functions the source never reads are inert (GenericTheme's 55 option items' `isSelected`, Rm's header `process`), and neither holds a script or an unread listed entry.
6. **The play presentation program** (S7-06, split by its own design into ordered PRs, play first: built are S7-06a import folds, S7-06b the fuller AST with the captured object graph and effects by role, S7-06c partial evaluation into the expressions, and S7-06d the program (register code over Lua's booleans, integers and floats for play's `draw` callbacks, numbers' values and destinations' `timer` callbacks, with narrow state slots, verified in `Skin::new` and run each play frame after the fields and timers); of S7-06f and S7-06g the prepared text (constants, the direct chart-text fields and their empty-string fallbacks, no frame-time code), and of S7-06f the existing-state bridges and the image-set value, and the construction-time `main_state.offset` from the configuration; of S7-06e, e-1: the reference executor and replay harness over the program's callbacks (`lua_replay`), e-2: a play state's rounds through the carry and the frames production builds no play frame for, and e-3: the expression-lowered consumers against their closures; of S7-06s the score data across a practice play state's rounds; to come are the accepted observers (a construction-time `timer_util.timer_observe_boolean`, one `when` slot per instance, with its own design of when it starts: the source's starts on the first frame the skin is drawn, loading and the practice page included), the rest of S7-06g's bounded text, S7-06h the integrated evidence and gate): the AST that keeps literal values, assignments, branches and loops, loops compiled only once a surveyed play skin needs one; the captured object graph and partial evaluation (the configuration in critical callbacks under the capture proof, the import facts, constant tables, helpers with known arguments; `skin.type` is not folded, since only bundled skins outside the support target read it); the typed program in `skin-model` with its install-time verifier, its executor in `IN_PLAY`, Lua's numeric semantics, bounded text and the source's callback order; the direct text and judgment-count aliases and accepted observers; the development-only reference executor, an offline harness replaying recorded frames outside any play; and the integrated evidence (cap witnesses, fuzzing, allocation and timing). The expressions stay, and consumers move one by one. **Gate:** When each surveyed play skin is imported under each of its option values and file candidates on the owner's PC, it shall import or refuse with a specific located reason, with no unexplained omitted part and no critical freeze; when the offline harness replays the same frames through its compiled callbacks and the reference executor, their values, types, errors and invocation traces shall agree; and when the model builds its frames with the program, they shall allocate nothing. On the owner's PC, with S7-06a's folds and the undefined-source rule ("Policy"), Starseeker play7's default configuration imports with every image and font path it draws checked, its default close part's image with no source ignored with one `UndefinedSource` note. S7-06b reads callables by the source's serializers (roots, inert functions, scripts and unread entries opening the analysis), keeps the fuller AST and the captured object graph with graph-proven bindings, and walks effects by role (the effect walk over the modeled subset, writers never lowering, `Interference` at the open site, the full-tree role scan and the analysis time budget). With S7-06b, `lua_report --autoplay off --class normal` (`--level 12` for GenericTheme) on the owner's PC gives all five surveyed play skins closed, GenericTheme play7 imported, Rm play7main stopped at `play.lua:1764`, and ECFN play7, play7wide and Starseeker play7 imported as before. With S7-06c, GenericTheme play7 imports with `play.lua:1267` lowered, and with S7-06f's existing-state bridges `:2657` lowered too (it was replaced as the unmapped option 2244 before); Rm play7main lowers the EX-score numbers at `play.lua:1764`, `:1767`, `:1786`, `:1789` and `:1793` as critical conditionals, replaces its alternative-BGA draws as the unmapped option 40, and stops next at a critical gauge with a timer (`critical Gauge: Timer`, read as the gauge at `play.lua:2620`/`:2633`, then a critical cover at `:2955` predicted), outside S7-06; ECFN play7, play7wide and Starseeker play7 keep every disposition. With S7-06d, Rm play7main compiles `remain-rate-num` and `remain-rate-adot-num` (`play.lua:1831`, `:1834`) into the program under each Hi-Speed Type and Scratch Side value; its other values that divided or called `math` now stop at the unmapped `main_state.event_index` (`:1800`, `:1815`, and `getHiSpeed()`'s callers at `:1846`, `:1849`, `:2435`, `:2438`, `:2450` and `:2453`), and GenericTheme play7, ECFN play7, play7wide and Starseeker play7 keep every disposition. With S7-06d-2's located writes (a frame-time assignment to an upvalue voids only the proofs through that cell), GenericTheme play7 under Score Graph=Normal and Slim imports, its `max_timer` latch (`play.lua:1671`) a decorative writer, and Rm play7main under Target=My Best imports, `diff-mybest` (`:3145`) and the draw reading its `_number` (`:3155`) decorative constants; the program running those writers per frame is S7-06d-3's (state slots), whose first part gives play skins the source's point (number 100, the latch's guard) and the frames the state clock that `main_state.time()` reads (design.md "beatoraja Lua Skins"), and whose second part compiles destinations' `timer` callbacks into the program with narrow state slots: GenericTheme play7 under Score Graph=Normal and Slim compiles the latch as the timer of both MAX-line destinations (`play.lua:1671`), and Rm play7main under Target=My Best keeps `diff-mybest` (`:3145`, `Writes`) and the draw reading `_number` (`:3155`, `Interference`) decorative constants, their best and target scores not held in play. With S7-06d-3b a practice session's rounds carry the slots and the point the source keeps across them, so the MAX line a full-score round lit stays lit in the next round, as the source shows it, when that round's last build ran the program on its complete view (design.md "beatoraja Lua Skins"). With S7-06e-1, `lua_replay --autoplay off --class normal --level 12` over the `full` (7 notes, every one PGREAT) and `mixed` (13 notes, every judgment, misses and empty POORs) scripts agrees on GenericTheme play7 under Score Graph=Normal and Slim (both MAX-line timer callbacks, `play.lua:1671`, on a system image and a plain image, in every converted value, start, elapsed, installed start and sampled age), on Rm play7main at its default and under Target=My Best (`remain-rate-num` and `remain-rate-adot-num`, `play.lua:1831`, `:1834`, raw and converted, with every root the reference cannot run to its end covered by a reviewed isolation entry kept with the skins), and on ECFN play7, play7wide and Starseeker play7 (no program callbacks; every source invocation of a modelled kind attributed to its occurrence's record); perturbing the point by −1 in the reference makes GenericTheme's two timer callbacks disagree from the frame the point reaches 200 000. With S7-06e-2, `lua_replay --script rounds` (loading, the practice page, and three section rounds: every note on time, a rearmed READY and a quit, every note on time) and `--script rounds-unwitnessed` (the first round's last judgment never built) agree on GenericTheme play7 under Score Graph=Normal and Slim: the carried round shows both MAX-line timers on, at the first round's start, from its first READY frame, the round after the quit and every round after the unwitnessed one show the slots refused as the carried-state limit, and the roots that stop on loading and page frames are covered by reviewed entries kept with the skins; Rm play7main at its default and under Target=My Best agrees over `rounds` as well. With S7-06e-3 every expression-lowered `draw`, `op`, number value and image-set selector of a modelled kind is compared with its closure on every play frame the reference reaches it: GenericTheme play7, Rm play7main, ECFN play7 and play7wide agree over `full` and `mixed`, and GenericTheme over `rounds`; Rm over `rounds` agrees too: with S7-06s a practice round reads the play state's score data (the EX score and the score rates beside the point) until its own first judgment, so `info-rate-num`, `score-rate-num` and `score-score` show the previous round's values on its later rounds' frames before then, as the source does; perturbing the note count (+1) makes GenericTheme's `notesleft` disagree on every frame. Program gating follows the source's per-kind prepare gates, a negative image selector, the judge's no-judgment and selected-grade gates and its installation pruning (design.md "beatoraja Lua Skins", "Built"): a destination the source removes at installation compiles no program callback, its kinds' gates join the program's gate, and what the import cannot decide without reading a file (an image whose declared file does not load among them) keeps its callbacks, noted at each as a schedule deviation ("Differences from the source"). With it, the same `lua_replay` runs agree with the reference on the negative selector, which refuses a latch under its gate, and on option-, keyframe- and static-condition-removed numbers, which invoke nothing; no surveyed skin reaches any of them, and every surveyed configuration imports as before. GenericTheme under Display #TOTAL=On still refuses: its `gaugetotal` text (`:2775`) opens the candidate at `play.lua:2780`, arithmetic on `total_percentage`, a local assigned again and so of no known kind (the concatenation at `:2781` is in the walk's subset), and the text itself is outline-font composition of a float: S7-06g's. The gate covers every option value, not only the default configurations (§1): the configurations refused today import, each kind by its own step: a critical interference, an unresolved callee and a live-state read inside a helper by S7-06d's program, which runs that code per frame instead of refusing it; `main_state.offset` by S7-06f's answer from the configuration; a protected probe of a missing part by item 5's error-returning `dofile`; a Lua `op` that is not a table by S6e's first PR (no condition, as the source reads it); a critical `SourceRect` past its file's right or bottom edge by S6e's edge cells (§3 "Images"); and a construction-time Lua error that the source's interpreter does not raise (one option value, raised at a `string.match` call, where the source loads the skin; why the two differ is not yet traced) by tracing the divergence between the sandbox's library and the source's and matching the source, S7-02's surface; and the callbacks replaced today convert, a division and a library call in a value (S7-06d), `main_state.number` 12, `main_state.option` 400 and `main_state.event_index(55)` by S10-13's producers, `main_state.number` 153 and `main_state.float_number(113)` by S10-10's, and `main_state.option` 40 by S15's BGA settings. Checked by importing each option value and file candidate with `lua_report` and by the side-by-side comparison against the source player under the values that change what is drawn.
7. **Runtime Lua for the screens outside play** (S7-07, render side, tried early on the stateful selection and result skins): the configured pass's VM kept for a selection, decision or result screen, its host modules answering from the screen's snapshot, per-frame instruction, memory and time budgets, typed-constant fallbacks with a note, and every VM dropped or quiesced before READY; the runtime in a module of its own that the play's installation never holds, a `cargo xtask check-arch` rule with negative fixtures failing when an `IN_PLAY` module or the program's executor names it, its types or `mlua`, and each runtime's quiescence acknowledged before recording opens (no callback running or pending, its collector stopped, resumed only on its own screen, destroyed only outside the in-play interval, across the tail, Closing, aborts, retries and recovery). **Gate:** When a play follows a Lua-driven selection screen, the play shall allocate nothing, and a separate count of each VM's Lua calls, instruction steps and collector work from READY through the audio tail or Closing shall be zero; when an `IN_PLAY` module or the program's executor names the runtime, its types or `mlua`, `cargo xtask check-arch` shall fail; when a runtime callback errors or exceeds a budget, it shall give its typed constant with a note and the screen shall keep drawing. Complete for selection and result (S7-07a to S7-07d), its gate checked by: a play after a Lua-driven selection, `a_play_leaves_the_screens_runtimes_at_rest_until_its_end` (`crates/player/src/main.rs`, through the rig's preparation, start and end, with and without a render failure), `the_runtime_draws_each_frame_and_rests_through_a_play` (`render_side/tests/selection/warp.rs`) and `a_result_runtime_rests_through_a_play_and_resumes` (`render_side/tests/result.rs`), with the play's allocation guards in CI's release steps; the boundary, `cargo xtask check-arch`'s R6 tests (`xtask/src/arch.rs`, `r6_rejects_each_way_of_naming_the_backend_outside_screens` and the other `r6_` tests) and its privacy compile harness (`xtask/src/privacy.rs`), which checks itself with the runtime's items widened; a callback's error or budget, `callbacks_run_on_the_kept_vm`, `a_shared_book_keeps_a_sites_error_once_per_lifetime`, `the_budget_stops_the_runtime_after_three_frames`, `each_frame_cap_stops_a_call_alone`, `a_budget_spent_part_way_gives_the_rest_their_constant`, `the_memory_cap_stops_the_runtime` and `a_host_panic_stops_the_runtime` (`crates/skin-beatoraja/tests/lua_runtime.rs`), with the screens drawing on, `a_runtime_past_its_budget_stops_and_the_screen_draws_on` (selection WARP) and `a_stopped_runtime_leads_a_full_strip` (result). On the owner's PC, the dev-tools switches `TAPESTRY_DEV_SCRIPTS` (a budget forced out, a collection after each frame, no runtime) and `tapestry measure select` (each select and result frame's preparation, runtime and build spans, and each screen's lost refreshes from a PresentMon capture) give the real-app checks and the runtime's per-frame cost at 240 Hz: on the owner's PC with ECFN select and the ECFN and Starseeker result skins, at most 400 instructions, 30 host calls and 0.31 ms of runtime in a frame, and a select or result frame's p99 under 0.4 ms. Not verified: 144 and 60 Hz; the collector's natural work, which Lua does not report; Starseeker's FAST and SLOW marks after a replayed play, which no capture shows (an owner look, #189); and the select and result screens' lost refreshes under design.md "Frame Pacing in Refreshes", whose one-to-one pairing fails on about one present in 27 000 timestamped 2.0 to 2.8 ms after its capture, with or without the runtime (S10-16 diagnoses it). The decision screen's runtime is not wired: no surveyed decision skin has a callback that needs it, and such a callback keeps its typed constant by role with its note. The play's own interval is checked through the rig, not through the whole live loop.

The screen adapters follow: play through S10-07, then decision, result and selection through their S10-09 PRs, each using S7-06's program where it works and S7-07's runtime outside play. Each activates its Lua skins only after the steps it needs.

Against S10: S7 runs beside S10-06, reusing the exact playhead already landed and changing neither its producer nor the default renderer's adoption of it; S10-04b keeps its own prerequisites, S7 not blocking it, and maps newly available readouts only when their contract lands, unavailable speed-policy selectors staying unsupported; S10-08a runs in parallel, S7-01 to S7-05 targeting the existing chart-text interfaces and S7-06's text drawing with glyphs prepared before play; and S10-07 keeps its prerequisites and adds S7-03's and S7-04's gates, owning dispatch, cancellation, dependency tracking, candidate ownership, installation, pending installations admitted for choice and Start, and per-attempt specialization checked against the snapshot that is final after the preset's refresh (design.md "beatoraja Lua Skins"), while activation follows the S7 row above.

S10, in order (each a planned contract until its PR lands, except where marked done):

1. **Ordered batches and premultiplied colour** (S10-01): `SpriteBatch` as one ordered list of runs drawn in order; the explicit 64-byte `Instance` with rotation about the pivot in the shader; premultiplied colour with the blend equations of §6, "Render integration"; the atlas and the fixed 1920×1080 projection kept; built-in sprites, menus and results migrated. WARP tests of alpha-additive-alpha overlap, translucent coloured sprites, fading feedback and translucent menu text over known backgrounds, with the Japanese title's expected image unchanged; the allocation test counts the first frame, later frames and a full batch, each span stopping its count before anything is asserted. Done.
2. **Atlas array, projection, clips and resize** (S10-02): the atlas texture array with the built-in atlas in layer 0 and a skin's mipped pages after it, checked before allocating and kept on refusal; the flags word's layer and nearest bit; the canvas projection for play frames, menus and results staying fixed; the bounded clip table with scissors projected per target size (D1); staging sized from the skin's capacity, the simple skin keeping 8 192; `Renderer::resize`, the window's coalesced size slot and wake, DPI changes through the same path, a minimized window skipping configuration and presentation while messages and handshakes go on, and menu, loading and launch text laid out again on a size change. WARP tests of atlas layers, filters, pivots and adapted canvases at 16:9, 16:10 and 21:9, consecutive differing, absent and empty clips across a resize, a refused atlas keeping the one in use, a skin's capacity past 8 192, and one renderer resized, minimized and restored with its play state; unit tests of the size slot's coalescing and of the render thread following it; the allocation test adds an applied resize, the first frame after it and later ones. Done.
