3. **Attempt preparation and the pre-READY lifecycle** (S10-03a): `Rig::prepare_attempt` after the preset's refresh and before loading ends, with `Preparation::{Ready, Cancelled, Refused}` and the located `PreparationRefusal`; the preset refreshed before every play, the first round included; refusals routed to selection, the practice page or the direct play's error, with the number given back; fresh `game::Attempt` data each play; the play phase published before a terminal transition (`PlayView::playing`); the five chart labels kept from one ingestion; every thread checked before recording opens; a practice fitted at each Start and each round to the refreshed preset by gauge ID, a Start or round whose fit resets a value the player set not opening, the page stating the reset and showing what the fit gave; `last_note_end` by the contract above; the player's owned `AttemptContext` and `RenderMsg::PrepareAttempt` drawn with `SimpleSkin`, the screen's recovery separate; typed loading updates beside the lines, bound to the loading lifetime. Done.
4. **One ordered batch, typed build results and the reserved overlay** (S10-03b-1): `SpriteBatch` as the runtime model's `QuadBatch` (a `Sprite` is a `Quad`, with the model's `Blend` and `Run`), so `SimpleSkin` and a model skin write into one batch, sized by the skin's sprites and runs (`PlaySkin::run_capacity`) with a reserve after them that only the batch's owner opens (`QuadBatch::with_reserve`, `open_reserve`); `PlaySkin::build`, `Renderer::build` and `Screen::build` returning `Result<(), skin_model::BuildError>`, allocation-free: a refused frame stages and submits nothing, the renderer builds that attempt no more until the next attempt's preparation, keeping the atlas, the clips and the device, and the render thread stops the play as a screen failure while keeping the screen, which only the next attempt's preparation builds again, device and surface failures keeping the screen-dropping recovery; the render thread's app region a guard ending on every way out, before a refusal is reported; the measurement overlay moved out of `SimpleSkin` into the staging, after any skin's sprites, from 1 024 reserved sprites in one reserved run. Unit tests of the reserve, of the overlay after a skin that fills its sprites and runs, of a refused frame leaving nothing staged and of the app region ending on an early error; WARP tests of a refused frame submitting nothing, the skin built no more in that preparation, and the next preparation drawing with the atlas and clip kept, and of the overlay over a skin that fills its batch; a render-thread test of a refused frame stopping the attempt with no build or draw after it, the screen kept and built once, the next preparation building again, and the app region counting nothing after the refusal (dev-tools); the allocation test adds the fullest overlay to every frame, a refused frame with its count ended before its diagnostic, and the overlay whole after a full batch. Done.
5. **The declarative skin on the play screen** (S10-03b-2): `DeclSkin` drawing a `SkinBundle` (the compiled skin, its atlas pages and matching `UvTable`, clips, scratch side and text producer), a fresh `Play` per attempt writing into the batch directly; the preparation and frame adapter: the borrowed `PlayContext`, the five chart labels and the applied target at preparation, chart loading only dropping the previous `Play`, `playing` from the same `PlayView` borrow as the tally, gauges and completion, the bounded effects slice in order across drains, and until their PRs `Layout::AUTHOR`, zero and disabled covers with `adjusting` false, tempo, duration and multiplicative hi-speed readouts `None`, no lane gain until S10-04b-2 (the lanes then draw only the judgment line) and the bundle's scratch side; transactional installation (`Renderer::stage` uploading and validating the atlas, bind group, clips, staging, instance buffer and the fresh attempt with its text before a `PreparedToken`; `Renderer::commit` against its request and device generations, after readiness, the restore check and the cancellation and failure checks and before the loading acknowledgement, on retries and practice rounds without a loading lifetime too; refusal, cancellation, timeout, supersession and late completion discarding it whole; healthy-device refusals answered as `Preparation::Refused`); rebuild recovery restoring the committed bundle from its retained CPU description, counted in `Renderer::memory`, never rereading files or substituting `SimpleSkin`, then preparing a fresh attempt; an `AttemptText` seam preparing each attempt's text; the five chart labels carried through `AttemptContext`. Production draws `SimpleSkin` unless `player.toml` chooses a play skin, which startup installs (S10-07b). Done.
6. **The loading presentation** (S10-03c), in three PRs, all landed: S10-03 is complete and S10-07's loading prerequisite is met; #83 and S9 (S9-01 to S9-03) have landed; S7's gates (S7-03 and S7-04), S10-09a and S10-09b have landed; S10-05, S10-06 and S10-08a still come before S10-07, and a declarative skin is chosen in `player.toml` at startup (S10-07b).
   - **The portable loading runtime** (S10-03c-1, R3 "The loading runtime"): `Skin::prepare_loading` and `Skin::build_loading` with their own fields, timer starts and readings, `when` starts and loading text, drawn through the play's evaluation and drawing; the `screen` timer, from `ready` in a play and from the origin while loading, which the importer gives untimed destinations and image cycles (D3); the loading class of every field and timer (`loading_field`, `loading_timer`), read sets taken at the skin's load and the descriptor checked each frame; the lanes' judgment line without a chart and the gauge's empty frame from `LoadingGauge`; the loading text slots installed and cleared by `TextSlots`; a refused build refusing the state for good; `SkinFrame::loading` removed, a play keeping `loading` off and `load.percent` at 100; the loading build in `IN_PLAY`. Model and importer tests, the allocation test's loading builds, and the `skin_json` fuzz target building each import's loading state. Done.
   - **Render ownership and installation** (S10-03c-2, "Render integration"): `LoadingFrame` and `PlaySkin::build_loading` returning `Result<(), BuildError>`; `LoadingPresentation` owned per installation (`Installation`), kept by the same bundle within a lifetime and fresh for another bundle or lifetime; `Renderer::install` with the shared request and device generations; the installed loading text checked against its installation and resource generation (`LoadingTextKey`); the selection transaction (`RenderLink::select`, `PendingSelect`) with its withdrawal; chart choices and practice Starts admitted only once the skin is installed (`AppRig::start_ready`), a refusal routed back with its located reason (Q4-A); recovery restoring the committed installation and the lifetime's origin kept outside the screen; the presentations in `Renderer::memory`; a refused loading build reported end to end as `ScreenError::LoadingRefused`; interim frame values until S10-03c-3; the loading frame path in `IN_PLAY`. WARP, render-thread, player and allocation tests. Done.
   - **The player's lifetime and frame bridge** (S10-03c-3, "Render integration"): the loading clock begun once per loading lifetime (`RenderLink::begin_loading`); the lifetime's whole state numbered on the producers' side (`LifetimeState`, `LoadInput`, `LoadingUpdate`), the slot and the render thread taking only newer states; the load's progress mapped to `LoadState`; the gauge descriptor from the validated preset, replaced by the preset `begin_play` refreshed; the chosen chart's text only from its own lifetime and revision, never the previous chart's, prepared through the installation's producer, staged and installed under its `LoadingTextKey`, bounded and counted in `Renderer::memory`; a bundle replaced within a lifetime keeping its clock; the covers interim until S10-04a/04b; never building chart A's or B's `Play` while B loads, with the end-of-loading acknowledgement kept; the bridge's per-frame path in `IN_PLAY`. Windows tests of a stalled A→B load and its pixels, render and allocation tests. Done.
7. **Controls, hi-speed and the start gate** (S10-04, design.md "Play Adjustments" and "Play Lifecycle", the start gate), in five PRs landing in order: three foundation PRs (S10-04a-1 to S10-04a-3, together S10-04a) and S10-04b in two, its dark foundation (S10-04b-1) and the activation (S10-04b-2). Each is green on Windows CI with its portable policy, model and replay tests on portable CI too, coordinated with stage 2 (#70). A foundation PR implements and tests its part and changes nothing the player can reach beyond what it states; S10-04b switches everything on at once, so no partial control ever runs against the visible-time ladder and no gate runs without its controls. Not in stage 3: a 20-level mode, historical classic tables, preset combinations, LR2's normalization, external calibration and the SCROLL and SPEED channels.
   1. **The adjustment model** (S10-04a-1, after S10-03; `game` only): the portable types (`SpeedMode`, `HiSpeedReference`, `HiSpeedSettings` and the covers with their switches) and `game::PlayOptions` with its validation, an in-memory API that reads and writes no file; the exact arithmetic (checked, reduced integer ratios for `H`, the duration and the green number, `Solve`, the bounds and the zero-area endpoints); the `game::adjustment` reducer with the transitions, the dispatch table, target precedence, gestures (the double press, digital repeats and acceleration, exact axis accumulation), ordering and cancellation, ownership and transactions, the READY generation and the presentation head by phase, F1 and F2 among its controls; `PresentationSnapshot` with `adjusting`, published through a prepared triple buffer; tempos given by the caller. Its paths are in `IN_PLAY`, with allocation guards over the reducer and the publication. Tests inject exact tempos. Production does not call it. **Gate:** when every dispatch cell, opposite input and held transition is exercised, only its permitted actions shall occur, and when the reducer drains observations in different splits, sequences equal in time and context shall give the same state.
   2. **Recording and input** (S10-04a-2, after S10-04a-1): the whole replay change: `ReadyHistory` and the cancelled-before-playback status in a format bump that writes a play with a genuinely empty history and no starting tuning made up from the ladder, older versions refused; the writer, the decoder with the history's reservation and decode limits, fixtures, fuzzing, `replay::verify` and `eval-cli` reporting a capture with no played attempt, and the result and personal-best consumers, the rebuild included, never giving such a capture a score or a play count. The wire types stay in `replay`, which depends on neither `game` nor `input`; the conversions are in `game`. In `input`: `input::AdjustmentTap`, the physical hold latch and signed axis movement read before the scratch's conversion; one source table per attempt and reusable opening buffers in place of an allocating `SourceSet::open_play` per opening. In `game`: the binding types for START, SELECT and the auxiliary keys (E, W, Up and Down by default, controller buttons captured), the scratch's adjustment directions and reversal, and the refusal of role collisions, built and tested without changing `device.toml` or key config. Production writes the new format with an empty history and is otherwise unchanged.
   3. **The start lifecycle** (S10-04a-3, after S10-04a-2): the start gate (a READY candidate, its cancellation, the wait, the rearm and the commit through the real input and audio handshake checked by generation, the input thread's hold latch taking part, an opening deferred back into the same loading lifetime); the evaluator's, the model's and the render side's reset in place on a rearm; the cancelled-before-playback capture for any quit, retry, close or failure (a failed restore plan included) after recording opens and before the transport commits, counted as no play; F1 and F2 through the reducer; condition 270 reading START or SELECT held from the snapshot; scripted rigs and fixtures. A hold cached by the game loop does not satisfy the gate (design.md "Play Lifecycle", the start gate). It lands in two PRs: S10-04a-3a, the gate, the rearm and the cancelled capture in `game`, `input`, `eval` and the audio worker (the engine holding the transport until the pass reaching its commit point admits it, so a BGM event before chart time 0 starts at the commit), run through the scripted rigs, the gated storage admitted with the recording reservation, and condition 270's reader of the snapshot; and S10-04a-3b, the player threads' wiring kept dark: `PlayerRig`'s gate (`player::start_gate`) installed only by the development option `--dev-start-gate`, a rearm handed to the input thread through a bounded queue with the readers and buffers reserved at the first opening, and to the audio worker with a round's restore planned again in place in a spare box (`SeekPlan::replan`, `Restorer::replan`), so a practice round's rearm allocates nothing, and every restore a rearm replaces handed back to the game thread, never freed on the audio thread; the exchange's buffers admitted with the recording (`game::GateStorage`); a cancellation the audio thread does not acknowledge in time failing the output, so no READY opens after it; a refused rearm's capture sealed with every event that fits; the render side's reset in place, the play side's rearm count read before its effects are drained by generation, so no effect of the cancelled candidate is drawn and none of the next is lost, and `PlaySkin::rearm` starting the skin's runtime over (`DeclSkin`'s timers, starts and reducer, `SimpleSkin`'s note and judgment state) with its prepared bitmap text and memory kept, which the renderer's memory witness checks over repeated rearms of a declarative skin with bitmap text; the opening's loading lifetime paused rather than ended (`RenderLink::pause_loading`), so a hold at the opening draws the same lifetime again from its origin on the loading clock (`RenderLink::resume_loading`) and the opening ends it; and the practice round's gated test. All of it runs in scripted rigs and tests; in production the gate, the cancelled-before-playback save and the reducer's F1 and F2 stay off, the ladder keeps F1 and F2 and condition 270 keeps its D4 difference until S10-04b-2 switches them on. **Gate:** when every held transition and READY cancellation is exercised, only its permitted actions shall occur, all cancelled input shall remain recorded, and the next READY shall use a fresh origin.
   4. **Authoritative speed, readouts and activation** (S10-04b, after S10-04a-3, S10-06's tempo provider and shared exact playhead, and S6c-13's tempo sidecar): the references and the event tempo from the sidecar, the practice speed applied once; the settings' defaults and the refusal of a `player.toml` with `visible_time_ms`; START, SELECT, the auxiliary keys and the scratch's directions in `device.toml`, with its version bump, and their key-config steps; the production controls: the gate, the cancelled-before-playback save and F1 and F2 through the reducer, with the old application path removed so nothing applies twice; condition 270 live, which retires D4's difference for it; both skins on the exact original-lane gain (`LaneView`'s gain replacing `visible_time`, unavailable in structural and loading frames), `SimpleSkin` included; the source projections (`FrameReadouts::hispeed_x100` and `duration` from `Hs`, the eight duration variants in slots 51–74) and the native block in slots 75–85 (`field::COUNT` 86, after S9-01's fields 37 to 50), validated before anything is mutated, with `BuildError::InvalidReadouts` and its poisoning kept for malformed frames only; the importer's 1312–1327 with their guards (§3), 372 and 373 still unsupported in play and 92 still the chart's metadata; imported skins' taps and mines at a zero span suppressed instead of drawn on the line (R6); the visible-time ladder, `visible_time_ms` and the old live field path removed. It lands in two PRs: S10-04b-1, the dark foundation, pure and changing nothing the player draws: `game::readouts`, the exact source and native projections from a presentation snapshot, a tempo, the references and the speed, every duration and variant whose rounded ms or green number would pass `READOUT_MAX` given as unavailable; `skin_model::Gain` with its checked range, `LaneView`'s gain, `Scale::from_gain` and the zero-span suppression; slots 51–85 with `FrameReadouts`' variants and native block, `native_values` and their validation before anything is mutated; the importer's 1312–1327 with their guards, condition 270 keeping its D4 difference; and `DeclSkin` reading the lanes and readouts its `FrameInput` carries, while the player's frames pass no gain and unavailable readouts and `SimpleSkin` keeps the visible time; and S10-04b-2, the activation: everything else at once (the settings and `device.toml` v2 with the key-config steps; the production gate and `game::AdjustDriver` fed by the tap's markers, its fences and credits, attempts entered at loading and attached where the chart is final, the display offset cut in at a drain; the tap's presentation focus, deadlines and the window's speed keys for autoplay; both skins drawing one snapshot per frame through `render::presentation`, `SimpleSkin` on the gain with its covers and native HUD line; the BPM range; condition 270 live; the ladder, `visible_time` and `--dev-start-gate` removed). With S10-04b-2, S10-04 is complete. **Gate (S10-04b-1):** when a frame is built from a snapshot and tempo, every source and native projection shall match its exact witness and validity; when no field is left, no imported note geometry shall be drawn. **Gate (S10-04b-2):** when the tempo, a cover, the target or `H` changes, both skins and every projection shall consume one coherent generation, and retry and practice shall follow the transition table exactly.

   Acceptance for S10-04 as a whole, beside the gates, each tested in the PR that brings its part: when a modifier is held before loading completes, the loading presentation (its animation, progress and chosen chart's text) shall go on without recording opening; when a late hold cancels a READY, its origin and events shall stay unchanged in the history and no authorization it held shall start the transport; when a release rearms READY, prepared resources shall be reused, timers and effects reset by generation, and nothing allocated; when the winning attempt is re-evaluated, its judgments shall match the live ones (scratch, LN releases, early inputs and empty POORs included) and cancelled candidates shall not affect them; when SUDDEN+ is shown again, only Floating shall solve; when SELECT is used in Classic, neither the mode nor an unused target shall change; when a target is unreachable, the actual green number and the saved target shall stay distinct and inward steps shall work; when HIDDEN+ changes, `H` shall not; when LIFT or SUDDEN+ reaches 100 %, drawing and readouts shall stay finite and no skin, an imported one included, shall draw future notes on the line; when source and native rounding differ, each shall keep its own result and validity; when held controls cross a modifier, target, scene, focus or result-gate change, no new action shall occur until a release and a fresh press; and when the reducer takes an augmented input stream (the adjustment controls' observations in receive order, with each display-offset change inserted as a cut at the position of the first drain that read it), different splits of that same augmented stream shall give the same state; a display-offset change takes effect at that cut, not at its key's receive time.

   Portable tests, the exact witnesses: at 150 BPM, G 300 and no cover, `H` 3.2, 500 ms, green 300; LIFT 300 ‰ with SUDDEN+ 200 ‰, `v` 0.56, `H` 224/125, `Hs` 2.56, native 500 ms and green 300, source 14, 314 and 316 at 200, 300 and 140; `H` 3.2 with LIFT 300 ‰, 350 ms and green 210; Floating's one faster key, `H` 3.7, 16000/37 ms, green 259, target 300, a retry back at 3.2, and Classic's 3.45 kept across a retry; every cover off at 150→300 BPM with `H` 3.2, a fine step giving 3.21, not 1.6; SUDDEN+ on at 0 across the same change, a decrease clamped at 0 yet solving to `H` 1.6 and green 300; at 300 BPM with cover 0.3, G 300→299 giving `H` 336/299, 1495/3 ms, green 299; `H` 2.56 with cover 0.2 at 300 BPM hidden (312.5 ms, green 188), then shown (Floating `H` 1.28, green 300; Classic 2.56, green 150); exactly 502.5 ms giving native green 302 and source 312, 313 and 1313 at 503, 301 and 302; at 10 BPM a requested 48 clamped to 20 (1200 ms, green 720) and at 100000 BPM 0.0048 to 0.01 (240 ms, green 144), the target 300 both times; 19.8 + 0.5 → 20 → 19.5, and 0.3 − 0.5 → 0.01 → 0.51; full SUDDEN+ at `H` 0.01, 0 ms and green 0, reopened to 900 ‰ giving 0.32, 500 ms, green 300; LIFT 1000 with zero native duration, the source multiplier's dependants unavailable and the configured whites available; practice at 150 % from START 150 playing START 225, `H` 32/15, 500 ms; a delayed drain at G 360 and cover 500 ‰ (+1 at 150 BPM, −1 at 300) ending at cover 500 ‰, `H` 2/3, 600 ms whatever the drain splits; SELECT with F1 and G −1 at one time at 300 BPM giving `H` 480/299 and G 299 in either packet order; a hold with 100 ms of READY left not starting the play, its opening kept, and the release opening a full READY at once. The presentation head: a long load solving at 150 BPM to `H` 2.56; a practice round at section 60 s with a 2 s lead-in and 300 BPM from 57.85 s, the display offset +100 ms set while loading, solving at 300 BPM to 1.28. A device removed and reconnected while waiting and across a rearm, the record round-tripping against its one source table. Allocation guards with both skins over the first and later inputs, the reducer, a frame, cancellation and rearm, full covers and a saturated history; the F-series memory witnesses at the schema's most fields and programs, several covers and bar groups, a saturated history and repeated rearms, no readout field adding lane visits or quads; replay fuzzing over invalid history ranges, origins and source ids, the limits and unstarted captures, and skin fuzzing over the appended schema and endpoint availability.

   Windows CI: the real input, player, audio and render bridges, scripted plays and the `DeclSkin` allocation test, keeping S10-03's WARP and CPU comparisons and loading tests. Manual checks in issue #189: Q and W and captured controller buttons, the scratch binding unchanged; the scratch's direction, wrap and fractional movement, digital repeats and acceleration; fine speed with every cover off, and Floating across BPM changes and STOPs; native and source green numbers and whites in pixels, OFF labels, an unreachable target's feedback and the 100 % ends; Floating against Classic when SUDDEN+ is shown again, persistence across retries and the next chart, and practice speed; holds in the last 100 ms of READY, repeated restarts, the audio cancelled, a release eligible at once and the history kept; adjusting during play with judgments and misses as usual, focus and device loss, and holds across scene changes.

   Documented differences (D4, with the readouts): original-lane scaling and a green number that counts LIFT (`play/LaneRenderer.java:324-330`); additive Floating steps where the source steps in proportion to its base (`:237-245`), and SELECT in green units where it sets milliseconds (`:173-175`); the cover correction at the current tempo, HIDDEN+ left out, LIFT corrected together with SUDDEN+ and one final solve (`play/ControlInputProcessor.java:231-243`); the fine step with every cover off, independent of any correction (the same lines); solving when SUDDEN+ is shown again, in Floating only, where the source sets the flag alone (`LaneRenderer.java:217-219`); positive bounds on a solved multiplier, where the source resets it unclamped (`LaneRenderer.java:206-209`); exact native rounding beside the imported 313 and 1313 orders (`skin/property/IntegerPropertyFactory.java:292-315, 541-556`); MAIN prepared deterministically, free of hash order and empty references (`LaneRenderer.java:130-150`); the SUDDEN+ switch persisted (`play/BMSPlayer.java:836-851`); auxiliary keys only while a modifier is held, no wheel, window hold or both-button quit, and every auxiliary control recorded (`ControlInputProcessor.java:135-206`); repeats on monotonic input time and exact axis accumulation (`ControlInputProcessor.java:246-290`, `input/BMControllerInputProcessor.java:218-226`); and a late hold blocking READY, a restart after the release and no clearance delay (`BMSPlayer.java:474-475, 500-515, 605-616`). No Java or `f32` pipeline, abnormal-input artefact or last-digit float error is reproduced.
8. **Bitmap loading text, JSON fixtures and inline trim clips** (S10-05, after S10-03c and S6c-07; before S10-07): bitmap loading lines prepared outside the loading build and carried through the existing bounded staged-text path, installed at the next loading frame under its installation/resource key, lifetime and text revision. Bitmap-only bundles need no raster-text producer. Changed or unavailable fields clear their slots and composites immediately; unchanged slots and every timer stay. Preparation and installation retain the chosen-chart text contract, including replacement, restoration, resize, refusal and stale-completion handling. Staged bitmap storage joins `Renderer::memory`, installed glyph storage remaining with the loading presentation. Each fresh attempt keeps `DeclSkin::prepare`'s bitmap attachment; a READY rearm keeps its prepared text attached while runtime state resets in place.

   Render consumes each `Run::clip_rect` together with its fixed clip, through the applied canvas projection and D1's edge rounding, clamped to the target. Empty intersections draw nothing; subsequent unclipped runs restore the whole target. The model's trim rectangle is already in canvas coordinates: the quad keeps its uv, pivot and rotation. The fixed clip table remains installed with the atlas; moving inline clips require no growing clip table.

   Windows tests use self-made JSON, bitmap font definitions and page images through `read_header`, `Config::defaults`, `import`, atlas creation, `SkinBundle` and the real installation path. They assert distinct bitmap glyph pixels after chart changes and fresh attempt preparations; the real loading bridge's stalled A→B transition, partial metadata, changed fields and composites, cancellation and stale attachments; followers and independent cover paints under coherent changing covers; and partially trimmed paint with its upright clip. Cover movement may use the existing loading frame's settings while production control activation remains with S10-04b. A separate model fixture tests fixed-plus-inline intersection, differing neighbouring inline clips, an empty intersection and an unclipped successor across a resize; JSON destination clips retain their existing unsupported treatment. Fixtures assert visible content and pixels inside and outside clips, so removing bitmap attachment or inline clipping fails.

   Extend `render --test alloc` over real prepared bitmap attempts and loading presentations: first and later builds, changing/full covers and clips, an applied resize, refused and poisoned builds, and exhausted sprite/run capacity with the overlay retained. Count the production inline-scissor resolver as well as build/staging, with preparation and GPU work outside the count; every span has its positive control and ends before diagnostics. Retain the existing SimpleSkin, menu and result coverage, count staged bitmap memory until installation or discard, and register any new in-play sites. Windows CI, including the release allocation test, remains the gate; model changes retain portable checks.

   **Gate:** when a fresh attempt uses another chart's labels, its bitmap glyphs shall show those labels; while B's loading is stalled after A, its animation shall keep advancing and only B's available text shall draw; when a field changes, its affected slots shall remain empty until the matching replacement installs without restarting timers; when an attachment is stale, it shall install nothing; when a run has fixed and inline clips, only their intersection shall draw, an empty intersection drawing nothing and a following unclipped run drawing normally; and when the first, later, resized, refused, capacity-exhausted or rearmed frame runs, its in-play work shall allocate nothing.

   S10-07 owns production skin choice at startup for all four kinds, each with its own configured choice (`player.toml` `[skins.<kind>]`, design.md "Settings Files") and report, its CPU import and packing, the reports, and play's activation, reusing the existing bundle and installation transaction through `RenderLink::select(kind, …)`, the per-kind slots and the shared admission of S10-09b. Skins load at startup only, one kind at a time on a spawned `skin-import` thread joined before the next (owner decision); a later change comes only from S11's explicit skin change. It measures real skins' atlas texels per kind against the shared limit through the activation's log. S10-08a supplies rasterized outline/default-font text, D13 fallback included; S10-05, S10-06, S10-08a-1, S7's gates (S7-03 and S7-04), #83, S9-01 to S9-03 (in that order), S10-09a and S10-09b precede S10-07. Its acceptance: no import runs after startup except the attempt's specialization, joined in Preparing. In three PRs:
   - **S10-07a, the import seam** (done): the `[skins.<kind>]` settings and their load-time refusals, kept by the select screen's writes; `player/src/imports.rs`'s `build` (folder, header, configuration by name, import, atlas, bundle with a text producer only for rasterized text) and `run_cancellable` (completion only after the job's thread ends, cancellation and the 2 s stop bound at close, a caught panic and a silent thread end told apart); the stdout lines, the select screen's notice text of a non-play kind and the log summary with the bundle's texels and the committed total; the sandbox's dev-tools VM hooks (a live-VM count and a barrier in a VM's destruction). The text layer's admission charge landed with S10-08a-1.
   - **S10-07b, activation** (done): the startup imports on both launch paths (song selection: play, selection, decision, result; `tapestry play`: play, then result, a refused play skin failing the run with exit 1 before any recording), JSON play skins and Lua play skins that read no chart fact installed, the non-play kinds' real choice, their refusal report and notice; `RenderLink::atlas_limits` gives the packing limits and target of the screen built last. Each kind's texel figures come from the startup log; recording the owner's sets' figures is a #189 item.
   - **S10-07c, specialization** (after S10-07b), in two PRs (design.md "beatoraja Lua Skins", installation and specialization):
     - **S10-07c-1, the dormant seam** (done): the play level label (`ChartLabels.playlevel`, the loader's trimmed text), the attempt's facts and the level's parse in `player` (`imports::facts`), the job runner with its two signals (`run_job`: the attempt's quit joins the job, the run's end abandons it past the stop bound), the pack extracted from `build`, `specialize`, and the pending outcome of a Lua play skin's base import, which startup still refused.
     - **S10-07c-2, activation** (done): startup marks a pending play skin pending on the render thread (`RenderLink::pend`, play's `Slot::Pending`) and admits charts on both launch paths; each attempt's preparation specializes it first, reusing the one kept while its facts hold, with the stage line or the preparation page "スキンを準備しています"; the bundle is staged with the attempt and installed by its commit, an attempt with the skin in use refused while pending; the pending presentation per loading lifetime; the logs (facts, job wall time, staging time; the reused line); and the dev-tools witnesses the child prints (no VM open and every job joined before the render thread prepares; no job, Lua pass or instruction step from recording's open to the play's end). Unverified on the owner's skins: GenericTheme and Rm are pending and admit charts, and their specialization refuses at their next unsupported construct until S7-06b and S7-06c, so a pending skin's drawn loading state and play stay a #189 item until then. An early start in Loading or Arrange is decided from the logged job and staging times.
9. **Seamless loading** (9.1 to 9.3 done and 9.4a built; the rest planned; design.md "Play Lifecycle" for the decision screen and the loading state, "Load Pipeline" for the coordinator, prefetch and the ledger, "Chart Containers" for the compact form). The Windows baseline comes first, as manual items in issue #189 and a dev-tools measurement: choice-to-READY time by stage on real packs (OGG, NTFS, Defender, cold and warm caches), path resolution per `#WAV` instrumented inside the loader, folder parse rate at caps 1 and 2 in background mode with each priority call's result, the evaluation subset's serialization and hash time, and running beside OBS. It decides the PCM cache, disk persistence and the default caps. Speculative keysound decoding and folder prefetch pass their legal review before their PRs (design.md "Chart Containers", Legal review). Then, in order:
   1. **The coordinator** (done: #409, #418; after S10-03c): permits, scopes, generations and the commit, wrapping today's load with no flow change; the scan and the background loudness measurement take permits. Tests: the permit cap, cancellation releasing reservations, and A→B→A reusing a running job.
   2. **The lifecycle split** (done: #394) of `prepare_attempt` into the reusable part and the final readiness. Tests: refusal, restore, commit and discard, the thread looks and the end of loading, unchanged.
   3. **The record's starting tuning** (done: #384; its own replay format bump), its own PR ahead of the flow change, holding the play adjustments as recording opens (design.md "Play Adjustments"): serialization round-trip, value bounds, the previous version refused with `UnsupportedVersion`, and design.md's "Recorded today".
   4. **The decision screen and the loading state**, in two parts. **9.4a** (built: #428, #433, #440, #443, #446, #451, #458, #464, #469; its real-app checks continue in issue #189; after items 9.1 to 9.3 and S10-07), the flow with the built-in decision presentation, in one PR or with a second that switches the flow on: the importer reads the play skin's `loadend` (D14), kept in µs, a skin declaring none getting the floor, and so does a pending play skin until its specialization is committed in the lifetime; the decision screen, built in for 1,500,000 µs from the choice, with its input epoch, which ends at the play's loading state or the practice page; the decision screen kept until a delayed 1× read is ready, Back and the quit key past its time cancelling the read; the loading state's end condition (the minimum from the acknowledged first frame, the load's end, the tuning wait, focus) on the play path and at a practice Start; the window handler routing releases and marking repeats; the opening transition; the input offset permission revoked on leaving the result screen; the practice path through it (P, the decision screen, then the practice page, with no decision screen per Start or round), its 1× bank and loudness reused by a Start at 1× and the bank released by a Start at another speed. Headless tests: the decision screen always followed by the loading state on the play path, also when the load finished during it, and by the practice page on the practice path, a practice Start's loading state honouring the minimum and the tuning wait as the play path's does, a quit there returning to the page, and a round within the loop passing through no loading state; on the practice path the epoch ending at the page, a key held through the decision screen needing a fresh press there and a fresh Start acting, and a tuning key held from the page through a Start needing a fresh press in its loading state; a delayed 1× read keeping the decision screen until the chart is read, Back and the quit key past its time cancelling the read to selection, and a refusal arriving then returning to selection with its reason; a skin declaring 500 ms giving 1 s and one declaring 1,500 ms giving 1.5 s, then the unchanged READY state; a delayed first frame moving the minimum's start, an earlier lifetime's acknowledgement ignored; a held tuning key, one held at its limit, extending past the minimum and the load's end, READY 1,000,000 µs after its release, a tap restarting the wait; window press, release and repeat routing and a removed source (START and SELECT held on a controller come with S10-04b); dwell input without effect, auto-repeat included, and a fresh press needed after it; focus loss keeping the loading state, an emptied held set never opening unfocused, controls rearmed and the wait restarted on its return; the opening gap's length measured and held to a bound; adjustments just after the final check, during the settings-dependent preparation, during a delayed commit and a delayed end-of-loading acknowledgement, and just after recording opens, the record's starting tuning always the settings as recording opened; the failed revalidation never blocking the window thread; result → selection → loading with offset keys changing nothing; loading-time tuning in the starting settings, the record and the settings files; during a minimum-only wait and a tuning wait with the load finished, a quit returning to selection promptly and close or a thread failure ending the run, recording never opening; a refusal or a quit in the gap returning to selection with the attempt's number given back and tuning keys working, and at a practice Start a preparation refusal, a logic refusal and a quit in the gap returning to the page with the reason as its notice and tuning keys working, a commit or end-of-loading error or a thread failure ending the run with that cause; a refused load held until the decision screen's time is up; retry and direct launch unchanged. **9.4b** (after 9.4a and S10-09f; part done: the code is built; remaining, its acceptance checks: the integrated check (a PR of its own) of the full configured set's progression, load, commit, play and the skinned result after it with the decision skin resident, the fade's pixels and the timed refusals' destination screens; the real-app acceptance on the owner's decision skins, side by side with the source, input mashed and held, no skin, a broken copy and labels not ready, and the full resident set with the result skin after a play; and the selection pacing evidence, rapid cursor moves through charts whose labels are cold counted by "Frame Pacing in Refreshes" against the decision skin unconfigured) activates the installed decision skin in that flow (design.md "Play Lifecycle", the decision screen): chosen at entry, the built-in presentation when it is unconfigured or refused or the chosen chart's labels are not ready, and taking over at once when the skin's build is refused mid-scene, its 1,500,000 µs from the original choice; the skin's declared scene time and fade-out, its input time starting timer 1 and changing nothing else, since the whole screen stays inside the input epoch; its resources prepared before entry and never replaced by the play's preparation. Tests, from the choice at 0: a skin declaring `scene` 2,500, `input` 500 and `fadeout` 500 ms, with commands at 100 and 600 ms both discarded, timer 1 off at 500 ms and on 1 µs later, timer 2 at 2,500 ms and the transition at 3,000 ms, the fade following the scene (design.md "Play Lifecycle"); a build refused at 1,000 ms giving the built-in presentation at once and the transition at 1,500 ms, and one refused at 2,000 ms ending the screen at once on its route: the loading state on the play path, and on the practice (P) path the practice page once its chart is read; the chart ready before the scene ends and after it, the loading state following in both; labels not ready at entry giving the built-in presentation and its 1,500,000 µs for that screen; a refused decision skin giving the built-in presentation; fresh presses needed after the epoch; and no clock restarted by a fallback.
   5. **The ledger and the shared sound cache** (`Arc<Sound>`, keyed by file SHA-256 and conversion), with foreground reclamation. Tests: speculation never causing a foreground refusal, and a refused load releasing what it reserved.
   6. **Cursor prefetch**: the parse, the re-resolution at choice, the screen's CPU preparation, the attempt's reusable preparation begun at the choice while the sounds decode, and a speculative bank behind the ledger. Tests: changed bindings (a new file beside a fallback, an appearing sound, swapped targets) refusing reuse, and a file changing during its read.
   7. **Folder prefetch**, in memory only: the compact form (the source bytes for a `#RANDOM` chart), whose codec starts in a permissive L2 crate; the system-friendliness checks in Windows CI.
   8. **Library summaries**: struck; the scan writes them (S10-09c-1, design.md "Loading and Song Selection"), so no summary writer or summary pass remains.
10. **Seeds per attempt** (planned, independent of item 9, can land now; design.md "Seeds per Attempt"): the Arrange state between Loading and Preparing (design.md "State Model"), which retries and rounds enter too; a seed source given to the session in place of the run's clock seed; a new seed for every full play and retry, one per practice page; the transform's stream derived from it; a `#RANDOM` chart's source bytes kept with the loaded chart and its branches resolved again at each retry, the ingested chart kept untransformed and each attempt's transform applied to it; the bank reused when it covers every sound the new draw references with unchanged bindings (assembled by file from shared `Arc<Sound>`s) and built through the loading state when not, returning to Arrange (in Arrange without a presentation, for `tapestry play`); the screen's chart prepared again for each draw; the loudness key naming the chart file (its SHA-256 and the loader version) and the union of its sounds instead of the draw, in a library schema bump, with the loader exposing every `#WAV` declaration regardless of `#IF` branch, resolved to files as the active ones are. Tests: two retries of a `#RANDOM` chart with a fixed seed sequence recording their own seeds and resolved hashes, each verified by `replay::verify`; a chart without `#RANDOM` retried without ingesting again; a file changed on disk between attempts not reaching a retry; two draws with equal sound tables but different referenced sounds not reusing the bank, a covered draw reusing it with no loading state, an uncovered one passing through the loading state and back to Arrange with no second seed taken, the first play's draw made in the loading state with the choice's seed and found by Arrange without resolving again, a practice round never passing through the loading state and a sound file changed on disk mid-loop not reaching the next round, a retry's screen chart matching its new draw, two consecutive transforms each reproduced from the record's seed alone, and the quit key cancelling that load to selection; a retry drawing another branch reusing the stored gain with no measurement; a chart file changed on disk measured again, and so a chart whose replaced sound file only a branch not drawn references; a practice page's Starts and rounds keeping one seed. The lane transforms themselves are not scheduled.
11. **The exact default renderer, the tempo handoff and practice timestamps** (S10-06, after S6c-13; before S10-04b and S10-07), in two PRs:
   1. **The exact default renderer** (S10-06a): `SimpleSkin` owns one `ExactPlayhead` per attempt, prepared in `PlaySkin::prepare` with the attempt's section, dropped by a chart's load and kept with its representatives' buffer by a READY rearm; a `PrepareError` refuses the attempt through `RenderError::Skin` and `Preparation::Refused`, leaving nothing of it drawn. Each frame samples the playhead once at the display time, and `layout::Scale` and `note_y` take that position and the initial rate. The long notes' behaviour, the stored `pos`/`end_pos` distances and the native 16-pixel notes and 8-pixel mines stay. Tests: every fractional-time endpoint's anchor equal to the prepared head (the high-BPM tap at 1 µs centred on y = 880, where the map put it at 839.885); STOP plateaus with and without an endpoint alias; a WARP pixel regression over taps, long-note ends, bar lines, a display offset and a practice section; a refused preparation keeping the screen; rearms keeping the representatives and the attempt memory; and `render --test alloc` over first, later, resized, full and rearmed frames, full and sectioned. The benchmark and the scripted expectations prepare the attempt; the scroll drift guard compares the copy given equal positions and rates.
   2. **The tempo handoff and practice timestamps** (S10-06b): `game::AttemptTempo`, the portable tempo provider over the `ChartTimeline` of the chart as loaded at the attempt's speed, prepared with every attempt (`Loaded::attempt`) and handed over in `Attempt` and the player's `AttemptContext` with its section: `TempoMap` answers the adjustment reducer with the original tempo at a playback-time head, and the START, MIN, MAX and MAIN references are prepared outside play in original BPM, MAIN weighted by endpoint rows over the round's targets, its histogram charged before it is allocated and freed after, and all four unavailable when a tempo has no exact ratio. The frame's playback tempo, the projections and every production readout stay with S10-04b, which activates them. Practice measures are the bar lines' stored times, `ChartTimeline::bar_ids` in order, in place of the reconstruction from positions. Tests: a speed change giving the newly loaded timeline with the speed applied once at preparation and at a later event; MAIN by its rules, by endpoint row rather than µs, and over a practice section; an unavailable ratio; equal-time and post-STOP bar lines keeping their own measures; and `game --test tempo_alloc` (the histogram allocated once and freed, a refusal allocating nothing, the reducer's lookups allocating nothing), also in CI's release allocation step.

The JSON import is complete only after S6c: S6a and S6b alone do not meet it. S6a to S11 and the screen PRs make existing beatoraja skins usable before S12 starts the authored format. The exit (design.md "Roadmap"): real local Lua skins of each supported screen kind are usable through the player, by that row's gate per local skin and screen kind, every check passing: (a) the import passes with each option value and file choice toggled alone from the default, through the conversion report (§3) with one run per toggle; (b) every remaining import note, every runtime note (the report's runtime frame, and a scenario run's: an id the runtime does not answer, a callback's error; each named through S10-32), every id the import answers with a constant (a bridged constant or a constant image index, which gives no note), and every difference seen in (d) is assigned to a planned row, a feature tapestry does not have yet (§1, https://github.com/shuymn/tapestry/issues/476) or a deviation (§3, "Deviations"); (c) a scenario play with the skin reports `[allocations] render_app` 0 (play; decision through S10-30, after S10 item 9.4b draws decision skins); (d) one frame per screen is read from a scenario capture (selection and result by the scenario's capture axes, decision through S10-31) and shows the skin working: no essential part (§3, "Screen profiles"; play's critical roles) missing or wrong, and every difference closed, a deviation or a feature tapestry does not have yet, one waiting on a planned row keeping the check open until that row lands. A refused import, a runtime stopped by its budget, memory cap or a panic, or a screen drawing its built-in presentation in the skin's place fails the row. The gate's per-skin table lives in https://github.com/shuymn/tapestry/issues/4, with the measurement's main commit and date, and skins named only when their authors publish them. Beside it, the play standard skin is playable and fun, every non-play kind's built-in fallback is verified, transitions keep presenting, and the in-play allocation checks pass. Real skin assets stay outside the repository.

## 7. Compromises and decisions

### What stage 3 ships

| Wish | Ideal | Stage 3 | Later |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Other aspects | Reflow per aspect | Anchored regions, background fill, side inset; imports pillarboxed | Per-aspect overrides |
| Player layout | Drag anything | Seven fixed regions, lane width, key-driven adjust mode, numbers in the file; imported skins not adjustable | Mouse drag with `egui`; per-region opacity |
| Judgment-line height | Its own setting | `playfield` y; lift stays a play setting | Revisit if players ask |
| Enlarging | Vector everything | @2x, text at final size | SVG, SDF shapes, MSDF text |
| Shrinking | Perfect | Frame-aware mips, nearest for pixel art | — |
| Resize during play | Re-rasterize | Anchors and fit recomputed; assets refreshed between plays | — |
| Export | Everything | 16:9 1080p `.luaskin`, shared field set, regions as offsets, a report of what was left out | 16:10 and 4:3 bakes, an upstream flag; aspect, mips: never |
| Expressions | General | Typed, range-checked integers, Lua-translatable | Built-ins the conversion report asks for |
| Animation | Curves per key | Four eases per track | Per-key ease and in-out in the authored format, split into keys on export |
| Imports | All existing skins | beatoraja JSON and Lua, under every option value | LR2 (decision 1) |
| Skinned navigation and result | Every screen skinnable | Selection, decision and single-chart result imports, each with its built-in fallback | Other play modes and courses |
| Graphs | Every graph and visualizer | Result gauge history, judgment and early/late timelines and timing distribution; the play score graph, notes and BPM graphs, timing and hit-error visualizers, selection density and BPM previews (S10-10) | Folder distribution graphs |
| Skin configuration | Any screen, chosen anywhere | Four production configuration slots; S11's GUI and skin-select skins | More screen kinds |

### Decisions

1. **LR2 skins.** (a) The LR2 importer right after stage 3. (b) Inside stage 3, also before the authored format. (c) Inside stage 3, after it. **Decision: (a).** beatoraja-family skins are what current players use, and the runtime model is built to grow with its first consumer, so the LR2 importer adds its own semantics (cubic and per-key ease, the LR2 IDs) when it comes.
2. **Where layout adjustments live.** (a) Device scope (follows the monitor and the seat). (b) Player scope. **Decision: (a),** keyed by skin and aspect class; the physical setup is what the adjustments depend on.
3. **Screens narrower than 16:9.** (a) The canvas grows taller. (b) Letterbox. **Decision: (a);** anchors already handle it.
4. **Scratch side.** (a) A player setting that the lanes widget lays out, exported as a one-of option. (b) Not in stage 3. **Decision: (a).**
5. **Name of the format and its file.** "tapestry skin", `skin.toml`.
6. **Fast/slow on the play screen.** (a) Add fast and slow counts to `Tally` in `eval`. (b) Leave them out. **Decision: (a);** it is pure counting in the evaluator.
7. **Screens in stage 3** (owner, 2026-11-03). Stage 3 covers four independently configured skin kinds: 7-key play, song selection, song decision and single-chart result. Non-7-key play and courses come later; skin-select skins are S11's work, not one of these four kinds. Its parts:
   - **Selection** (https://github.com/shuymn/tapestry/issues/179): minimal folders (enter, return) beside the existing all-chart view and search; tables, favourites and advanced search come later.
   - **Other modes** (https://github.com/shuymn/tapestry/issues/177): charts of modes other than 7-key are not shown in selection at all. When other modes come, selection switches between modes and swaps their folders and tables together. The refusal at choice stays.
   - **UI strings** (https://github.com/shuymn/tapestry/issues/176): named string IDs, locale choice and fallback, before new public text bindings and S11.
   - **Practice page** (https://github.com/shuymn/tapestry/issues/223): stays built in.
   - **Graphs** (owner decision): the four result widgets, and the graphs and visualizers skins draw elsewhere: the play score graph with its score figures, the notes and BPM graphs, the timing and hit-error visualizers, selection's density and BPM previews (S10-10). Folder distribution graphs stay out.
   - **Screen timing:** a skin's declarations are respected. The decision screen lasts the skin's declared scene time with its fade-out; its input declaration starts timer 1 (S10 item 9.4b), but the whole screen stays inside the input epoch and no command acts on it; a skip after the input time is planned (https://github.com/shuymn/tapestry/issues/478). The result screen drops commands until the skin's input time has passed, waits for the skin's outro (at most 10 s) before retry, back or the quit key take effect, and leaves on its own after a positive scene time. Built-in presentations keep native timing (the decision screen 1,500,000 µs). The chart still loads during the decision screen, and the play's loading state still follows.
   - **Authored format, export and standard skin** (S12–S14) stay play-only for now.
   - **Ordering:** #83 and S9 (S9-01 to S9-03) land before S10-07.
8. **Where a beatoraja Lua skin runs** (owner, 2026-11-04). (a) Keep lowering callbacks into the expressions and close each blocker on its own. (b) Compile the Lua the skins use into a bounded presentation program for every screen. (c) Run Lua each frame on every screen. (d) Compile for play; run bounded Lua at runtime on the screens outside play. **Decision: (d).** Play needs folded constants, import facts, numbers, text and some float and string work, which a program with Lua's semantics represents without allocating, while no evidence shows a Lua VM can run in play without allocating or collecting; the stateful callbacks of the surveyed skins (853 of 1 381 unique functions, all in two selection and result families) are on screens outside the in-play guarantees, where a bounded VM is allowed. Play stays (b)'s program with no Lua from READY through the audio tail or Closing; selection, decision and result may run Lua in the sandbox, under instruction, memory and time budgets, falling back to typed constants with a note, every VM dropped or quiesced before READY, and use the program where it works. The steps: import facts (S7-04), paths and choices (S7-05), the play program (S7-06), the runtime outside play (S7-07), then the screen adapters. State slots in the program come only if a play skin turns out to need them.
